Seneschal elvira

Political Tenets
Politics needs rules to survive--laws, manners, personal codes. Even the most jaded, unscrupulous politician has some line he tells himself he won't cross: "I don't torture" or "I'll never be impolite." Perhaps he started his career with lines far clearer than the one he swears by now. Tenets (Vampire, p. 172) are the precepts forcing Kindred to measure their actions against their beliefs. Political activity demands painful choices, for Kindred and kine alike. In a political chronicle, the Tenets represent the core moral beliefs the characters must contend with in a backstabbing and partisan world. They're the ethical foundation they work up from, the ideals they betray.
Here you'll find some political Tenets ready to slot into your chronicle or serve as a jumping-off point to inspire your own. Remember, Tenets are never actual limits on what characters can do. They're meant to provide boundaries for characters to test, step over, and fall back on.
Choose Tenets that will be difficult to uphold if you want to bring mechanics like Humanity and Stains into the foreground and play a chronicle focused on moral issues. If you choose Tenets that are easy to keep, Humanity and Stains tend to fade into the background, and moral struggle fades with them.

35

Chapter One: Playing Politics

Courtly Tenets
 Never break your word.  Respect others; demand respect.  Do not cause harm unsanctioned by the Court.
Egalitarian Tenets
 Let no king rule you.  Do not replicate old systems.  Do not cause lasting harm to those weaker than
yourself.

Intelligencer Tenets
 Do not let others discover your secrets.  Never get caught in trouble without a backup
plan.  Do not leave evidence behind of any crime.
Guardian Tenets
 Never leave a job unfinished.  Do not kill an innocent.  The guilty should answer for their crimes.

A RAT IN THE NEST A good betrayal can give any story a marvelous sting. If a player is interested in making their character turn against the rest of the coterie in betrayal, it's best for them and the Storyteller to plan the twist together.
FORESHADOWING The best betrayal should be foreshadowed for the whole troupe, either with hints during play or scenes explicitly showing the traitor's actions. A sudden stab in the back is an unwelcome surprise to most; as the culmination of a story's creeping unease, it's a satisfying conclusion. If players are exclaiming "I knew it!" or "That's what that was?!" rather than "Why? What's happening?" a troupe has adequately laid out the clues to support a double cross within the coterie.
There's a sweet spot in the length of a betrayal plot: long enough to set up tension, but not so long that it adversely affects play. Try using a Pressure tracker (p. 34) set at four or six boxes, set up so both Storyteller and the "Rat" can see its countdown. After a few sessions of foreshadowing, fellow troupe members may have trouble separating character from player, hesitate to include the traitor in plans, or get frustrated out of character when wins are undercut by a traitor. The Storyteller should be sensitive to growing tensions to ensure play remains fun for everyone. One way of doing this is to make sure most of the coterie's wins or losses are independent of the upcoming double cross: personal victories, new allies, successful missions.

36

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

PLACES AND PARTIES

Schemers and lawmakers need places to meet and events to attend. Every city is different, and every domain has its own nooks and crannies. But if you're looking for a few places to wheel and deal, try these.
Elysium
In Camarilla cities, Elysia are where the Court gathers, with and without its ruler--in their absence, a representative will usually be present. Some take place only rarely and move around; others happen once a month at the usual five-star hotel, as doors close "for a private event," and the lights glimmer long past midnight.
For fledglings, Elysium is the best place to find patrons, mentors, and potential allies within their city. For the more established, it's where they confirm their alliances and get a good look at their rivals. Some Elysia are strictly open to the ruling faction only; others welcome even the unbound at the Prince's temporary mercy.
Non-Camarilla domains have similar gatherings and may even refer to them as Elysia, but nothing is as wound in etiquette and unspoken expectations as socializing in the Ivory Tower.

City Hall
A big, square black steel building, City Hall is located fairly close to the city's center, surrounded by office buildings and the downtown courthouse. Closing hours are 7 PM, but there are always lit windows and people working late. It's not impossible to get a meeting with one of them--test Contacts, Influence, or any appropriate Social Skill at Difficulty 3.
Kindred society puts a great deal of effort into ensuring its finances and properties fly under the radar. But walking into the city tax bureau (4th Floor, to the right) and sliding over some fake tax returns is a great way to get a particular corporation or operation audited. Chatting with Planning and Development (7th Floor, just behind the potted fern) can get a street swarming with construction workers and rumbling backhoes. Efforts to manipulate mortal government must always maintain the Masquerade in order to avoid SWAT teams busting down haven doors or murder investigations. But City Hall is a fantastic place to plant seeds that blossom into distracting, delaying annoyances a few weeks later.

37

Chapter One: Playing Politics

The Pit
The skeleton of an abandoned factory surrounds the pit of a foundation dug deep into the earth. Weeds and chain-link fencing sprout around it, and there are signs of daylight visits from mortals looking for a place to graffiti or drink. The Pit is a domain's gathering place to let loose with Disciplines. Enemies challenge each other to duels in it, leaders hold gladiatorial games within its confines and execute traitors, and Blood Sorcerers perform largescale rituals away from prying eyes. The crowd on the Pit's sidelines is ready to let loose, hungry to feel the Blood coursing through them. An insult or an accidental slight here could lead to a full-on brawl. It's a good place to watch your back to avoid getting jumped (Resolve + Leadership).
The Server Room
It's the beating heart of a domain's digital sphere. The rows of blinking servers house financial documents, counterintelligence records, schemes against an agency of mortal hunters, and the backups and originals for dozens of Masks and falsified records. There may be two or three such rooms across a city, each carefully hidden and surrounded by protective measures. The pressure of ever-changing mortal hacking techniques means access is kept extremely exclusive. Even

vampires who are "plugged in" (p. 34­35) rarely get the privilege to use a domain's private servers. Defending this place is vital to a domain's security, and if players' characters have Masks linked to its contents, they have a vested interest in keeping it as safe as possible. Discovering where the servers of rival domains or factions are hidden (Wits + Technology) is the constant quest for spies and informants. They risk their unlives if they succeed in finding one.
The Sanctuary
The faithful in a domain need a place to gather. They choose a tranquil, unassuming space: a repurposed house on a quiet street, an actual old church, a small museum. The central room is furnished sparsely but comfortably, much like any space of worship. A wooden cabinet against one wall houses religious equipment and prayer books. The only truly notable feature is the big square drain in the floor. Books line the walls in the study, and the kitchen has bags of blood in the locked fridge. This is a place of power for whichever faith is dominant in a domain. It could be a literal supernatural nexus or merely a factional stronghold. The faith leader holds enormous sway here. Getting a good word in with them (Charisma + Persuasion) can affect the entire congregation's opinion.
The Hanging Gardens
Night-blooming flowers fill the air with sweet fragrance. A fountain splashes behind low vines. Tall hedges droop over the stepping stones. The gardens grow somewhere spacious and private in the city: in a private courtyard, a fenced-in backyard, or the roof of a reclaimed industrial building. They are a place of carefully cultivated beauty and conspicuous civility. Celebrations and solemn functions are held here: A Monarch's anniversary sees 261 lanterns hung from branches and floated on the water, one for every year since she was Embraced.

38

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

The Assembly holds a gathering here to celebrate their Independence Night. The Collegium honors visiting scholars with a drink beneath the lilacs. The gardens are only accessible to a particular faction, and internal rivalries must be set aside for the night. If a night here goes smoothly, it is a recuperative and centering experience for the faction as a whole. If the night goes awry, it's a very good place to embarrass someone (Manipulation + Etiquette).
The Back Room
It's the basement of a social club, the blocked-off "event space" in the cafe, or the vast cavern of a furniture warehouse. It's not particularly beautiful or storied, and it belongs to no one in power. This is neutral ground--a place for factions to meet without having to set foot in each other's territory. The proprietor isn't allied with any major faction. She rents her space to anyone and holds weekly "speakeasies," where members of any faction can gather for drinks, entertainment, and debate. Here, vampires talk about anything and everything--religion, politics, movies, conspiracy theories--and tempers often flare, but the hostess has a strict two-strike ban rule for troublemakers. The opposing city leaders consider the place their go-to for necessary meetings. They're tense summits, with both sides bristling and ready to attack at the slightest hint of betrayal. If the proprietor is suspected of allegiance toward one side or another, her authority collapses.

speak to lowly fledglings. Most importantly, in several cities, the Solstice is considered a truce night: conflict between Kindred is punished by their faction leaders, even if they'd usually welcome such skirmishes. It's the perfect time for undead from out of town to visit. The general party atmosphere and guarantee of safety make it a night for glad-handing (Charisma + Politics) or perhaps approaching a dangerous rival to broker a peace deal (Composure + Leadership).
Fledgling Ball
In some Camarilla circles, once a fledgling has achieved a certain amount of experience, they're officially brought out into Kindred society with a ball and declared a neonate. Like a cotillion or quinceañera, a Fledgling Ball is an elaborate event. A fledgling's sire traditionally shoulders the cost of hospitality and costume. Guests mingle and marvel at entertainment designed to evoke the sire and fledgling's clan. The ruler of the domain is always the guest of honor at a Fledgling Ball, and he formally welcomes the new neonate into his Court with a small gift or token. Getting an invitation to a Fledgling Ball is quite easy--there's a relatively small number of Kindred in a city, and most sires want to have a big crowd at their event. Getting snubbed is a personal insult and indicates significant spite from either the neonate-to-be or his sire. 

Solstice
The winter solstice is the longest darkness of the year, and it is a night of revelry and diplomacy in Kindred society. Princes and Barons host lavish events, and there's almost always the guarantee of an Elysium. Even the most aloof Kindred elders have been known to unbend for the night and

39

Chapter Two:
THE IVORY TOWER
The Camarilla is a collection of city domains, a sect, and a value system dedicated to ensuring that the Kindred can remain hidden within a protective cocoon of comfort, power, and privilege. Historically, Camarilla domains have provided a politically staid, conservative, and changeless environment where it was difficult for a young vampire to make their mark. Tonight, this has changed in many places. With the triple challenge of the Beckoning, the Second Inquisition, and the Third Anarch Revolt, power vacuums are opening up. Suddenly, even neonates have a chance to become Princes.
Understanding the Camarilla's political landscape is the first crucial step to gaining power and influence in the sect. This chapter takes you inside the Ivory Tower to present how its hierarchies work and what global and local issues it is dealing with now, helping you more effectively plan how to take advantage and begin your political ascent.
41

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

THE POLITICAL ANIMAL

Particularly in the Camarilla, many Kindred see scheming and diplomacy as intrinsic to their being. They're liars and manipulators, they have several identities and Masks, and neither their allies nor their enemies know for certain what they really stand for. For Ventrue, Toreador, and even Tremere in particular, politics is part of their identity, but sometimes even the outcast Gangrel and the sorriest Ravnos make a play of their own.
Chapter One gives a selection of possible motivations, from status to feeding territory, and these remain as true in Camarilla domains as in any others. What's different is the environment in which the characters act, which is what this chapter is about. What kinds of moves can you make specifically in Camarilla circles?
Here are a few to start with. Acquire a Title: You want to be the Prince, one of the Primogen, the Sheriff, or even just a Hound. It's a good, clear goal, and you'll know you've achieved something when you get there. The number of Kindred gunning for the less glamorous titles might be lower than you think, so it can be a good idea to start modestly. Assist with Governance: Surprisingly few Kindred are actually interested in the nightly work of running a domain. Everyone wants to be the Prince for the power it brings, but once you have to hunt for unlicensed childer in the suburbs, there's a distinct dearth of volunteers. This means that the local Prince, Seneschal, and Sheriff are going to look favorably on anyone who wants to help with the boring aspects of their offices.

Be a Gormless Lickspittle: Kindred love flattery the same as anyone else. If you're willing to butter up your betters and don't suffer from a surfeit of dignity, you can get far by talking up those with power.
Betray: It's usually a good idea to keep your betrayals secret, but in a particularly depraved Camarilla domain, a flashy backstabbing might gain you status. It shows you have what it takes to swim with the sharks.
Gossip: Repeat what you heard or make up new stories about your allies and enemies. The Masquerade makes vampire media impossible, so gossip and spying are the main ways to know anything. If other vampires think you're in the know, it's even easier for you to seed stories that are to your advantage.
Grant Favors: Make yourself useful to other Kindred. You can acquire boons, but even less formalized reciprocity may also work to your favor. In addition to having others owe you, you also learn a lot about their goals and operations while you help them. On occasion, it even pays to ask for nothing in return.
Gain and Hold onto Territory: Meaningful territory will almost always be claimed already, but older vampires often act as patrons, liege lords, or landlords in return for favors. This means anyone can gain territory if they bargain well enough. The real challenge is holding onto it, policing its borders, and making sure others respect your claim. A vampire with a properly held territory has a lot more weight in the internal politics in and between Camarilla domains.

42

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Join a Political Block: Maybe the local Nosferatu have formed a political opposition block, working against the inner Court because of constant discrimination in Elysium. Convince them that their cause is yours, and you can get them to do things for you.
Leak Information: You can send a local Second Inquisition agency the location of your enemy's haven. There's no way this can lead to blowback, right?
Splurge on Hospitality: It's one of the Traditions for a reason. The more extravagantly helpful you are when hosting other Kindred, the stronger your territory seems and the more powerful you look. In the world of the dead, things are what they look like. If you seem important, you are.

For culture to endure, empowered order must raise walls to protect it and keep them strong over time. Libraries, for knowledge to be committed to parchment and ideas exchanged. Gardens, for the preservation of herbs and the study of medicine. Cities, where a society can develop traditions and sacred rites. The Camarilla is one such protective power.
 Tegyrius, Haqimite representative in the Camarilla

CAMARILLA TIMELINE For a broader timeline, see In Memoriam, pp. 25­30; Chapter Three "The Rod of Lachesis: Kindred History" which goes further into depth with Kindred history.
Late 1300s: English peasant revolts fan flames of revolution growing among younger licks. 1435: Hardestadt the Elder calls a convocation of elders to deal with the problem of rebellious childer. 1493 (The Convention of Thorns): The leaders of the childer rebellion meet with titled elders to negotiate. The Camarilla is established and the Traditions formally codified. 1990s: The blood god Ur-Shulgi awakens and starts casting out Banu Haqim who maintain mortal faith, causing a schism in the clan and turmoil within the Ashirra. 1999: Xaviar withdraws from the Camarilla, attempting to bring the Gangrel with him. 2006­2013 (The Fall of London): Mortal hunters systematically target and destroy Kindred havens, driving the survivors out of the city. 2008 (The Fall of Vienna): The Tremere Prime Chantry is destroyed by mortal hunters, and the Pyramid topples. 2012 (The Conclave of Prague): Theo Bell takes out Hardestadt the Younger, igniting the Anarch Revolt to fire and blood again. Clan Brujah is formally kicked out of the Camarilla. Clan Gangrel follows. 2017 (The Vermilion Wedding): Tegyrius Blood Bonds himself to Victoria Ash, symbolically joining the Banu Haqim to the Camarilla. 2019 (The Conclave of Chicago): Lasombra diplomats make a deal with Camarilla dignitaries to allow individual Lasombra membership, though the price a domain leverages for the privilege is often high.

43

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

THE KINDRED CLANS

The Tower was founded by elders from seven clans: Brujah, Gangrel, Malkavian, Nosferatu, Toreador, Tremere, and Ventrue. These were the pillar clans, until the Brujah and the Gangrel left, and a sizable number of Banu Haqim joined in their stead. Individual Camarilla Courts can include members of all vampire clans, but these hold the most overall influence.

same), Kindred who blame them for their loss of territory, gossipmongers, and the extraordinarily conservative. They cannot know, when they meet another Kindred for the first time, whether they will be perceived as a rising star within the sect or an unwelcome infiltrator. It's no easy position, but it can offer opportunities as well, especially for recent settlers who have yet to choose their closest allies.

The Pillars
of the Tower
These are nights of political upheaval, and even the official Camarilla clans have challenges to contend with, as well as advantages, on both sect-wide and local levels.
Banu Haqim
There were always some Banu Haqim in Camarilla cities, and following Ur-Shulgi's dominion over many of the clan in the late '90s, this number grew. Since Tegyrius and Victoria Ash were Blood Bound to each other in the Vermilion Wedding, the clan enjoys official membership. But this gift horse has a mouth full of rotten teeth and has caused tension in many city domains. A Camarilla Child of Haqim often has to handle suspicion and jealousy from Gangrel and Brujah (who are upset that they are no longer treated the

 The Fifth-Generation methuselah Tegyrius is the current Banu Haqim Justicar, despite not having claimed the title.
 The Banu Haqim have old enemies within many Camarilla cities. The only reason they joined the sect was to get to them. And now they're coming.
Malkavian
Prophets of any kind often receive a mixed welcome.
It isn't uncommon for other Kindred to look to the Clan of the Moon for insight in times of turmoil. Even individuals who are normally treated as outsiders can gain a glimmer of importance when they might be holding answers others want. Many a Malkavian has discovered that premonitions and knowledge granted through the so-called Tapestry, their rumored clan-wide psychic link, can help them grasp for power. Not every Malkavian is genuinely psychic, of course. But often, simply letting others think you have all the answers can get you where you need to go. Others

are tirelessly bombarded by premonitions of crises to come, but they either do not believe or are punished for spreading information they weren't meant to access.
It can be a tricky business, being a Visionary. And that's without including all the trouble for those whose symptoms manifest in ways that would be so unacceptable to the Tower they have to hide them and make up different ones. OCD seems to be in vogue at the moment. A very real affliction, it has garnered a reputation for predictability, which appears much more palatable to most Princes than, say, hearing voices.

 Ivo Reo, the Gaelic Seventh-Generation Oracle who is said to have foretold many important changes to the sect, works closely with the Inner Circle.
 Because of their mysterious psychic connection, Camarilla members of Clan Malkavian still work closely together with their Anarch cousins. Whenever the Tower attacks an Anarch city, the Children of the Moon get their own out first.
Darling, the Courts of the Tower are simply a continuation of the monarchies of our old world. They're as complicated and dangerous to navigate, and just as fun. Learn to play by the rules, and when to break them, and you'll rise quickly in their ranks.
 Victoria Ash

Nosferatu
Many Princes have severely limited the use of digital networks within their cities following the widespread resurrection of the Inquisition, but among the Nosferatu, there are still those who find ways of staying well-connected, having practiced untraceably communicating over secret servers since the conception of the internet. Chief among the political tools of a young Nosferatu is their perceived, and often real, ability to access information others can't. It's a bargaining chip that is only increasing in value, now only surpassed by their rumored knack for cheating mortal surveillance systems. Even a fledgling Nosferatu can shake up a Camarilla Court by trading in security, information, Masquerade-safe tech, or even just by taking blackmail money to shut up about the supposed secrets they've uncovered. As a downside, many Kindred view Nosferatu with suspicion, worrying how much they know about them and what it'd take for them to share it with the wrong people.
Rumors
 Certain members of Clan Toreador carry a deep grudge against the Camarilla's security expert Ambrus Maropis and won't reveal why. It's because he mercilessly catfishes them whenever he discovers them online.
 Before her term was up, elder Molly MacDonald, the former Nosferatu Justicar, was supposedly destroyed in Paris during a secret meeting with members of the Ministry. Depending on your source, it was a Second Inquisition attack or was engineered by the Ministers. Still, every so often, somewhere around Europe, there's a Kindred who claims to have seen a redhead fitting her description: stunning when seen from the right angle but with a face half-eaten by decay.

45

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Toreador
Prominent members of the Clan of the Rose often guide the sect's culture and handle prestation, which means that in the race for control of a city, they tend to start ahead. A Toreador Keeper of Elysium makes sure to note every Kindred's habits when they visit and will remember when they need a favor, for instance. A younger Toreador can also easily come off as frivolously eccentric, making other Kindred see them as someone easily manipulated or unimportant. This can become a weapon when they need an element of surprise--or backlash, when the trouble that comes to find them is more than they can handle.
Rumors
 The Toreador Justicar appointed in 2011, Diana Iadanza, has been hunting down members of Clan Gangrel since their departure from the Camarilla. Stunning but ruthless, she prefers to keep this business private, striking suddenly and without mercy.
 The Fifth-Generation Prince of Paris, François Villon, has left his seat for the Beckoning, and Paris is in the hands of an impostor.
 Victoria Ash and her Banu Haqim husband never quarrel. When he is upset, he withdraws to work in a soundproof chamber designed to keep out even an army of Victorias. It drives her mad like nothing else.

Tremere
With the fracturing of the Pyramid, thaumaturges have been forced to renegotiate their positions within the Tower. Some find that their Blood Bonds to their masters being broken makes it easier to forge new alliances, while others struggle to influence Courts the way they used to. Tremere who once implicitly trusted one another no longer have a reason to. A clandestine arms race for esoteric materials has begun--in the scramble to control clan libraries and the secret Rituals within them, it's every Tremere for themselves. A clever sorcerer might make much of their newfound freedom, trading redwork for political influence or even offering hope to other Kindred longing to be freed from Blood Bonds. Blood Sorcery is as mysterious to many others as are the secrets of the universe. An up-and-coming Tremere can go far if they can master the art of appearing always to know more than they actually do.
The Tremere Pyramid supposedly looked like this: led by a council of seven, each councilor had dominance of seven pontifices, each pontifex had seven lords, each lord had seven regents under them, and each regent, naturally, controlled a chantry of their own. I'm not a numbers guy, but to me that doesn't sound right--do the math, and you'll see that we'd all be ruled by Tremere. Shit, we'd all be Tremere. That Pyramid of theirs was about to consume us all.
 Anonymous Brujah
My clan used to bring me such clarity. I knew what to do and whom to turn to when there was any kind of complication. I didn't have to worry about anything but keeping my head down and perfecting my craft. I miss it. Even the verbal beatings. After 200 years of being ordered around, it starts to feel like that's how it's supposed to be.
 The Tremere Yordan

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Rumors
 The Pyramid didn't really fall. It was performatively pulled down from within to hide it from the wider sect.
 Karl Schrekt, former High Pontifex and the head of the traditionalist House Tremere, is known to be committed to rebuilding the Pyramid. But he won't stop there. No longer trusting large parts of his clan, he intends to purge the disloyal bloodlines. What he and his house are currently doing is determining who that is.
Ventrue
As figures of authority, members of the Clan of Kings have, in many cities, been the primary targets of enemies of the Tower. As a result, the clan has suffered significant losses. But the Patricians know to grip the throat of opportunity firmly when it's bared. Where one falls, another rises, and younger Ventrue stand ready to declare themselves for the freed-up thrones of their former kin. Among the Kindred, members of Clan Ventrue are among the most fervent Camarilla traditionalists. They keep the Tower strong through strict belief in--and adherence to--its hierarchies. A Ventrue conspirator may scheme to topple his Prince but never think to do away with Princes in general. Elder Ventrue sometimes refer to the clan's Directorate--a society of the elite for the elite, wherein decisions concerning the whole clan are made and obscured. In some cities, neonate Ventrue have started whispering that they should establish their own version. Younger Ventrue often draw on their connections, or claimed connections, as a favored political weapon. They know half the battle is won in advance if you can make your opponent fear the consequences of defeating you.

Rumors
 Anne Bowesley, the former Prince of London, barely got out of the city when it fell to mortal hunters and is licking her wounds somewhere, planning her return. A known diablerist, some wonder who she will consume to get there.
 Claiming to have been named Justicar for Unlife, the elder Lucinde is dedicated to eradicating the Camarilla's enemies by all means necessary. Specializing in covert operations, she is supposedly at the head of an intelligence network that regularly weeds out weak links from within the sect.
THE USES OF FAMOUS KINDRED Storytellers aren't the only ones using the names of recognizable elders to create a little extra excitement. Your average Camarilla Court may never see the shadow of characters like Tegyrius and Theo Bell, but names have power.  A Prince whips his courtiers into submission,
threatening them that a former despot will return if given half a chance.  A sire entertains their childer with stories about the great deeds of their sire's sire.  A Camarilla city becomes divided as a rumor flourishes that a famous Anarch is approaching to collect members for their revolution.  A Toreador taste maker decorates themselves with silk kisses they insist have been pressed by Victoria Ash or Helena herself.  A pretender claims to be someone feared and admired, taking the chance that no one in their city has the experience to tell the difference.  A new Court law is presented as coming directly
from a known elder.
· An unpopular Blood Hunt is defended as being sanctioned by a well-known Justicar.

Other Clans of
Influence
According to traditionalists with intel from the European epicenters of the sect, these clans are either no longer part of the Camarilla or, as is the case for the Lasombra, not officially included. This means they may be barred from Primogen Councils and certainly shouldn't have a Justicar representing them. Still, they have enough members that in some domains, when the Kindred speak of the sect, they think of these clans as well.

Brujah
One Brujah is working for a cause that depends on them acting from within the sect. Another would like to rebel but has no one to turn to. A third has no idea that Bell defected and thinks they're still doing what their elders want them to. In cities that have little contact with the outside world, the Kindred population may not even be aware that the Second Inquisition has begun.

The trouble is that there are plenty of Kindred who do know and blame the actions of Bell on all his Blood. To be a Camarilla Brujah tonight is therefore to be the first suspect when the leaders smell something rotting in their city.
Often they are seen by other members of the Tower as one of two things: traitors or traitors-to-be. The best tools Camarilla Prometheans can reach for are planning ahead and moving carefully.
Rumors
 Briefly the Justicar for Clan Brujah from 2011­2012, Manuela Cardoso Pinto has remained loyal to the Tower. A faithful Catholic, she doesn't shy away from extreme methods to get back at her enemies, but she always makes sure to seek absolution afterwards.
 The once-loyal Camarilla soldier Theo Bell defected to the Anarch Movement after shooting the head off the elder Hardestadt at the Convention of Prague in 2012. Since then, Bell has started a collection. The heads of elders don't last long, which is why he has them cast in plaster before they disintegrate.
Gangrel
From the perspective of traditionalists, Clan Gangrel was awfully quick to leave the Camarilla once the Brujah did. Some claim that's because a large majority had already left and joined up with the Anarch rebellion in the late 1990s; others say they were never truly faithful to the Tower to begin with. The smeared reputation means that Camarilla Gangrel will often have to prove themselves repeatedly. It also means that the ones who do are sometimes asked to act as double agents or negotiators in dealings with Anarchs. Most Courts also agree that throwing good soldiers out of a city when war is at its doorstep isn't the best idea. A loyal Gangrel can be a real asset in a fight.

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Rumors
 The last Gangrel Justicar, Geoffrey Leigh, disappeared following the events of 2012. Most assume he joined Gehenna, but the truth is he joined the Anarch Movement as a spy for the Tower.
 The former Justicar Xaviar encountered an Antediluvian before he announced his clan's departure in 1999. He was destroyed soon after by Lucinde, who personally drained him. As their souls did battle, she inherited his memories of the meeting in which the ancient in the ground devoured his entire coterie. It marks her now with a fear of being alone in isolated landscapes.
Lasombra
There have always been Lasombra Kindred in Camarilla cities. Tonight, there are more than ever before as the Sabbat has headed into the heart of the Gehenna War. Lasombra, who had been able to fly under the radar in Sabbat territories before, were suddenly conscripted and realized that they would be much more comfortable as members of the Tower. In domains with an existing Lasombra presence, new arrivals might be received graciously, depending on their Camarilla kin. Other places, it's not unusual for Courts to demand significant proof of loyalty before they will consider allowing Lasombra to settle.

Clan Lasombra's tendency to ruthlessness means others think twice before challenging them, and sometimes a moment's hesitation is all you need to get ahead. Their reputation as skilled schemers also equates competence in the minds of many, meaning that even a young Lasombra has a good chance at being taken seriously when they interfere with domain politics. No one fully trusts a Lasombra, but it's commonly agreed upon that having one on your side is a major asset.
Rumors
 The elder Montano, who has been a part of the Camarilla since its inception and was branded a traitor by his mostly Sabbat clan, is a prominent member now. However, the former political firebrand now feels the pull of the Beckoning more than most and spends the majority of his time in deep trance, trying to find a path through the Abyss to suppress it.
 One of the first Lasombra to join the Sabbat, the killer Talley has been working to facilitate the Lasombra's entry into the Camarilla in Chicago by taking out high-ranking members who oppose it.
The Tower is a web of intrigue and old grudges, precariously held together by spite, debt, and mutually ensured destruction. I have no idea how it stays as strong as it does. I suppose that's why I'm not in charge.
 Yordan, Tremere survivor of the fall of Vienna

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

FACES OF THE TOWER

As a system of power and authority, Camarilla domains are better at distributing information at the higher levels of power than their rivals among the Anarchs. Older vampires simply know more and have their own longestablished methods of communicating with each other in ways that are inaccessible to the Second Inquisition, whether by messenger birds or redwork.
Because of the persistence of Camarilla power structures and the way ancillae and elders

communicate (or gossip) across domains, many famous Kindred are known for their influence. For a neonate learning about them for the first time, the only problem is that often the rumors are decades, even centuries, out of date. You may know what Rafael de Corazon did at the founding of the Camarilla, but does he even exist anymore? And if you meet someone claiming to be him, how do you know if they're lying?

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

The Camarilla isn't a Cult
Cults are characterized by:
1. Isolating members from the rest of society; replacing the family unit with new social units.
2. Absolute authoritarianism without accountability.
3. No tolerance for critical inquiry.
4. No meaningful financial disclosure regarding where funds come from and what they go to.
5. Unreasonable fear of the outside world, such as impending catastrophe, conspiracies, and persecutions.
6. There is no legitimate reason to leave. Former followers are enemies.
7. Followers are punished when they transgress.
8. Followers are pushed to constantly better themselves.
9. The group/leader is always right.
10. The group/leader is the exclusive holder of the truth.
The Camarilla is nothing like that.

Friday 23:15
X told me I have this night to pack my bags and head out of town. I don't know where to go.

Can we meet?

Em?

Saturday 00:05

Saturday 01:01
I didn't mean to break any rules. But it's my mom. She's not got long anyway, the doctors say... who would she tell?

Did they get to you?

Saturday 02:20
Pls stop just do what they want.

I'll try to keep an eye on ur mom k?

Saturday 02:25 Kk

!

Love you ))

Not Delivered

Note: While the Camarilla isn't a cult in the traditional sense, the way the Bahari or the Church of Set are, it may still be useful to consider how certain cult-like tactics sometimes find a use where they are not expected.

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Victoria Ash
One of the most famous Toreador in existence, Victoria Ash may be found both at the top of Camarilla society and in the gutters, in the Mask of a young ingenue accompanied by her latest mortal lover. Unlike many elders who eventually succumb to ennui and lose touch with the mortal world, she has somehow managed to stay interested in the ever-changing world around her.
52

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

An entirely self-created member of the vampire elite, in life, Victoria Ash was born out of wedlock to a poor French alewife. Hungry and lost, she became a military camp follower, exchanging sex for sustenance with soldiers far from home. Despite her dire origins, her remarkable beauty and ability to mirror whatever the people around her wanted to see brought her increasing success. The stature of her lovers increased as she remade herself into an increasingly sophisticated courtesan.
Embraced by an undead pimp, Victoria's entrance into the world of the undead was not the most dignified, but her chameleon-like ability to insinuate herself into any social scene soon saw her in the company of the most refined Toreador and Ventrue of the Camarilla. Her social influence is tremendous, culminating in the Vermilion Wedding with the Banu Haqim Tegyrius designed to symbolically bring the Camarilla and the Ashirra closer together. In a Camarilla Elysium, she's almost always the highest-status vampire present.
Yet Victoria spends only a small amount of her time in vampire politics or hosting glamorous galas. Usually, she's pretending to be a mortal or a neonate who doesn't know anything but is excited about everything around her. These personas rarely last longer than a few months, but a memorable one catapulted her to brief success as a pop star before she gave it up. This is at the core of her mystery to her fellow elders: how is it possible that she gets so much done, when they themselves feel the sands of time slipping through their fingers in an increasingly incomprehensible world?

Clan: Toreador Generation: 8th Embrace: 1650 Blood Potency: 5 Humanity: 7 General Difficulty: 6/5 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 9, Social 10, Mental 10, Disciplines 9 Secondary Attributes: Health 9, Willpower 10 Exceptional Dice Pools: Etiquette 12, Persuasion 12, Subterfuge 12 Disciplines: Auspex 4 (Heightened Senses, Premonition, Scry the Soul, Spirit's Touch), Celerity 4 (Cat's Grace, Fleetness, Blink, Unerring Aim), Dominate 3 (Cloud Memory, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 1 (Resilience), Presence 5 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Entrancement, Irresistible Voice, Majesty) In Stories: Victoria Ash can be a powerful political patron. She's interested in the new and often supports neonates and fledglings due to their novelty value. She takes interesting people as lovers, but her favor tends to be fleeting, and she leaves a wake of discarded people behind her. As a social force, Victoria can be extremely destructive. Toreador socialites compete for her favor, performing deranged acts of fealty just to be noticed. Anarch neonates realize the girl they were dating turned out to be a Camarilla elder out on a lark, and their trust in their ability to read people around them is permanently damaged.

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Ambrus Maropis
There are many ways to have political influence inside the Camarilla, and sometimes power is in the hands of Kindred who don't see themselves as political actors at all. Such is the case of the Nosferatu Ambrus Maropis, running one of the best Kindred information networks from an ostentatious apartment in Cyprus.
54

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

In terms of style and habits, he's nothing like the elders and ancillae who define power in the Elysia of the Camarilla, yet on a nightly level, his influence transcends the limitations of domain. In a world where the Second Inquisition forces vampires into an analog existence on a fragmented map of disparate domains, he has managed to retain a global perspective.
Embraced in his late teens, most of Maropis' existence, both while he was alive and now that he's dead, has been online. He's deeply connected to mortal culture, whether through anime fandom, multiplayer games, or online roleplaying. He has a rich variety of online personas, identities, and Masks he switches between depending on the needs of the situation and a wide variety of contacts who don't know who they're really dealing with.
Something in Maropis' style and ability to keep ahead of the Second Inquisition online has caught on among many younger Camarilla Kindred, turning him into an icon of Kindred culture. He's a legend, a ghost in the network rumored to help those who follow him. He has many disciples, though it's unclear how many of them actually communicate with him directly.
Although Maropis is envied by many Kindred much older than he is, his commitment to upholding the Masquerade and demonstrable effectiveness have made him untouchable. He sees himself as an iconoclast using mortal power structures and systems to play the role of an information broker under the noses of vampires far more powerful than he.
Yet in truth, Maropis is one of the more reputable members of the Camarilla. He plays the game of politics in his own way, leaking information, performing services, humiliating his Toreador enemies, and demonstrating his power as necessary.

Clan: Nosferatu Generation: 11th Embrace: 2008 Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 7 General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 7, Mental 9, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Persuasion 8, Streetwise 9, Technology 10 Disciplines: Animalism 3 (Sense the Beast, Feral Whispers, Animal Succulence), Auspex 1 (Heightened Senses), Dominate 1 (Cloud Memory), Obfuscate 4 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage, Ghost in the Machine, Vanish), Potence 3 (Soaring Leap, Prowess, Uncanny Grip) In Stories: Maropis shows what a young, dynamic Camarilla might look like now that the staid rule of the elders has weakened. Although he spends his nights in physical isolation, he doesn't believe in sequestering himself when it comes to information, dealing happily with the young and the ambitious before anyone else in the sect has noticed them. He prides himself on always being the first to notice when a new player has entered the game. As a character in your game, Maropis is a tool you can use to connect the players' characters to much bigger schemes. They may not understand the game the Nosferatu is playing at first, but in time, they'll come to realize that their small corner of Camarilla politics is just a piece of a bigger puzzle.

55

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Fiorenza Savona
What's the difference between the power of the Kindred and the mortal superrich? Money and political influence can take you much further than the powers of the Blood. At least, that's what the rising Ventrue star Fiorenza Savona believes.
Embraced late in life in a deal she herself initiated, Savona had already walked the halls of power for decades as a mortal. Born to wealth in an Italian family residing in Mexico, she chose a career in international politics, moving between NGOs and the UN. Both her parents had been career diplomats whose lives were eased by old money, and she made the most of what she inherited in contacts and opportunities.
Savona believed that wealth follows power and influence, always seeking to use what she had to change the world to her preference. In terms of values, she was a chameleon, always more interested in systems of power and how to use them rather than adhering to principles. She did have one rule, though: if you stuck with her, you would be rewarded.
56

She has held onto this idea in unlife, building a faction of young, wealthy, and ambitious Kindred inside both the Ventrue and the Camarilla that spreads well beyond her home base of Mexico City. They believe that the Beckoning presents a chance for a new generation of the dead to inherit the power of their sires, assuming control over both the worlds of the living and the undead.
As a person, Savona enjoys her luxuries and presents a façade of unshakable confidence, but many are surprised at how reasonable she seems. If you give her everything she wants, there must be something you desire that she can help you with. Her ability to influence mortal business and politics with such nuance is unsettling to many older Ventrue who have lost their touch with the world of the living.
Clan: Ventrue Generation: 8th Embrace: 2008 Blood Potency: 4 Humanity: 6 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 8, Mental 9, Disciplines 7

Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 9 Exceptional Dice Pools: Persuasion 9, Finance 10, Politics 10 Disciplines: Animalism 2 (Sense the Beast, Feral Whispers), Dominate 5 (Compel, Mesmerize, Submerged Directive, Rationalize, Terminal Decree), Fortitude 2 (Unswayable Mind, Toughness), Presence 4 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Entrancement, Irresistible Voice) Clan Bane: Fiorenza keeps her preferences a closely guarded secret. In Stories: Savona's purpose is to demonstrate that in the modern nights, a neonate can become a Camarilla power player by leveraging their unique abilities. In her case, it's an understanding of mortal power and politics, but a player's character can leverage anything they have, as long as it's difficult for older Camarilla power players to access. In this sense, Savona is a revolutionary figure in the conservative Camarilla establishment. Instead of being content to wait for the inheritance of power to pass on to her in the fullness of time, she shows that you can grab hold of it right now.

57

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Aisling Sturbridge
One of the best known and dynamic Regents in the Tremere traditionalist faction, Aisling Sturbridge differs from many of her clanmates in that she's building for the future, not just trying to survive in a collapsing hierarchy. Her leadership of the Chantry of the Five Boroughs in New York has brought enough notice to make her elders worry about her, but the Tremere are currently in such disarray that they can't do much to stop her ascension.
Born to a family in banking in upstate New York in 1890, Sturbridge's path to occultism was through the Catholic faith of her parents. She became interested in the mysteries of the faith and gradually moved to more esoteric pursuits. She had a knack for languages,
58

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

teaching herself Latin and Ancient Greek so she could read original sources.
Escaping family commitments, she arrived in London as a young woman seeking to learn more from the local occult scene. This also caught the attention of her future sire and his clan, the Tremere. She was Embraced in Vienna in a ceremony that later fell into disuse because of the vagaries of the 20th century.
Sturbridge sees herself as the inheritor of a grand occult tradition that she must protect, develop, and elevate. Although she has been initiated into most of the inner mysteries of her clan, she remains a seeker. She knows that this won't be a solitary pursuit but something that requires her entire clan... and the help of others as well.
Sturbridge's success has been due to two important factors: occult ability and the willingness to do the work when it comes to the politics of her local domain. A Tremere Regent can't just sequester herself in the chantry. She has to associate with the high and the low, conducting her business in cocktail parties and dark alleys alike. Sturbridge's habit of forming questionable alliances with outsiders when it suits her purpose is the biggest cause of controversy inside the clan.
Clan: Tremere Generation: 10th Embrace: 1911 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 7 General Difficulty: 5/4

Standard Dice Pools: Physical 8, Social 7, Mental 9, Disciplines 9
Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 9 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 10, Investigation 10, Occult 10 Disciplines: Auspex 5 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition, Scry the Soul, Spirit's Touch, Clairvoyance), Celerity 2 (Cat's Grace, Fleetness), Dominate 3 (Compel, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 2 (Unswayable Mind, Toughness), Obfuscate 2 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage), Blood Sorcery 5 (A Taste for Blood, Extinguish Vitae, Blood of Potency, Theft of Vitae, Cauldron of Blood; Rituals: Blood Walk, Craft Bloodstone, Communicate with Kindred Sire, Ward Against Spirits, Deflection of Wooden Doom, Ward Against Lupines, Defense of the Sacred Haven, Ward Against Cainites, Escape to True Sanctuary) In Stories: Aisling Sturbridge is a dynamic young ancilla, one of the cohort of Kindred who have risen to prominence in the Camarilla in recent years. She has been able to operate freely as a Regent because the elders who might otherwise have checked her ambition have vanished. Sturbridge's role in political stories is either as a dealmaker or an opponent. Despite her curt manner, she's good at finding arrangements that benefit both sides--or at least seem to do so. As an opponent, she's dangerous because of her ability to operate from behind façades. You won't know she's gunning for you because the attacks come from seemingly unconnected sources.

59

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Tegyrius
What's the difference between the Camarilla and the Ashirra? According to the members of both sects, they're separate in terms of traditions, power structures, philosophy, history, and geography. Yet in the broadest terms, both are conservative alliances of traditionalist domains seeking to preserve the Masquerade and the hierarchy of vampiric power. No wonder they get along.
The Banu Haqim Tegyrius is one of the architects of the current rapprochement between the Camarilla and the Ashirra. A Banu Haqim who had walked the halls of power among the Ashirra for centuries, he was prompted to seek closer relations because of the start of the Gehenna War and the awakening of the blood god Ur-Shulgi.
60

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

An ancient in his own right, Tegyrius served in the armies of Alexander the Great while still a mortal. As a vampire, he's been a staunch believer in the power of law, hierarchy, and justice as a method for keeping the Beast in check, both personally and across Kindred society.
From his perspective, the Camarilla is a young sect still in the process of establishing itself. He hopes that the current cooperation between the Ashirra and the Camarilla in fighting against the Sabbat lasts. To this end, he agreed to the Vermilion Wedding, where he symbolically entered into a Blood Marriage with the Toreador Victoria Ash.
In the modern nights, Tegyrius is a traditionalist of such pedigree that he stands out even among the elders of the Camarilla. Confused by his comparatively young wife, he has come to realize that the Camarilla needs him and his experience if it's going to last. As such, he has felt duty bound to seek ever greater power inside the sect he has joined by alliance.
Inside his own clan, Tegyrius stands against the power of Ur-Shulgi. Where the blood god seeks to return the clan to a pre-Islamic tradition under his own rule, Tegyrius aims to preserve the culture and tradition developed among the Banu Haqim and in the Ashirra. An erudite man, he sees Ur-Shulgi as a terrifying monster risen out of the bloody prehistory of all Kindred.
Clan: Banu Haqim Generation: 5th Embrace: 330 BCE Blood Potency: 8 Humanity: 4 General Difficulty: 7/5 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 9, Social 10, Mental 10, Disciplines 10 Secondary Attributes: Health 12, Willpower 10 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 12 (History), Etiquette 12, Melee 12, Leadership 12

Disciplines: Auspex 5 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition, Scry the Soul, Spirit's Touch, Possession), Celerity 5 (Cat's Grace, Fleetness, Blink, Unerring Aim, Lightning Strike), Dominate 3 (Compel, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 5 (Resilience, Toughness, Fortify the Inner Façade, Draught of Endurance, Flesh of Marble), Obfuscate 2 (Silence of Death, Unseen Passage), Potence 4 (Soaring Leap, Prowess, Spark of Rage, Draught of Might), Presence 5 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Dread Gaze, Summon, Majesty), Blood Sorcery 5 (Corrosive Vitae, Extinguish Vitae, Scorpion's Touch, Theft of Vitae, Baal's Caress; Rituals: Wake with Evening's Freshness, Ward Against Ghouls, Eyes of Babel, Illuminate the Trail of Prey, Truth of Blood, Dagon's Call, Deflection of Wooden Doom, Ward Against Lupines, Incorporeal Passage, Ward Against Cainites, Shaft of Belated Dissolution, Warding Circle Against Cainites)
In Stories: Tegyrius represents a tradition of power spanning centuries. He seeks to preserve the structures with which he's comfortable, but in doing so, he has to associate with the young and the new. The only way for tradition to persist is through change, revealing the enduring paradox at the heart of his character.
For even established players' characters, Tegyrius represents ancient power out of place in the modern nights. Yet despite his alienation, he's still active in the affairs of the great sects, able to lay down the law when he finds it necessary. He's not an ally or an enemy but a force of nature, enforcing ancient customs and forgotten laws before moving on.

61

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
Viraj Dey
Dey used to be a British civil servant working in Brussels before Brexit. He flew to see his family in India once a year, saved money for vacations with his wife and children in places like the Maldives and Cape Verde, and looked forward to retirement when he could devote himself fully to his passion for travel.
In his 40s when he was Embraced, Dey had been an ordinary man working in politics. Unfortunately, a Camarilla Malkavian ancilla needed somebody who understood how the European Union worked and chose to Embrace Dey precisely because of his simple competence. He looked like he'd make a loyal minion.
62

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

That didn't happen. Perhaps it was the trauma of the Embrace or the effects of Malkav's curse, but once undead, Dey suddenly had a grand vision of politics and power and how he could change the world. He believes in something akin to the Anarch dream of Carthage, but with a Camarilla twist: The time will come when the Masquerade falls, and a thousand years of enlightened vampiric rule begin. Sometimes Dey's vision becomes downright rhapsodic, as he sees Kindred Blood used to make mortals deathless and a mass spiritual awakening when the need for lies falls away and Kindred finally reconcile themselves with their Beasts.
For the time being, the world is not ready. Dey believes that the Gehenna War and its risen blood gods present a final test for Kindred and kine alike, a historical evil they must vanquish before they can emerge into a perfect world.
Dey's visionary approach to politics has made more than one Prince and Justicar decide that he's a threat to the Masquerade, but they haven't moved against him because he has proven to be supremely talented in manipulating mortal politics. Whatever the end goal, currently, what he does aligns perfectly with the needs of the Camarilla, so they have chosen to ignore his prophetic side. He has the ability to talk about defeating the Sabbat and the blood gods in a way that makes younger Kindred listen.
Clan: Malkavian Generation: 10th Embrace: 2010 Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 9

General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 4, Social 6, Mental 8, Disciplines 8 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Persuasion 8, Politics 10, Finance 9 Disciplines: Auspex 4 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition, Share the Senses, Spirit's Touch), Dominate 3 (Cloud Memory, Dementation, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 1 (Unswayable Mind), Obfuscate 4 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage, Mask of a Thousand Faces, Conceal), Presence 1 (Awe) Clan Bane: Delusions of grandeur (Social); Dey believes he can sometimes see the quality of a mortal's soul by looking into their eyes. In Stories: Dey is a conservative revolutionary, someone who wants to vanquish the threats to the Camarilla so that it can evolve into a system of perfect, open global rule over the living. His arrival in any Camarilla domain is a crisis for the local Court because his apocalyptic visions may inspire the neonates and fledglings a little too much. However, Kindred also seek him out for favors in the running of their sordid businesses. This is also a good perspective for the players' characters when they encounter Dey. They have a practical need for his influence over mortal politics and ability to manipulate the system in his favor, but in the course of their association with him, they hear some truly alarming ideas expressed with the sincere belief of someone who has seen the face of God.

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Seven Laws for Controlling the Kine

Play to their fantasies
People need hope, so let them think they have it. Say what they want to hear, even if it requires empty promises. They'll give you everything for the chance to fantasize.

Make them depend on you
The dog may bite the hand that feeds it. But intelligent people do not turn on you if you offer them what they think they need. Often, even minor conveniences can be enough.

Let them think they have power People who think they can change their circumstances at any point won't feel pressed to do so.

Do not isolate yourself
A lonely target is vulnerable to attack. Be a part of the community you feed on and make them feel they know you.

Keep your hands clean
Never make yourself complicit in actions you can get another to carry out for you; have an alibi for every order given.

But don't appear too perfect
Perfection is suspect to the kine. Let them think they know your weakness to avoid attracting their jealousy. Pay attention to any who try to take advantage.

Never show doubt; act boldly Insecurity is the mark of the outsider and the liar. Confidence signals right and will open any door and ward off suspicion.
 Hilmi An-Nasr, Salubri of the Ashirra, comments on a text by Fiorenza Savona
