Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

TITLES IN THE TOWER

Every Kindred represents a risk to the society that they are part of, and that risk is only worth it because they contribute. In Camarilla domains, the Court structure organizes and legitimizes this, ensuring that everybody knows who is in charge of which branch of the city structure. Each title comes with responsibilities

and expectations, as well as opportunities for exploitation. Which ones are filled depends on the size of the city and its needs.
Use this section as inspiration for how to play as a titled Camarilla courtier and to better understand what kind of conflicts might arise when dealing with them.

COMMON TITLES  Prince, also known as Sovereign, Prefect, Premier, Governor  Seneschal, also known as Chamberlain, Advisor, Lieutenant  Primogen, also known as Senator, Presidium member, Councilor  Whip, also known as Junior Councilor, Council Secretary  Herald, also known as Whisperer, Harpy, the News, Ambassador, Voice of the Prince  Sheriff, also known as Praetorian, Scourge, Inspector, Executioner  Keeper of Elysium, also known as Master or Mistress of Ceremonies  Principal of Faith, also known under various religious titles  Court Ritualist, also known as Court Sorcerer, the Redworker, Keeper of the Cyprianus, Prestidigitator  Shadow · Guest Hostage

66

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
Prince
Claiming praxis in a Camarilla city can be a violent affair or one done elegantly through subtle manipulations. Tonight, ancilla and neonate Kindred have increasingly been able to take over from the elders who ruled before and, in the ensuing turbulence, have been replaced several times by others like them. Tasks:  Interpreting and enforcing the Traditions  Allotting hunting grounds within the city  Holding Court and judging conflicts between
courtiers  Protecting the city's Kindred  Deciding who gets to sire and how  Keeping informed on what goes on in the wider sect
Playing the Prince: Who is planning to take over, and how are they already carrying out their schemes to take you down? Question your courtiers. Question their mortal servants, too. A Prince must never rest easy.
Playing as a Prince doesn't exempt you from the every-night existence of the vampire. You still must hunt and feed and find something to comfort your cold heart as well. But beyond your private haven, it's an existence that lets you spend a lot of time in the spotlight of sect politics, making and breaking alliances to make sure you stay in control of your Court.
Successful Princes do not rule alone but as the head of a network of political collaborators. To play as a Prince, you probably want your coterie to have roles in the inner Court and delegate as much as possible to them.
67

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Seneschal
A Prince's right-hand advisor is empowered to take over when the Prince is unavailable, and a clever one can be the difference between political success and ruin.
A Seneschal can be an official title or not, and often doubles as a Herald, Sheriff, Principal, or Ritualist.
Tasks:  Whatever tasks the Prince doesn't do  Guiding and supporting the ruler  Claiming temporary praxis to protect a city
when the Prince falls or disappears without a replacement ready
Playing the Seneschal: As a Seneschal, you may spend your time investigating courtiers vying to get closer to the Prince, advising (or manipulating) the city ruler, and working tirelessly to ensure the Court trusts you (or has no alternatives) should something happen to the Prince. You might make regular social calls to all the Primogen, subtly attempting to influence a coming vote.
A Seneschal can move both in the shadows and the light of a Kindred Court. They're often the target of suspicion, but they aren't always watched.
Primogen
Vampires aren't known for their inherent mutual trust in one another. This is why it's common for rulers to be supported by a Primogen Council of Kindred representing the most influential factions in their city. Traditionally, only the seven pillar clans were allowed representation, but tonight, the selection of Councilors often depends on who has most to say in a given city. In a city where 40% of the Kindred belong to the Church of Set, the Court will want to listen attentively to what the Setite Hierophant thinks. Sometimes each Primogen represents multiple factions, particularly in larger cities, where there are more factions than seats at the Primogen Council table.

Some cities do away with the Prince and are ruled only by the Primogen. Chicago famously was without a Prince for decades.
Tasks:  Representing the interests of their
faction--whether coterie, clan, or something else--at Court  Holding council to discuss city problems and come up with potential solutions to present to the Prince  Informing themselves of Kindred concerns in the city  Taking action to depose Princes who put wider Camarilla goals at risk
Playing a Primogen: To play a Primogen can mean engaging heavily with city politics, investing time, funds, and blackmail materials to work towards the future you envision. It can mean maintaining a mortal Mask who regularly meets up with local government officials. It can also mean simply attending the monthly meeting to vote and otherwise doing as you please.
Whip
Many Primogen would prefer to limit the amount of time they have to spend listening to the complaints of their faction's members. They sometimes appoint a Whip, who will handle that task for them, sorting through what they should consider and what they can ignore. Some Primogen have their Whip report extensively to them on the actions of their constituents.
Whips are also often empowered to sit in the Primogen's seat temporarily, if the Councilor is indisposed.
Tasks:  Performing as the stand-in for the Primogen
outside of the Primogen Council  Ensuring that decisions made in the Primogen
Council are sufficiently communicated to their faction

68

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Playing a Whip: Playing as a Whip includes carefully maneuvering to keep the trust of your Primogen and faction while being the one who often has to carry bad news back and forth. For the most part, this is a role that allows you to be a part of the Court without most of the responsibility that comes with honor and dignity. Nobody outside their own faction really cares much about the Whip--until they have to fill in for their Councilor who mysteriously disappeared.
Herald
Authorized to speak for the Prince, the Herald announces new decrees to the Court. As they sometimes act as ambassadors or get tasked with carrying important intel, they tend to have more cross-territory contact with other Kindred than most. In cities under lockdown, the Herald is sometimes the only one allowed to leave.
Heralds are sometimes also Seneschals or Keepers of Elysium, and a large Court can have a couple.
Tasks:  Announcing the decrees of the Prince  Keeping the records of prestation  Conveying messages between domains  Representing the Prince in other cities  Remembering and retelling the city's Kindred
history
Playing a Herald: There are many ways to play a Herald. One is to pretend to be a silly gossip, letting your mouth run in all the wrong ears, so you'll know who you can trust when the stories start to spread. Another is to take advantage of the freedom of movement to travel between domains, maybe even making friends you shouldn't.

Sheriff
It looks good on paper, being a Camarilla Sheriff. In reality, it's often a shitty, thankless job. You get the pleasure of chasing down ungovernable licks, all manner of dirt that has entered the city unannounced to drink, willfully obvious idiots, and half-crazed beasts. Is your night going to be spent minding your business, slapping some fingers for reaching outside of their personal domain, or tackling a crazed serial killer with the appearance of a 15-year-old girl and the strength of five horses? Who knows?
Most Sheriffs utilize a network of younger licks to help keep them informed about the goings on in the city and to handle lesser issues, often known as Hounds or Deputies.
Tasks:  Ensuring that the Traditions and any additional
Princely decrees are followed  Investigating suspected crimes and transgressions  Capturing lawbreakers  Carrying out punishments
Playing the Sheriff or a Hound: It's a privileged position to be the hand of the law. It's also a bloody difficult thing to do that no one ever thanks you for. A Sheriff might be tempted to help cover up crimes instead of carrying out consequences. They might even have to commit more to make the whole thing go away.
Playing as a Sheriff often means dealing with danger and high risk with no reward, but it can also give you the freedom to circumvent the Traditions more than most.
Playing as a Deputy or Hound is much the same, except you get a shittier deal, the Kindred respect you less, and if the Sheriff finds out you're bending the Traditions, you'll be in deep trouble.

69

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Keeper of Elysium
Being the Keeper of Elysium can be a real pleasure, assuming you have something of a talent for hosting and event planning. In cities with permanent Elysia, the Keeper is a titled courtier. In cities where the location moves, so can the title. Because of the services they provide, Keepers are often immensely popular, high-status Kindred.
Tasks:  Maintaining an Elysium location under the
Masquerade  Hosting regular Elysia  Providing entertainment and cultural sustenance
for the city's Kindred  Ensuring the safety of Elysium attendants by
taking the proper security measures
Playing the Keeper: You're a nightclub owner, some sort of blood-doll pimp, a charming hostess, and a cold war-type spy all in one. Either you're having an awful lot of fun, or you're just about to crack. The Court expects something extraordinary, but where can you find contortionist twins this time of year?
The role of Keeper is one that can be more or less demanding, depending on the expectations of the Court and the size of the city. It's never a full-time job, unless your mortal mask makes it one, so playing as a Keeper will give you plenty of opportunities to pursue different types of stories.
Principal of Faith
Some Kindred Courts include a religious authority, whose task it is to guide the city's Kindred population to make the best choices for the sect. This can be an honorary title only, a ceremonial and mostly social role, or sometimes it can be a position of real power so influential it rivals Prince or Primogen. At times, the Principal is barely tolerated at Court but fulfills a desperate need among city Kindred for some sparks of hope and guidance in

what they experience as the end times. A Principal of Faith can easily double as a
Seneschal, Herald, or Ritualist. In very religious domains, perhaps the Principal is also the city Sheriff with the authority to punish a wide range of transgressions.
Tasks:  Guiding the religious community of a Kindred
city  Keeping an eye on cult activity  Guarding and remembering holy texts and
Kindred lore  Leading Kindred ceremonies
Playing the Principal: Some city Kindred respect you above all other authorities; others want you fed to feral dogs. You get to figure out who is who, challenge your own mortal beliefs as they are confronted with Kindred reality, and heaven knows, if a blood god does manifest, you'll be the first one they all turn to.
You can also occupy yourself by grasping for as much power as possible. Look at what the other titled courtiers are doing. Why is it the Ritualist performing the coronation? Why does the Keeper of Elysium welcome the Court? Who told the Herald they were the best voice of the Prince when you are right here, speaking for the gods as well?
Court Ritualist
A thaumaturge can make a decent existence for themselves in a Camarilla city as that city's chosen sorcerer. In return, they draw on their craft to strengthen the city's defenses or perform tricks and wonders for the Court. They may also be in charge of merely symbolic rites, such as the "coronation" of a new Prince or the welcoming of fledglings into the sect as full neonates. A Camarilla city can have several Kindred sorcerers, but there's usually only ever one main Ritualist.
In the past, a coterie of Tremere usually held this role in a city, but not every city had even a single

70

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Tremere. What was once a rural or ancient practice has returned to larger cities in the modern nights.
Tasks:  Learning and leading Rituals for the protection
of the Court  Guarding a Court collection of occult materials  Leading Kindred ceremonies
Playing as the Ritualist: No one can know you invented all of these impressive rites by yourself and that they aren't actually from an ancient Tremere library. It worked for Blavatsky, it'll work for you. Add just a dash of real sorcery, Oblivion if need be, and the flickering lights of the dying satellites above your chosen ritual site will do the rest.
Regardless of the nature of your tricks, the comfort you provide can be very real. What will you use it for? How far will you go in manipulating and guiding the city's Kindred? What will you make them give you for your help? And how will you handle the Principal of Faith and all their claims on ceremonies that are rightly yours?
Shadow
Sometimes when a coterie enters a city, the Court appoints a Shadow to keep an eye on them. A Shadow can also be assigned to a new coterie of local childer. The Shadow is officially meant to guide their wards and often doubles as a Mawla, but some are just glorified Court spies. Any city Kindred can be appointed as a Shadow. While it is a job that many Kindred consider as convenient as jury duty, others eagerly accept it for the chance to grow their influence.
Tasks:  Welcoming, informing, and guiding their wards  Ensuring that the culture and rules of the city
domain are properly understood  Reporting to the Court on the actions of their wards
Playing as a Shadow: A Shadow is just a normal city Kindred who has been given a temporary title. It

can be a way to wiggle your way into more influence, as you finally have the ear of higher-ranking courtiers, or an opportunity to delegate tasks you aren't interested in doing yourself.
For troupes that want to play mixed generations, having the older character play the Shadow of the younger coterie can be a good way to do it.
Guest Hostage
Sometimes neighboring domains, or conflicting factions, find it useful to exchange hostages. In Camarilla cities, these are generally treated with respect, as befits a society sworn to the Tradition of Hospitality. This custom can be very elegant--as set by the example of Tegyrius the Vizier's arrival into Toreador territory--or it can be a simple transaction of insurance. Depending on the importance of the exchange, a Court may choose to send someone important to them or use this as a way to get rid of individuals who have fallen out of favor.
Tasks:  Representing a Court, bloodline, or clan in
another domain  Acting as insurance that treaties will not be
broken
Playing a Guest Hostage: You play as any other member of your coterie, doing as your Mawla tells you, attempting to work off your debt to the Court, seeking to please your elders. You make something akin to friends and find a kind of joy in the company of certain local mortals. Sometimes you remember you were stolen from your real home, your haven, your mortal connections and immortal loves--what will you do about it?

71

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

RULES AND TRADITIONS

The Camarilla exists to protect Kindred interests. Three in particular: keeping the Beast at bay, shielding Kindred societies from their enemies, and ensuring that every member can feed (be that through population control or the division of territories). These aren't easy tasks, and everything the sect does is meant to serve them. This includes its culture. Where a bitter old lick might claim that the Sabbat is held together by a shared experience of otherness and the Ashirra was united by faith, Camarilla territories are held together by tradition.
The Six Traditions
The Traditions are the closest thing the Tower has to a codified international law and the backbone of the sect's culture, as they determine what you should not do but also set the target for what you should. They are usually the first thing you must agree to before you are Embraced by a Camarilla Kindred, and you must confirm them when you are accepted as a full neonate member of the sect. That doesn't mean every domain interprets them the same. There are local variations, and, although ignorance is no excuse, just as with mortal laws, some individuals get away with more.

The Tradition of the Masquerade
The most important of Camarilla rules is a promise to keep the mortal world ignorant of the existence of Kindred. It is a responsibility to mask as mortals, infiltrate mortal societies, and clean up any messes that might suggest to mortals they are prey.
Variations
Monstrous Destruction: The Court actively locates and destroys all other non-mortals in their territory to ensure total control over the kine. Casual Compliance: Trusting the mortal urge to self-gaslight, a Masquerade breach is not considered as such unless it has actual consequences. Absolutist Conservatism: The Court has a clearly defined list of transgressions and punishes them harshly, even when they do not result in a Masquerade breach.
Exceptions
Prince's Pet: A favored Kindred gets away with risky behavior; less-favored individuals are assigned to clean up after them. Impossible Challenge: Some Kindred are walking Masquerade breaches because of their inhuman appearance, but as they offer something of importance to the city, the Court protects them and, if necessary, provides the infrastructure that keeps their existence from the kine.

72

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Fuckup Fixed: A close-knit coterie and other allies who will clean up accidents and provide an alibi can mean the difference between destruction and walking away from a major fuckup without a scratch.
Pitfalls
Family and Friends: Few Courts tolerate a lick baring their secrets to the ones they love.

Beastly Behavior: Feeding carries a risk; not feeding carries a bigger risk because it cannot be maintained. Mortal Surveillance: The society of the kine is growing increasingly Orwellian, and Kindred should assume they are always observed.
The trick is to make your actions look innocent. The vast amount of data that can be collected is not often thoroughly examined, and even AI attempts to sort through the noise don't return as many real, damning details as many would suspect.

TEN WAYS THE SI INTERCEPTS KINDRED COMMUNICATION 1. Utilizing existing surveillance systems: Street-level surveillance is accessed following suspected Kindred activity. 2. Bugging: Suspected Kindred meeting spots, dead drops, and hunting zones are bugged with microphones
and cameras. 3. Wiretapping: There's no real way to secure regular cellular calls from being intercepted. Two or three
software commands are all it takes to listen in on any given phone call. 4. Backdooring Devices: By hacking into a user's device and inserting malware, remote access can be achieved
and end-to-end encrypted apps monitored. 5. Phishing: SI scammers socially engineer content they assume to be especially tempting to Kindred to access
sensitive data through fraudulent solicitation in email, on a website, or through fraudulent text messages. 6. AI Pattern Analysis: SI organizations have started training language models on Kindred communications,
including statements made under torture, to discover patterns. The plan is to let said language models trawl internet forums and social media for matching anomalies. 7. Honeytraps: SI agents create profiles on social media, dating apps, trade sites, and more, posing as tempting vessels or other Kindred. They may monitor or even run supposedly secure forums. 8. Dark Web Scraping: Using specialized web crawler tools, agencies extract data from websites and forums normally hidden from traditional searches. 9. Postal Interception: SI agencies are not above the interception of traditional mail. 10. Undercover Agents: SI agencies collaborate with Kindred traitors and mortal Camarilla agents for intel. They have reportedly even been experimenting with creating their own lick agents, forcing Kindred prisoners to Embrace against their will.

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

I. The Masquerade Thou shall not reveal thy true nature to those not of the Blood. Doing such shall
renounce thy claims of Blood.
II. Domain Thy domain is thine own concern. All others owe thee respect while in it. None may challenge thy word while in thy
domain.
III. Progeny Thou shall only Sire another with the permission of thine elder. If thou createst another without thine Elder's leave, both thou and thy Progeny shall be slain.
IV. Accounting Those thou create are thine own children. Until thy Progeny shall be Released, thou shall command thewm in all things. Their
sins are thine to endure.
V. Hospitality Honor one another's domain. When thou comest to a foreign city, thou shall present thyself to the one who ruleth there. Without the word of acceptance,
thou art nothing.
VI. Destruction Thou art forbidden to destroy another of thy kind. The right of destruction belongeth only to thine Elder. Only the
Eldest among thee shall call the Blood Hunt.

The Tradition of Domain
The division of domains is what keeps Camarilla cities civilized and protected from mortal suspicion, ensuring that there are kine enough for every Kindred. It also lets the Court know whom to hold responsible when something problematic occurs. The city itself is the domain of its Prince, who divides it into lesser domains among their courtiers, who in turn can partition it out in return for favors, and so on. While each domain holder is the lord of their territory, they still answer to their liege lord.
Variations
Villeinism: City Kindred who owe their domain directly to the Prince are what fiefs were to medieval kings. But less-favored Kindred, such as fledgling coteries that have not yet been formally accepted into the sect, often only have permission to establish their haven in another's domain in return for service and obedience. Moving Domains: City domains aren't granted permanently but rotate every few years. Domain Neutrality: Some cities are not fully Camarilla but also have a sizable Anarch or Ashirra population. In such cities, "enemy" activity in sect territory may be tolerated to protect the peace. Restricted Feeding: Hunting in one's home domain is forbidden to protect the Masquerade. Mortal Responsibilities: Domain holders are expected to answer for certain mortal activities within their domain. This is especially common where Kindred masquerade as mortal authorities.
Exceptions
Alibi Provided: If a domain holder wasn't home at the time of the transgression and can provide a good reason for it, they can try to argue that they cannot be held responsible. (Super)Natural Disasters: It is an unreasonable ruler who would blame a domain holder for the destruction caused by hurricanes or packs of lupines.

74

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Pitfalls
Sudden Thirst: Letting Hunger build in unknown territories can be troublesome, as not all domain holders allow unrestricted feeding. Free Territory: Assuming that a seemingly unclaimed territory is up for grabs might cause problems when the original domain holder returns. Princely Whims: The Prince can take and grant domains, but sometimes the vassal being punished refuses to cooperate or takes it out on the new owners.
The Tradition of Progeny
Securing the right to decide who shall be allowed to Embrace and when helps the city ruler guard against Kindred overpopulation and the weakening of bloodlines. Increasingly, Courts are becoming aware that there is a correlation between generation and the abomination of the Duskborn and are planning accordingly.
The standard punishment for the unsanctioned creation of childer begins with the destruction of the childe.
Variations
Wartime Embraces: Sabbat Cainites notoriously Embrace undiscerningly when needing soldiers. Some Camarilla Courts will also lift some of their requirements when conflict threatens. Planned Embraces: The Prince doesn't allow members of their Court to choose or suggest their own desired progeny but has a list of mortal subjects to work through.

died otherwise (and why that would be a great loss). Neonate Deception: This Tradition can be skirted if everyone can somehow be convinced the childe was Embraced long ago and only just resurfaced.
Pitfalls
Tragic Accident: A Kindred who feels deeply for mortals can find it hard to resist granting the Embrace when there's no way to save their life. Guilty Conscience: A Kindred who drinks too deeply and didn't mean to kill might be tempted to Embrace. Obsession: It is dangerous to slake one's thirst on the same mortal often because their blood can become so sweet it feels like love.
The Tradition of Accounting
Camarilla domains would never work if their members did not monitor each other for transgressions. This is partly ensured by the Tradition of Accounting, which holds every sire responsible for their childer until those childer are officially accepted as full members of the sect. In return, a fledgling childe must obey their maker.
Variations
Eternal Bonds: Some conservative Courts honor the Tradition of Accounting even beyond a childe's full acceptance into the sect. Only their sire's exile, disappearance, or final death releases them. Shadow Guardianship: Instead of the bloodline sire, a Shadow can inherit the charge of a fledgling ward.

Exceptions
Special Childe: Forgiveness for an unplanned childe can sometimes be achieved if no one hears about the fledgling until after they have proven to be essential to the sect. From Death's Claws: Some Princes look more mildly on unsanctioned Embraces if the sire can prove it was unplanned and the childe would have

Exceptions
Anarch Upstart: A sire may be released from the expectation of Accounting if their childe declares for the Anarchs. Parricide: A childe who attempts to destroy their sire, or in other ways acts with significant malice toward them, may no longer be considered their childe.

75

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Failure of the Blood: Some Courts do not consider Caitiff and thin-blood childer to be of their sire's Blood as it has failed to express itself in them.

cannot travel through one end of the city to another without clearing it with every domain holder on the way.

Pitfalls
The Kind Sire: A childe can forget the Tradition of Accounting if their sire is too lax with them. Arranged Embrace: Some Courts cannot maintain the Tradition of Accounting if they also do not allow sires to choose their own progeny.
The Tradition of Hospitality
This Tradition is a remnant of pre-modern times, when people traveled on foot or rode horses and needed to rely on being welcomed along the way. Even though Kindred travelers can use more efficient means of travel, the road would still be too dangerous if they could not count on resting and feeding in Camarilla cities. Part of this promise is that visitors will present themselves to the city's leadership on arrival. This way, the Court can ensure the visitor doesn't pose a threat (beyond the expected) and point out any feeding restrictions.
Variations
Timed Presentation: The grace period Kindred can expect when arriving differs from city to city. Some Princes do not care if it takes up to a week for them to present themselves; some expect it to happen on the first night of arrival. Cultural Expectations: Some Princes look for any chance to claim disrespect of local customs so they can chase off visitors. Quarantined City: In times of crisis, Courts can choose to go into hiding and will actively avoid all contact with visitors, pretending that they aren't even there. Borrowed Time: Some Courts graciously accept visitors but limit the visit to a certain number of nights or weeks. Closed Domains: In some cities, the Tradition is also honored in every smaller domain, and Kindred

Exceptions
Visiting Authority: In principle, Kindred who represent a higher Camarilla authority than the local Prince are free to travel in and out without announcing themselves. Known Camarilla Enemy: The sect releases any city from the Tradition of Hospitality when there is an active Blood Hunt on the visitor.
Pitfalls
Assuming Free Reign: Just because a territory seems deserted does not mean it is. Cultural Differences: What is considered essential etiquette in one city may be rude in another.
The Tradition of Destruction
A Camarilla Prince's authority is anchored in their right to judge a vassal for destruction. At the same time, no other Kindred in a city may freely destroy another without their Prince's leave. The Prince's power isn't unchecked, however. A ruler who destroys too liberally is quickly deposed.
Variations
Sire's Right: Fledglings who have not officially entered the sect as full members also do not enjoy its protection. Considered to be their sire's property, that same sire is free to grant them final death if they desire. Abject Others: Certain categories of licks are free prey for anyone, such as Duskborn, Caitiff, Anarchs, Sabbat Cainites, or Kindred of a certain bloodline.
Exceptions
Destroy or Be Destroyed: Destruction in selfdefense can find forgiveness.

76

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Punishment Befitting Crime: Some transgressions are anathema to Camarilla Kindred, and unsanctioned destruction pales in comparison. Under the Influence: If a Kindred can successfully convince the Court that they were manipulated or not in their right mind, destruction may not be punished as it usually would--but there will certainly be other consequences. At the Edges of the Domain: Some ne'er-do-wells argue that a Prince's right of destruction ends at the edge of their domain, rather than with their subjects. Heading to the "international waters" of lupine territory or a neighboring domain has other risks, but leaving a rival to meet the sun in the countryside is a time-tested tradition. Prince in Name Only: As a result of blackmail or some other weakness, a city's Prince has been barred from the right of destruction in practice. The reality is that the right either belongs to no Kindred in the city or lies with another, maybe hidden, authority.

Pitfalls
Frenzy: A lick who cannot control their inner Beast, cannot control their drive to rip and rend without end. Unsafe Feeding Practices: Kindred who tempt fate by Blood Bonding to one another over repeated feeding sessions or indulge in the practice of circular feeding* risk accidental destruction. Being Embraced: The Embrace requires a taste of vitae. If the sire is very weakened, they may not be able to fight off the fledgling who starts their undead existence as a diablerist.
*Circular Feeding, also known as Bloodlooping or "the perpetual battery," is the practice where two or more licks drink from one another unceasingly and only avoid being fully drained because they fill themselves at the same time. It is looked down on by most Kindred.

PUNISHMENTS FOR CAMARILLA CRIMES THE SADISTIC RULER  Memory edited: happy memories erased, trauma added in, wishes and directives changed  Forced to feed only on animals for an extended period  Torture, such as the repeated removal of an appendage over and over again; torture of mortal kin THE STRATEGIC RULER  Haven, or other assets, including mortal connections, temporarily seized or re-allocated  Blood Bonded to a loyal courtier  Exchanged as a hostage THE HARSH RULER  Forced into torpor in the ruler's holding cells  Branded  Destroyed by fire, sunlight, or through diablerie THE DISTRACTED RULER  Being fined  Exile · Being tasked with something no one else wants to do

77

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Common Rules
and Courtesies
Camarilla domains have a culture as much as any society, and the Six Traditions are far from the only rules the Kindred are expected to honor. Below are some additional customs most cities observe. Naturally, Princes who rule through fear (such as the Caligula; see p. 18) can allow themselves a wide range of further eccentricities.

Prestation
Prestation is the practice of trading favors and boons. A Kindred's word is their bond, and to break a promise in Camarilla territory is a good way to ensure that you lose all respect. In most domains, granting a boon is a ceremonial procedure, but in some, you're considered bound the moment you voice intention.
Kindred society generally recognizes four categories of boons: trivial, minor, major, and life boons. This can become culturally tricky when two Kindred disagree on the size of a favor.

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Variations
Blood or Nothing: Prestation primarily counts for matters of life and death. A Kindred who has been saved from final death must repay their savior with a life in return. Ticking Clock: Favors incurred must be repaid within a certain time period, usually set during the negotiation. Inherited Debt: A debt can be passed on from one Kindred to another, or even be sold.
Exceptions
Favors of Office: A Kindred who acts in service of the Court usually will not incur personal prestation debt. Unfair Terms: Kindred who can argue that they were tricked can sometimes get out of fulfilling an unreasonable debt--if they can get the Court to care.
Pitfalls
Favor Debt: Kindred who aren't careful to keep count of the favors they incur can get in trouble when their creditors come calling. Nonchalant Promises: Failing to negotiate the terms of a favor trade can mean that there are no limits to the demands the owed party can make in return.
Blood Bonds
The Blood Bond is a contested subject in some Camarilla cities. Still, they are widely practiced to enslave mortal ghouls and often play a part in sire-childe relationships. Until recently, the Tremere were almost entirely organized around them. And of course, they have been used to secure diplomatic relations. Perhaps this is the very reason most members of the sect prefer to pretend that they are one tool among several, an occasional necessity, rather than the sensual indulgence that is the actual experience of the Bond.

Variations
Ignorance Is Innocence: The Court has forbidden informing fledglings about the Bond. It usually works about as well as teaching teenagers that babies sprout from flowers. Sanctioned Pairings: The city sanctions preapproved Kindred Blood Weddings and celebrates them with prominent rites. Only Almost: The city exclusively allows one-way Blood Bonds to high-ranking courtiers.
Exceptions
For a Cause: Blood Bonds done for a greater cause are exempted from limitations. To the Terror of Tremere: A vengeful Prince specifically permits other Kindred to Blood Bond Tremere to mock the clan for its inability to Bond in return.
Pitfalls
Conversational Faux Pas: In many cities, pointing out that two Kindred appear to be Bonded is extremely rude and something that might earn the careless commenter some serious trouble. Accidental Bond: It usually only takes three drinks from the same source of vitae to become Bonded. Paired with ignorance or desperation, accidental Bonds do happen.

79

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower
10 COMMON CITY COURTESIES 1. Greeting the Social Authority: Most cities have both a formal leader and an informal social authority.
Sometimes the Prince, or their representative, fulfills both these roles. But often, there is also a harpy or Keeper of Elysium who feel snubbed if city visitors fail to acknowledge them. 2. Elysium Etiquette: It is common knowledge not to use powers of the Blood in Elysium and to be on one's best behavior. 3. Feeding Respectfully: Unless a guest has been otherwise directed, most Kindred find it rude if they indulge too deeply on the mortal population. 4. Outcasts to Be Reported: Any and all members of a specific faction are considered enemies of the city, and you're expected to report them to a member of the Court if you should spot one. 5. Respecting Mortal Domain: The mortal contacts of one Kindred are not to be interfered with by another without prior agreement. 6. Courtly Amnesia: When visiting a city Court, it is polite to forget all about interactions with former courtiers unless the ruler themselves should happen to remember in conversation. 7. Curfew: Vampires are expected to be back inside their havens an hour before sunup. A host will invite guests to spend the day should they stay too long at a gathering. 8. Remnants of Past Times: A Kindred from the 1980s expects a firm handshake and a gift when playing host. One from the 1880s will be angry if you don't remove your hat inside or feed without saying grace. 9. Certain Diet Restrictions: Especially in Ventrue domains, a visitor can expect that certain flavors of blood are reserved for higher-ranking Kindred. 10. Honoring Age and Generation: The way of the Beast dictates that strength begets respect. Many elders have left their positions, but most Courts still expect subjects to treat a Ninth-Generation ancilla with considerably more deference than yesternight's fledgling.
Halima bit the inside of her cheek, hard enough to taste the iron of her own vitae, to center herself in the moment. Three pursuers were stalking her from the north end of the tunnels; she'd heard their voices travel through the pipes to her hideout just in time to throw a flashlight in her backpack and get out of there. Her knowledge of the underground would allow her to stay ahead of them down here. But eventually, she'd have to exit to feed. Scanning her mental map of the university's subterranean territory, with its web of utility tunnels, sewers, and waterways, she gave a start as she heard the unmistakable shuffle of something moving ahead of her. Rats, or something worse? She sent a brief thought of longing back to a former self, who'd been up there in the light. Then she pulled herself together. She'd wanted to avoid going through the sewers, but it wasn't like she had to breathe. And she felt fairly certain the suits her sire had sent after her would hesitate to follow there.
Halima was a good half hour deep in human waste when she felt the pinprick sensation of danger ahead. Turning the corner, she saw the creature: more chimera than her, it seemed to be part rodent, part overgrown fetus, and it was nestled into a fatberg like it never wanted to leave. Bizarrely, it smirked at her.
"I've been promised a reward for your watery blood, kitten. Shall I collect, or do you have something better to offer me?"

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

THE CITY

The quasi-feudal domains of the Camarilla are mostly locked in mutual agreements to leave one another alone, united by their shared allegiance to the sect and its Traditions. These domains are almost always city-based and controlled by a Prince or regent, who, in turn, grants lesser domains to their vassals within the city's borders.
Shadows of
Mortal Cities
A Camarilla city is the parasitic shadow of a mortal city, leeching off its politics and businesses. Some Kindred Courts were there before the cities they inhabit were built, and their courtiers masquerade as pillars of their mortal society. Others have established themselves as racketeers, hiding out in high-class treatment facilities in the guise of therapists and doctors. Some have needed to found a tech startup. Whatever the cover, while some Kindred may prefer to gloss over their dependency on mortals, a lick cannot go far without mortal collaborators.
Neighboring
Territories
Most Camarilla cities function as self-organized ecosystems, but Courts are usually aware of who their closest neighbors are. Other Kindred provide a shield against mutual enemies, but they also should not be too close unless the mortal population is large enough to handle it. A city like São Paulo can easily host several Kindred city domains, while suspicion would quickly grow out of control in Leeuwarden in the Netherlands. Anarchs must be

closely monitored for signs of ill intent. Autarkies and cities with suspected Sabbat presence or lupine dominance might represent dark parts of the map that Camarilla Kindred avoid when traveling.
It's good practice for Princes who share border areas to show one another respect. Sometimes hostages are exchanged--one childe for another--and gifts will be sent through representatives, who also share intel on the greater sect. Individuals who have fallen out of favor in one Court might try their luck in another, although cities with friendly relations might be in the practice of handing over criminals.
Manipulating
Mortal Politics
A mortal city inhabited by Kindred will, in some ways, be influenced by their internal politics. These are some ways Court politics can blend with mortal politics:  Urban Planning Lobbyism: Kindred with
influence in city zoning can quietly push for protection of Camarilla buildings under the guise of historical preservation.  Misinformation Campaigns: Manipulating mortal journalists to plant inflammatory stories can help you bury the truth of a Masquerade breach.  Crisis Engineering: You can leak a mortal scandal to distract city police and authorities from vampire-related incidents.  Conduit Contributions: Donate to a mortal political party through a straw donor to circumvent contribution limits and hide your identity as you influence the decisions of a mortal politician.  Philanthropic Work: A charitable foundation can be an excuse to host elaborate late-night events and also lets you launder money. Of course, you need to actually support a cause a little. For the optics.

81

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

SAMPLE CITY DOMAINS

The politics of getting a dozen Kindred to share a city peacefully and ruling over some 70 licks belonging to different factions, some of which you aren't even aware of, are different. These sample city domains let you know what kind of cultural particularities and problems you might expect from Kindred cities of different sizes as a vampire trying to gain influence or just make it as a citizen.
Small
At 12 or fewer Kindred, a domain is definitely small.
Examples: Matera, Bruges, Hoi An, Paraty, Burlington
Court Structure
In a city with no more than a dozen Kindred, there's no need to fill every possible position. Everyone has some informal role to play, but there's no need to bother with a Primogen Council or a Keeper of Elysium. Some titles remain particularly useful, however:  A Prince, who can represent the city when other
Camarilla Kindred appear in it and who can make the final decision when the domain cannot agree.  A Herald, who can act on behalf of that Prince and who can travel around with news if the population is spread over a larger area or are avoiding each other purposefully.

 A Sheriff, who protects the mortal population and the domain in general. The same Kindred likely have several
responsibilities. Perhaps the chosen Prince is also in charge of hosting social gatherings, and that is how they got the role. The Herald and the Sheriff may be one and the same, declaring new rules while they punish transgressions. Perhaps a Guest Hostage from another small nearby domain is also the Court Ritualist and was chosen specifically because the domain doesn't otherwise have anyone skilled in Blood Sorcery.
Cultural Particularities
No Privacy: Everyone knows everyone, including their usual habits. It's noticed pretty quickly when the Nosferatu Melchior, who feeds every night in different locations in the red-light district, stops showing up. He's also the first suspect when a Masquerade breach happens there.
Gossip Runs the Show: New arrivals can count on anything they do or say spreading quickly between locals, and if they piss off one, they may have a hard time winning over others. On the other hand, a small community may be more willing to entertain irregular ideas and behaviors, simply because the Court is itching for fresh entertainment.
A Little Strangeness: Small domains can sometimes get a little strange, especially if geographically isolated. If you haven't had a Camarilla visitor in decades and you only ever socialize with the same crowd of Enlightenment

82

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

enthusiasts, you may earnestly think Gehenna has already ended and the Ravnos are in charge.
Strengths and Weaknesses
Peacetime Versus Wartime: Small domains are easy to control but hard to defend. Attacks from the outside can be devastating if they happen.
Covering Your Tracks: It's easy to get away with breaking the Traditions if no one catches you in the act, but if they do, you'll have a hard time shifting the blame onto someone else.
Few Alternative Friends: Once you fall out of favor with the majority of the group, or just with its leaders, the city will become cold to you indeed. There'll be nowhere else to turn, so you'll have to make good with the same Kindred or accept defeat and find another city to call home.
Mid-Sized
Small domains tend to become mid-sized by the time they hit 15­18 members, and domains are definitely mid-sized at two dozen.
Examples: Munich, Budapest, Florence, Atlanta
Court Structure
A mid-sized domain can make due with a small central Court, consisting of:  A Prince and/or a Primogen Council to rule.  A Seneschal or Herald to advise and speak for the
leadership in its absence.  A Keeper of Elysium to gather the community.  A Sheriff or two to keep order.  The existence of assistant courtiers, such as
Whips, Hounds, and Shadows, is usually more a matter of temperament than need. In domains with several opposing factions, the number of titled courtiers can, however, grow to what might seem unnecessary proportions if everyone insists on being represented. The Court might not need three Principals of Faith to guide a population of 30 Kindred, most of whom are non-believers, but

if it offends the one Catholic ancilla not to have a title when there is one for the Mithraic faction and another for the Muslim, it might be easier for everyone to give him one.
Cultural Particularities
A Variety of Factions: Mid-sized domains combine several factions of Kindred. There can be a fair degree of competitiveness, as they vie for political control and leadership's favor, but such cities are still small enough that it's unlikely they shelter large, undiscovered groups of outright enemies of the sect.
A Good Place to Start: For the ambitious neonate, this is the perfect place to attempt to propel themselves upward since the Kindred population is big enough to provide several opportunities to win over allies but small enough that they can get a sense of who is who.
Some Normalcy: Camarilla culture is more likely to align with what is generally true for the sect, as visitors travel through on a semi-regular basis. However, eccentric or strict rulers often add on more rules, using Gehenna, faith, sorcery, or mortal politics as the excuse. They may not get away with forcing a specific diet on every Kindred citizen, but they can certainly impose a strict curfew or influence a general hatred for Clan Malkavian.
Strengths and Weaknesses
Big Enough to Matter: Big enough to be visible on the global map. Small enough to be vulnerable to attack by a few Sabbat or lupine packs, mortal military, or a wandering blood god from an ancient era. This vulnerability makes mid-sized Courts especially eager to attract and hold on to strong allies, and even young Kindred can get a chance to prove themselves here if they have something interesting to bargain with.
Access to Feeding Rights: Depending on the size of the mortal population, individual domains may be highly contested or spread out and comfortably large.

83

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Large
A domain becomes large around 50 Kindred.
Examples: New York City, Chicago, Paris, Seoul, Johannesburg
Court Structure
A large domain requires a sizable Court with every essential position filled and a support system of assistant courtiers:  The Prince (or Princes) needs a Seneschal and
cannot rule without being regularly challenged unless there is also a Primogen Council in place.  There is likely more than one Sheriff and a coterie of Hounds who support them in their work.  The Keeper of Elysium is an important title since without them, the Court never gathers.  If the geographical city is divided between several factions, some Camarilla and some not, there can be several Guest Hostages on each side to keep the peace, unless the city is in active civil war.  Large domains are more likely to include small communities that insist on a Principal of Faith, even if the domain is otherwise irreligious.

Cultural Particularities
Culturally Diverse: Large domains are less likely to have a citywide ban on specific Kindred factions, except for very rare or unpopular ones, because tolerance is required for the city to function night to night. While members of the same faction may clump together by having neighboring domains, you will still meet others who are vastly different from you when you attend Court and Elysium events.
The Importance of Tradition: These differences make the honoring of Camarilla culture all the more important--while you won't get stared at or punished for looking a bit different, crimes against the Traditions are harshly punished.
Systematic Rule: The Court doesn't have time to take individual particularities into consideration every time and tends to punish first and question later, if at all.
Strengths and Weaknesses
Easy to Fly Under the Radar: No one can keep track of every member of a large Kindred population at all times. Cities this size have new arrivals appearing on a regular basis, Kindred who enter torpor and wake up again, and individuals who change sides in struggles that ebb and flow like an ungentle sea. This makes it easy for an unsanctioned fledgling to fly under the radar, as well as for Sabbat spies to enter and set up shop.
Mouse in a Maze: On the other hand, once the city has noticed that you're in it, it can become very difficult to remain hidden. If you are the target, a Blood Hunt through the streets of a metropolis teaming with enthusiastic hunters will almost certainly be the end of you unless you find a way out of the city quickly.

84

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

CREATING A CAMARILLA CHARACTER

Not all Camarilla Kindred are created equal. If you're preparing a character for political play in the sect, you can find inspiration for what to consider here. Everything, of course, depends on whether your character is someone new to the sect or an existing Tower bat.
New Arrival
Newly arrived Kindred have to prove themselves before they can expect even a lukewarm welcome.
Accounting for Your Background
The Court will want to know where the players' characters come from, what they are, and what they've done before. A trusting or distracted Prince may take them at their word; most will require more.
Sire and Bloodline: Is your pedigree respectable? Is your sire someone with pull within the wider sect and this city in specific? If they aren't, sometimes you can trade favors for faked parentage.
Clan- and Diablerie-Detection: Courts have ways of determining a Kindred's clan and the nature of their vitae when there's doubt. Does your character have anything to hide, and if so, how will

they fool the Blood Sorcerer using Taste for Blood or the thin-blood alchemist with their suitcase full of chemistry experiments? If they can't, how do they convince them that their past is not a problem?
Sponsorship: Arrivals vouched for by a respected city member will be accepted sooner and start climbing the hierarchy. Do you already have someone on the inside who can recommend you?
Negotiating Your Position
Figuring Out Who Is in Charge: It is one thing to identify the official ruler of a city, and it is another to figure out who really pulls the strings.
Declaring Your Intentions: Before a Court will consider accepting a new coterie, they'll expect to hear its binding declarations of intent--preferably in front of several Kindred witnesses who'll swear they heard the same. How will your group convince the Court to give you a chance?
Making Connections: It can be a struggle to prove that you are worth serious consideration as a young Kindred in Camarilla territory. Finding out who can help you on your journey and convincing them to do so is essential.

85

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Dealing with Mortals
Identifying Retainers: Knowing which mortals are in the service of the sect is the best way to make sure you aren't being observed by the Court when you don't want to be.
Creating Your Network: A Kindred needs mortals they can count on to help them navigate the mortal city. Building such relationships is an important part of establishing yourself.
Existing Member
Perhaps you were Embraced into the city or have already been allowed to settle.
Faction and Allies
Sire: Who is your sire, and how do they help you within the sect? How do they hold you back, and what are your strategies for dealing with that?
Belonging to a Group: Every mid-sized or large city has several political factions, be they determined by clan loyalty, political agenda, age, beliefs, or something else. Which one is yours?
Allies and Enemies: Whom can you count on and why? What did you do to make them owe you a favor? Who has it out for you, and how did you piss them off?
Court Role: Every Kindred has a function within the sect system. How do you make yourself useful? Is it just keeping an eye out for intruders on a regular basis? Are you tasked with providing psychological crisis help to the Seneschal's mortal victims? Are you scrubbing blood out of the Prince's hot tub?
Navigating Court
Advancing an Agenda: The sect is a complicated political landscape, and even if you try to stay neutral, you will be used in the schemes of others. Eventually, you will have plans of your own as well. What are they?
Collecting Favors: Camarilla careers are made and broken in the favor trade. To climb high, make sure you are owed more than you owe.

Defending the Sect: Young coteries are usually frontline soldiers when a city has any kind of trouble. When have you been called into action?
Working Off Debt: Many Courts consider the Embrace the first favor a fledgling incurs and expects them to work it off. Arrivals from outside the city might incur their first local favor when they are allowed to settle. What other debts have you incurred?
Negotiating for Lenience: Every Kindred will at some point need someone to look the other way. It might be helpful to make sure courtiers like the Sheriff or the Shadow know what they can expect in return if you ever need their help.
Your Mortal Network
Protecting Friends and Family: Unscrupulous courtiers will use your mortal contacts against you. You'll need to make arrangements to stop that from happening.
Maintaining Your Network: How do you keep your mortal contacts happy so they keep helping you out?
Spies on the Inside: Which mortals in the service of other Kindred are you aware of, and are you working to get any of them on your side?
WHAT CAMARILLA SECT POLITICS ARE REALLY ABOUT The younger lick may fear that they won't survive long in the sect. They worry that if they fuck up, they will face destruction before they can make up for it. But the sect's goal isn't to destroy its members. It's to control them. Most courtiers want to take advantage of those below them. They want them to be afraid, but only enough to do as they are told.
This is also true: the Camarilla exists to shield its members from the Beast. Humanity is a resource like any other, and while Kindred must be in control of their emotions, pure psychopathy is not desirable. Thus, there is also softness in the system; it just doesn't often get to drive it.

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

GLOBAL POWER PLAYERS

The Camarilla is mythologized as a globespanning secret society, strictly controlled from the top. This is a story that serves those in power and tickles the imagination, so it is often repeated among Kindred. Perhaps it's also true: the founders remain, influencing every echelon. Another possibility is that they have all been destroyed or fallen into torpor, or that their childer have devoured them and their ideals, and the Kindred are on their own with only fabricated myths of a united leadership to guide them. For most, it will be difficult to verify the truth; they'll never meet any power higher than the Princes who rule the cities they hunt and hide in.
Until one--or a pretender--does make an appearance.
Inner Circle Member
The Inner Circle is said to be the true leaders of the Camarilla, to whom the Princes must answer if called. Their identities are a well-kept secret, but many believe them to be among the eldest from the founding clans.
Rumored Activities:  Meeting every 13 years to appoint new Justicars  Orchestrating the sect's responses to global crises
Possible Truths:  The Camarilla founders were the Inner Circle
and remain so--fewer than they ever were before, as several have been lost.

 The Inner Circle no longer exists, and the Justicars do what they please.
 The Inner Circle has gone underground to wait out the Second Inquisition.
 There are several Inner Circles competing for control behind the scenes.
 The Inner Circle has suffered a schism, and the wider sect will soon face civil war.
 The Inner Circle is a ploy by some ancient creature who plays the Camarilla Kindred as puppets.
Justicar
The Inner Circle's chosen swords are supposedly authorized to break all Traditions to protect the sect. They're said to serve for 13 years at a time and be the only Kindred in existence who know the true identity of the sect's leaders. Awe-inspiring symbols of the sect's power to control its cities, the idea of them can be terrifying to those who have something to hide.
Through the ages, the identity of the Justicars has sometimes been widely known and at other times uncertain. As of 2011, the known Justicars were Juliette Parr, Molly Macdonald, Diana Iadanza, Ian Carfax, and Lucinde. Since 2024, their identities have been veiled in gossip and uncertainty, though most agree Lucinde continues.

88

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Rumored Activities:  Destroying Duskborn, Caitiff, wights, and
mortals who know too much when a Prince fails to do so  Culling childer in cities where Princes have let them become overpopulated  Instating and removing Princes  Punishing crimes against the Traditions  Drafting Kindred to serve as Archons and for various other purposes  Calling conclaves and negotiating on behalf of the Inner Circle  Guarding the Camarilla's sleeping ancients
Possible Truths:  Lucinde is the only remaining Justicar.  The Justicars are the true leaders of the sect.  One among the Justicars is a traitor and has been
working against the sect from within.  The Tremere have claimed an extra Justicar seat.

Archon
The Archons are the professed field agents of the Justicars. There have been known Archons, or those pretending to be so, functioning as inter-Court diplomats or traveling executioners. However, for the most part, they work quietly, fulfilling their orders as they travel between domains.
Rumored Activities:  Spying on Camarilla Kindred to identify
unworthy members  Preserving the Masquerade  Fighting the Second Inquisition  Carrying out Blood Hunts  Acting as the blindly loyal servants of the Justicars
Possible Truths:  Every Justicar has several Archons beneath them.  Archons serve for unlife.  Archons are forced to serve for a set number of
years, but are owed a life boon when they finish.  Archons feed only on vitae.  There are Caitiff among the Archons.

THE PRETENDER There are many reasons a lick might pretend to be a Camarilla official. Doing so can mean a shield against critique or harm. It can grant them access to resources or services otherwise unavailable to an untitled domain visitor. Conversely, Camarilla officials are favored targets of the sect's enemies. Claiming to be an authorized autark can easily be the last thing you do before you become someone's meal.

89

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Imperator
Imperators have walked among the undead before, so the elders say, and they do so again now. It is a wartime title, and it belongs to the lick that rises up among others to lead the Kindred into battle. As certain members of the sect begin to arm themselves and their cities and make plans to defend against the horrors of what is coming, the Kindred are talking of Imperators again.
Rumored Activities:  Leading Camarilla forces in the Gehenna War  Building fortresses to protect the Kindred in
future battles  Choosing and training Kindred agents to protect
the sect

 Hunting the lupine threat  Hunting mortal hunters  Committing diablerie on elders to thicken their
Blood for war
Possible Truths:  The Inner Circle has personally chosen and
named their Imperators as their answer to the problems facing the Camarilla in this age.  Imperators are Kindred who have been Beckoned by ancients aligned with Camarilla goals and have answered the call.  Those named Imperators are simply those willing to lead in a fight.

DISSEMINATION OF INFORMATION IN THE CAMARILLA Down the Generations: From grandsire to sire, and sire to childer, history and legend pass through clans and bloodlines. Where childer have been carefully watched and selected for the Embrace, there is trust in one's maker and lineage. Where the bonds of family falter, this information is seen as less reliable.
Loremasters: Never solely the province of the Nosferatu or Tremere, Kindred scholars have collected information from oral histories and written records. Supposed loremasters have always been sought out both by truthseekers and Masquerade purists seeking to prevent Kindred knowledge from being found by mortals.
Peer to Peer: The young trade their knowledge before they learn the value of secrets. The old sometimes exchange information for the right price. Though many take stories of Kindred history and governance at face value, the cynical or cautious suspect it's all a game of telephone.
Publications: Hand-written scrolls and codices, woodblock printing, and eventually movable type have concerned elders among the Kindred since their inception, yet stamping out the written word entirely has never been accomplished. A few texts have famously been printed and disseminated through the ages, including various fragments of the supposed Book of Nod (and Aristotle de Laurent's famous version, which Ayisha Jocastation widely published), the Pastor Mortalis, the Euagetaematikon of the Church of Caine, and others. Most believe that Justicars and their Archons suppress most of these texts and destroy all they can find. Occult grimoires containing descriptions of Blood Sorcery or Oblivion are sometimes targeted for destruction, depending on how much information they contain and if an Archon would rather quietly steal the text than destroy it.

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

THE CAMARILLA AT WAR

T here are whispers Gehenna will be the war to end our kind forever. Others imagine it will cast the world into eternal night, and so they welcome it. The truth is not so exciting. Wars never end anything but lives. The fight can be everything, but the world keeps turning around us, and no amount of brutal sacrifice and suffering will stop its movement. There's no relief in sight. Fortunately, some of us thrive in the center of that dance of death.
 The Hand of Vengeance, Fatima al-Faqadi

The Tower was created to protect the Kindred, and it has done so for centuries. In this age, with the growing Second Inquisition, the threat of the Gehenna War, and the resurrected Anarch menace, the Tower will stand the true test. While some domains continue more or less as always, others are beginning to take certain precautions.
New and Old
Adversaries
The Camarilla's enemies are numerous. The hope that the Sabbat would consume itself
has been disappointed. Instead, there is a fear that the Gehenna War is quickly approaching territories it was never expected to reach, and that the Sabbat are at the front of the forces.
Anarch leaders are probably already collaborating with mortal intelligence agencies to wipe out cities like Boston, Chicago, and Paris.
Elders are being Beckoned, not just to leave but to act where they are--sometimes against their former allies.

Even within the Tower, minor cults are growing, exchanging the Traditions for their own twisted sacraments.
Numina, the forces from behind the veil, which the Kindred being undead can only halfway grasp at, are gathering. Ghostly presences, lupine congregations, mortal mystics, and ancient gods not driven by Blood may be rising even now.
States of Emergency
Camarilla elders haven't watched post-democratic mortal governments suspend normal constitutional rights and freedoms to tighten population control without getting ideas.
These are some signs Camarilla Kindred are preparing for war:
Closed Domains: Increasingly, some domains will halt travel in and out, breaking the Tradition of Hospitality to turn away Kindred visitors when they appear.
Surveillance Cities: Fear of enemies within the sect leads some Courts to categorically monitor every Kindred vassal, setting up informant networks and spreading rampant paranoia.

91

WHISPERS FROM THE INNER CIRCLE

In 2024, the Inner Circle met to discuss the appointment of Justicars for the next 13 years... or so many Camarilla Kindred believe. As lines of travel and communication between domains have broken, it's hard to get verifiable information on the greater sect, and the Kindred are left with more questions than answers. Who sits on this global council? What changes to the Inner Circle have the Beckoning and the Vermilion Wedding wrought? Does this mysterious Inner Circle even still exist?
The thing is, whispered rumors can be as powerful as any authentic proclamations an Inner Circle might make. Decisions and proclamations slowly filter through the sect in the modern nights, and sometimes what started as one harpy's charmed invention at a dance party becomes a convincing reality a few domains over.
Camarilla neonates know there are supposed to be seven Justicars, but some say there are now eight. One rumor has it that several influential members of Clan Lasombra joined the sect at the Convention of Chicago, but the clan still does not appear to be recognized as a pillar member. Another claims the office of the Imperator, thought to have disappeared with Karsh the Turk, is back. What might it all mean? Most courtiers can only guess.
Declared Justicars
According to rumor and hearsay, these are the Kindred who currently claim to carry the title of Justicar:

Kasim Bayar, Clan Banu Haqim
He's young, new, and out to prove himself. Kasim wears the mask of a corporate stooge, rolling into town with a business-goth aesthetic and taking over resources and connections left and right. His arrival upsets the local web of influence. He gives excellent, but unsolicited, advice, rarely letting others get a word in. He's one of those douchebags who wears an eyepatch... probably just for the vibes.
 Rex, neonate Brujah
Juliet Parr, Clan Malkavian
Parr has maintained her seat, and I hope you never catch her attention. She resurrects old feuds and uncovers hidden connections everywhere she goes. Whenever she arrives, she seems to dig up one elder in hiding who was supposedly Beckoned, as though she can summon them like a Toreador ancilla calling a wayward ghoul.
 Hafsa, neonate Tremere, to a friend

Francisco Dos Santos Almeida, Clan Nosferatu

I hear that sometimes DiPadua manipulates his young superior, and other times Dos Santos Almeida moves so fast in his erratic theater of terror that the elder has trouble keeping up.
 Overheard in Elysium somewhere in Argentina
Diana Iadanza, Clan Toreador
She was a longtime Archon of a previous Justicar, so Iadanza's second appointment looks like a move to entrench established power and create continuity. You know, she managed to hold onto her title despite a strong showing from Vidal Jarbeaux. I hear she's got a penchant for executing the young and ill-protected with quickly trumped-up evidence in theatrical presentations. I mean... A show trial is still a show!
 The ancilla Marlene, former Herald of Atlanta
Nathan Mendelssohn, Clan Tremere
The former sorcerer-Scourge has been put forward as Justicar by the remaining loyalists of House Tremere. He's a blatant opportunist, using the office to shore up the badly damaged power base of his house. Many would like to oppose him, but few dare.
 Whispered between neonate thaumaturges

Alexandra Lau, Clan Tremere
Lau Mei Yu is from Hong Kong and adopted a British name, like so many Cantonese people do. She only uses her traditional name with close allies, but it's not the most well-guarded of secrets. She led a conservative, reclusive existence until the fracturing of the Pyramid. Then she rose to prominence and power as one of the forces behind House Goratrix, quickly declaring herself as our Justicar. This is supported by an apparently legitimate missive from the Inner Circle. When Lau comes to town, the process of judgment tends to resemble a medieval witch hunt... If the accused burns in the sun, they were innocent. If they're immune, they're guilty.
 Bastien, Tremere neonate of House Carna
Lucinde, Clan Ventrue
Lucinde is the perfect unreachable, inscrutable judge whom even Princes and elders fear. By clan and lineage, she represents conservative power, but she has been known to judge elders, ancillae, and neonates alike, preferring capricious, symbolic judgments of an ironic nature. She isn't going anywhere.
 The Peacock of Warsaw
The Newcomer, Clan Unknown

One of them is said to be a neonate, but you know the old fucks consider anyone under 100 that, so maybe that doesn't mean anything... The newcomer has been turning up in domain after domain with an entourage of Archons, and every time, they torch the place... Ghouls who've been around for more than a decade. Kindred who leave a paper trail. It's bizarre.
 Toreador Primogen Albin Primrose to a colleague

Chapter Two: The Ivory Tower

Tightened Traditions: While a declaration of crisis can temporarily absolve breaks with tradition, it can also be used to tighten existing laws and introduce new ones. A city under attack by mortal hunters might, for instance, completely ban Camarilla lingo, including any mention of "Kindred" or "blood," for fear of electronic devices picking up such words.
Increased Ordeals, Punishments, and Blood Hunts: Courts punish suspected transgressions much more harshly. There are fewer exiles and more Blood Hunts, and vassals are strongly encouraged to participate. Increased suspicion also means more Ordeals--trials wherein accused Kindred undergo some manner of test to prove their innocence.
Examples of Ordeals:  Shared Memoriam to prove alibis and intentions
(see Guided Memory, Players Guide, p. 101 or In Memoriam, p. 145)  Interrogation of Kindred with True Faith using religious symbols  Interrogation involving torture of a mortal Touchstone  Destruction of Anarch, Sabbat, or independent licks to prove allegiance  Psychological evaluation  The fulfillment of dangerous tasks and challenges to prove loyalty
Changed Relations with the Kine: Courts that expect they'll soon have to defend themselves change how they interact with mortal society. Instead of investing in relations with the financial sector or local politics, they might become interested in military institutions, innovative tech, and the weapons trade.
Resource Hoarding: War isn't just battle and death. It's also espionage, financial trickery, blackmail, the race for real estate, and the manipulation of public opinion. While some Courts try to secure their future by gathering powerful allies to barricade themselves behind, others run misinformation campaigns to prep the kine against panic or buy up commercial explosives companies.

Enemies Become Allies: Suddenly, there is an Anarch arms dealer grinning ear to ear in Elysium, and no one is allowed to do anything about it because the Primogen Council has convinced the Prince that this alliance is the city's best defense.
Red Lists
According to rumor, the Inner Circle has a list of official enemies of the sect, Kindred who have become Anathema through betrayal or who are active in the movements that fight to take the Tower down. Individuals like Tyler and Theo Bell. In practice, the red list looks a little different depending on where in the World of Darkness you are and whom you ask. But all Kindred agree that those on the list are free game; they have lost the Camarilla's protection.
For Kindred on the ground, the reality is that local Princes and other rulers have red lists of their own, applicable to their domain and perhaps a few neighboring ones if they're particularly influential. The Red List, which encompasses the whole Camarilla, has the status of a myth, talked about but rarely confirmed.

94

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Uses of Red Lists
These are some different ways Kindred might use a red list to benefit the sect or for their own personal gain:
Barricading the Tower: News of red-listed Kindred tends to travel quickly between domains and thus helps stop the sect's enemies from traveling freely inside its territories.
United Against the Other: A city of Kindred who think they're hunting for the same enemy is a city that doesn't fight itself.
Political Victory: "Naturally, an Anathema cannot be a titled courtier."
Domain Annexation: "Anathema shouldn't hold domain."

Access: Claiming to hunt a red-listed lick might gain you access to otherwise closed Camarilla territories.
Revenge: Making others believe that someone is on the red list can be a good way to neutralize them.
Sanctioned Diablerie: It's always open season on red-listed Anathema, providing a good excuse for someone looking to strengthen their Blood with another's essence.
Anarch Status: A Kindred looking to infiltrate an Anarch domain might have a much easier time doing so if the Anarchs think they're an enemy of the Tower. 

You think you know how they're going to fuck everyone over, but when it happens, they still manage to surprise you. For me, the surprise was when the Second Inquisition started, the Camarilla leaked the havens of many Anarchs. I didn't realize they could be so fucking stupid.
I'm sure that it made sense when those fuckers were gathered around that big mahogany table in their meeting room, pontificating about using their enemies against each other.
That one maneuver helped the Second Inquisition hunters learn more about vampires than whatever they've compiled over the centuries in Vatican archives.
I worked for the Camarilla as an Archon for a long, long time, keeping up the Masquerade, rooting out Sabbat scum. You had to play the game. So I had two childer in the Anarchs, feeding me information. They were both fuckups when I found them, but they shaped up well enough when the Blood gave them a second chance.
I thought Tamika might even be Archon material herself. Troy took down a werewolf once, so he wasn't useless either.
Both of them were burnt by the sun when those Second Inquisition bastards dragged them out.
That's what I've learned about these Machiavellian fucks in the Camarilla. Sometimes the cleverest of them do the dumbest things. Maybe there was a time the Camarilla really did represent the best chance at stability for our kind. Or maybe I was blind from the start.
 Theo Bell, Brujah and former Archon

95

Chapter Three:
THE MOVEMENT
You know that thing they say, that war is the continuation of politics through other means? It makes it sound so clean, but I guess it's true. We got fed up with the Camarilla politics in this city, so I'm going to drink you dry. That's the other means.
 The Brujah Agata Starek, addressing a soon-to-be destroyed Ventrue
The Third Anarch Revolt has been a success. The ongoing revolution has made major gains against the Camarilla, establishing the Anarchs as one of the great vampire sects on par with the Ivory Tower itself, the Ashirra, and the Sabbat. While the Anarchs held domains before the Third Revolt, it reignited the Movement and spurred expansion into numerous new territories.
There's always a price to be paid for getting everything you want. Anarchs in possession of new domains have had to figure out how to organize and prepare for counterattacks, mortal hunters, and other oncoming threats. While older Anarch domains have long-established practices, many newly minted Anarchs insist on doing things their own way.
This means that while the core tenets of what it means to be an Anarch are widely accepted, the actual political arrangements of individual domains differ dramatically. The current setup of a typical Anarch domain is not a tradition that goes back centuries but is something a successful revolutionary came up with a year ago.
97

Chapter Three: The Movement

DOMAIN TYPES

Anarch domains are in flux, with several common types prevalent around the world. The basic Anarch unit of organization is the gang, typically with 3­10 members. The equivalent of the Camarilla coterie, many gangs have clearly recognizable leaders, but the Anarchs wouldn't be Anarchs if there weren't a lot of different ways to organize out there.
The Movement
Many among the Anarchs still remember the dream of Carthage. According to legend, Kindred and kine coexisted openly and peacefully in a utopian society where mortals gave their blood and vampires protected them and taught them wisdom. This perfect society was then destroyed by Roman Ventrue and Malkavians who were jealous of Carthaginian success, leading to two millennia of anti-Carthaginian propaganda.
Some of the most utopian Anarch dreamers espouse a return to these ideals. They argue that the Masquerade must be abolished and that there's no need for the Kindred to take by force what so many mortals would freely give.
Leaders: Utopian domains are often led by a single charismatic individual. The dream is particularly vibrant among the Brujah, but Malkavian or even Ministry visionaries are not unheard of.
Success: The odds are stacked against this dream, so any success is modest at best. On a local scale, it might mean a small insular town where every resident has been inducted into the Carthaginian blood cult.

Carthaginian domains are exceptionally unstable, but before they fall, they can provide wonderfully willing herds for an opportunistic Anarch gang.
Failure: The Second Inquisition. The Camarilla and its Justicars. Even fellow Anarchs. Both Kindred and hunters unite in seeking to demolish any attempt to establish a domain according to Carthaginian tenets. Failure is just a question of which enemy shows up first.
For an unscrupulous Anarch gang, spreading rumors that a rival harbors Carthaginian sympathies is a good way to cause trouble. Such domains have a way of attracting Kindred scholars interested in ancient history and sometimes feature visionaries and occultists able to teach unique abilities before they must escape the community's inevitable demise.
Fake Anarch Domain
For most vampires, their very existence is predicated on pretending to be other than what they are. As such, it's no surprise that some Anarch domains are just Camarilla in disguise. Maybe the domain is surrounded by Anarchs, and camouflage is deemed necessary, or an Anarch boss is planning to become a Camarilla Prince step-by-step. Maybe the domain's Court defused an Anarch revolt by pretending that it was successful.
Whatever the case, the domain is Anarch in name only.
