Leaders: The domain is ruled by a Baron with an advisory council composed of the oldest Anarchs in the domain. When asked, the Baron denies any resemblance to Camarilla leadership structures.

98

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Success: The domain functions as if it were a Camarilla domain, and maybe one night it'll make the switch. When it happens, nothing changes.
It can be difficult for an Anarch gang to embrace Camarilla philosophy. The Ivory Tower has become paranoid, and fear of Anarch demagogues agitating among the neonates is founded in bitter experience. It might be easier to make the switch if the entire domain does so at once.
Failure: The Anarchs are not stupid. Many are exceptionally politically minded and will surely realize that this is not a genuine revolutionary domain. When that realization comes, the hapless Baron might be faced with an uprising of their own.
A revolution is a chance for an Anarch gang to make a name for themselves, regardless of whether it's against a Prince or a Baron.
Gang War
Not every Anarch is a high-minded ideologue. Many, perhaps most, are just ordinary licks trying to make it night to night, keen to look out for their territory for fear of poachers. A domain rife with gang warfare has no use for principles or ideals. What matters is destroying the enemy before they destroy you.
"The enemy" being other Anarchs. A gang war can be devastating, often leaving over half of the domain's vampire population in ashes and inviting in hunters who take note of the inevitable Masquerade breaches. Leaders: Each gang has its own leaders, intent on taking out the others. Success: A domain suffering from a gang war can stabilize in many different ways. One gang can achieve a total victory, shaping the domain to their liking. The gangs may tire of the war, negotiate peace, and become a different type of domain. Or the Second Inquisition may swoop in to destroy everybody. If an Anarch gang is willing to engage in a bit of brutality, a war is when fortunes change very quickly. You might go from having no territory

to controlling the whole city in one night. Or you might be destroyed by someone else going for the same thing.
Failure: If Kindred conflict escalates into total war, even the ostensible winner is so weakened that someone else is bound to swoop in and take over. Camarilla Princes certainly have the ability to reclaim their lost domains by stepping in after the Anarchs have destroyed each other.
There's no reason the aftermath of a gang war can't be exploited by the players' gang or coterie. They just have to time it correctly--after the fighting has died down, before anyone else shows up.
Old Anarch Domain
The old-timers in this domain remember the Second Anarch Revolt, and for a particularly venerable European domain, maybe even the first. For a long time, the domain was a rare bastion of freedom in the midst of conservative Camarilla rule. While it kept the fire of the revolution lit, the turmoil of the Third Anarch Revolt was a shock.
Suddenly, real change is possible and all these young licks refuse to accept the pessimistic attitudes of their seniors. More than one Anarch ancilla has been left in the dust as fledglings and neonates bask in the glory of the revolution.
Leaders: There's some variety in the leadership structures of old Anarch domains, but they tend to be stable and steeped in accumulated tradition. No matter if the leader is a Baron or the domain is ruled by a council of gang bosses, the system has grown comfortable, even ossified.
Success: An old Anarch domain is a repository of experience, knowledge, and even ideological principles. Older Anarchs who experienced the Second Anarch Revolt bring those experiences to bear to ensure new successes in the modern nights.
A gang from an established domain can leverage its experience and resources to become successful leaders in the Third Anarch Revolt, much like the Brujah ideologue Salvador Garcia has done.

99

Chapter Three: The Movement

Failure: Eventually, the revolutionary wave washes over the old Anarch domain, demolishing its elites, just as with any Camarilla domain.
For players' characters involved in the Third Anarch Revolt, such a situation is an opportunity to make sure that no dusty ancilla is going to claim their glory after the war has already been won. The future belongs to the young.
Ongoing Revolution
The domain has only recently gone over to the Anarchs, and revolutionary fervor is still in the air. Perhaps there are Camarilla holdouts to uncover or neighboring domains that also need to be freed from the rule of the elders. Whatever the specifics of the situation, the fire and fury of the revolutionary spirit haven't had time to die down.

Leaders: An ongoing revolution is often such messy business that its organizational structure is quite loose, consisting of a leadership council supported by various gang leaders. Common identifiable individual roles are those of an ideologue, who explains what the revolution is for; a war leader, who directs violent action; and emissaries and demagogues, who seek to win support for the cause in new domains.
Success: The revolution cannot be stopped. It spreads onwards until neighboring Camarilla domains fall as well in a shockwave of violence. Eventually, the energies of the revolution are spent, leaving behind a number of thriving Anarch domains.
For an Anarch gang, this presents an opportunity to grab new domains and make themselves the leaders of a pristine territory.

100

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Failure: The revolution turns on itself. Now that there are no obvious Camarilla enemies to strike, Anarchs find fault in their comrades, turning the domain into a vicious free-for-all where anyone can be denounced as a traitor at any time.
The stakes are high, but for a clever, ruthless, and committed Anarch gang, the general melee is an environment rich in opportunity. You can rise to the top or get destroyed, but at least you'll get to take your shot.
Open City
The domain has open doors to any Kindred who might wish to drop by for a peaceful visit. The local Anarch gangs are not particularly territorial, and core feeding territories, such as the nightlife district, are open to visitors. This is what the freedom promised by the Anarchs is all about! No Prince or Baron is going to tell you where you can or can't go.
Permanent residence might be a different question, though. In most open cities, visiting is fine, but staying requires bypassing all the usual barriers.
Leaders: To be able to function successfully, an open city usually requires a Baron or gang bosses who take an easygoing attitude to the affairs of the city but act with ruthless efficiency when basic security makes it necessary. If a visitor is breaching the Masquerade or acts like a Sabbat scout, there's no time for playing nice.
Success: The whole existence of an open city is almost always predicated on the fact that the free flow of Kindred brings some sort of benefit to the locals. This might be a tax of some sort, or a status as neutral ground for high-level meetings and the security this brings. Perhaps the locals calculate that the constant presence of visitors acts as a useful buffer against Sabbat incursions.
An open city presents plenty of opportunities for Anarch gangs who are used to more limited and guarded interactions between the undead. Enemies can parley, and deals can be made.

Failure: Sometimes, an open city is just a domain with leadership so weak that anybody can waltz in. Local Anarch bosses might pretend that it's because they believe in freedom, but in reality, it's because they can't defend their territory. As a result, hostile entities from all corners converge, eventually leading to the domain's collapse.
For a ruthless gang of Anarch cutthroats, this could be an opportunity. Maybe the players' characters sense weakness in the pirate utopia rhetoric of a nearby domain and decide to take over.
Pre-Revolutionary Domain
A Camarilla domain on the brink of revolution is crawling with Anarchs. Brujah demagogues from long-held Anarch domains sneak around agitating the local neonates to overthrow their elders, while martially minded revolutionaries make maps marking the havens of their enemies.
Sometimes, a pre-revolutionary domain is Camarilla in name only, having already gone over to the Anarchs on the level of nightly business. The Prince holds Court, but nobody cares; their power is limited to what they can see, touch, and hurt.
Leaders: The leader of the domain is theoretically a Camarilla Prince, but in reality, various Anarch gang bosses and demagogues hold sway as the local Kindred prepare to oust Camarilla loyalists for good.
Success: The revolution happens. Camarillaminded Kindred are kicked out, the Prince is in ashes, and the local Kindred get to work setting up a well-functioning Anarch domain.
For young, newly minted Anarchs, this is a golden opportunity to claim territory of their own, get revenge for humiliations suffered under the Camarilla era, and make sure they have a seat at the table with any future leadership arrangements.
Failure: It's embarrassing when a successful revolution devolves into bickering and infighting. It's even worse when it happens before the enemy

101

Chapter Three: The Movement

has even been vanquished. Sometimes, Anarchs manage to snatch defeat from the jaws of victory by turning against each other instead of making sure the enemy is really, truly destroyed. Patience is a common virtue among Camarilla Princes, and many are capable of just waiting for their enemies to destroy themselves.
A failing revolution is a wonderful opportunity for traitorous Anarchs. They can go to the Camarilla Prince and pretend to have been loyal all along, willing to help with squashing the insurrection in exchange for future considerations.
Social Experiment
Only the youngest may lead. Anyone who's been a vampire for longer than a year is automatically disqualified. Ghouls are just another type of vampire and must be treated equally in the domain. Thin-blood supremacy is demonstrated by the obvious fact that only they can walk in the sun. All vampires must drink each other's Blood in monthly ceremonies to ensure harmony. A new Baron is chosen annually on New Year's and destroyed when they leave office.
An Anarch revolution presents an opportunity to put ideas into practice. Ideologues who have long cherished their own theories of how Kindred society should be organized get their chance to demonstrate how it really works.
Leaders: Experimental domains are commonly led by a single charismatic individual or a committed coterie of true believers.
Success: It really works! Somehow, the grand ideas of the experiment have produced a stable, successful domain. Sometimes, this is because the ideas are genuinely good, but it's also possible that success is the result of the personal power and charisma of the ringleaders who mistake their own influence for ideological success. The difference becomes clear when someone less capable tries to copy the concept.
Maybe you are the visionary at the core of the experiment. When revolution happens, take your

shot and build the paradise you've always seen in your mind.
Failure: Not surprisingly, the common outcome for Kindred social experiments is catastrophe. Vampires are selfish, greedy, treacherous backstabbers, and trying to redeem them into new social configurations is an uphill battle. Utopia devolves into accusations of brainwashing, crumbling Humanity, and wights running wild in the streets.
In such carnage, opportunity awaits. The ruins of a social experiment are often fertile territory for someone to come in and build a more conservative Anarch domain under their own rule.
Wasteland
The domain is in ruins. Most of the local Kindred are in ashes, and the survivors cower in their havens, scared to come out. The cohesion of the local Kindred society has broken down, and nobody has a clear idea of who's where.
In its own way, the wasteland is a peaceful type of Anarch domain because there are so few vampires left to fight.
Leaders: None. Success: Nowhere to go but up. The slate has been wiped clean, presenting an opportunity to create something new. There's not a lot of empty territory for a young Anarch gang to move into, so this is a chance for those who dare to set things up according to their own preferences. Failure: The reason the domain is a wasteland is still there--the Second Inquisition and the Sabbat being the most common. In both cases, new Kindred who try to move in get destroyed just as the original inhabitants were. For players' characters fighting against the Second Inquisition or the Sabbat, the wasteland offers a chance to draw their enemies out into the open. After achieving a seemingly total victory, the attacker grows complacent, presenting an opportunity for those bold enough to exploit it.

102

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

CLANS IN THE MOVEMENT

Three clans dominate the Anarch Movement.

Brujah
"We do not fear change; we ride it, steer it, control it."
Clan Brujah has always been the heart of the Anarch Movement, driving its uprisings and revolts. For the most part, that doesn't make them act as though they're better than other Anarchs. However, in some cities, younger gangs of Brujah have banded together in what's becoming known as the Brujah United movement, refusing membership to any not of their own clan. These rebels are disappointed in the Anarchs at large, angry that they're too moderate, and itching to set the night on fire. Other, more experienced members of the clan do what they can to keep calm and think ahead, trying to navigate a political environment that's almost impossible to control and even harder to predict.
To be an Anarch Brujah tonight is to move between opposing currents in the revolution. Everywhere you turn, someone has an action plan or an ideology to sell you--and if you don't provide the right response, they may start to see you as another enemy. To stay true to your own ideals, you've got to be both slippery and decisive.

Character Concepts
Brujah United Member: Convinced of their own strength and the superiority of their own clan, this vampire is quick to action and just as quick to jump to conclusions in support of their own beliefs.
War Chronicler: They document the revolution to learn from the moves of its enemies, report back to their domain leaders, or spread its hopeful message.
Recruiter for the Cause: This Brujah knows that the Movement can only continue by growing, and they've taken the dangerous path of infiltrating enemy territory to recruit. They probably have high Leadership and Charisma, or at least a very good story of Camarilla oppression.

103

Chapter Three: The Movement

Gangrel
"No rules, no masters."
True Anarchs know that most of the Gangrel have been on their side since the beginning, yet too many remain loyal to the Tower. This means that while the clan is generally well respected in the Movement, it doesn't take much to be viewed with some suspicion, especially if you only recently escaped the Camarilla's claws. Known to be uncompromising and deadly no matter their focus, members of Clan Gangrel can

do well both as warriors and negotiators among the Anarchs, gaining influence on the basis that others often fear the risk of crossing them. On the other hand, young Gangrel can quickly find themselves in deeper water than they can handle, as older Anarchs overestimate their chances of making it through dangerous missions.
The clan's past servitude as "Camarilla watchdogs" means that a young Gangrel sometimes has to work hard to prove that they're not about to be taken advantage of and that they're not eager to return to the welcoming embrace of their former masters.

104

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Character Concepts
Survivor: Betrayed by their former coterie or faction, this Gangrel learned that loyalty is just another tool. They use others while they can, telling themselves that their freedom and the cause they fight for justify any betrayal.
Protector: This Gangrel has been put in the unfortunate position of following a well-meaning but lacking leader. Out of loyalty, they do their best to follow orders, but sometimes they have to do one thing and say another to protect their gang and the very leader they quietly disobey.
Warden: Once a Sheriff's Hound in a Camarilla city, this Gangrel is now an Anarch enforcer who knows exactly how the other side operates. They offer their expertise to the Movement, but everyone wonders if they're still running errands for some Court.
Nomadic Fighter: Not connected to any particular city or domain, they travel to the fight, wherever it is.
The Ministry
"Ah, revolution, what a splendid religion."
In recent years, a significant number of Ministers have joined the Anarch cause. This is partly due to falling out with power players among Camarilla luminaries, and partly to what they claim is genuine belief in the cause. Many Anarchs mistrust these new joiners, but they can't deny their usefulness. You gingerly welcome a Minister into your city, blink, and suddenly they've made themselves indispensable by installing security systems in all your safe houses, setting up a blood-smuggling system that totally circumvents mortal institutions, and introducing their mortal mask to the mortal union dock workers, so they now control the waterways.
They might not be eager to fight physically, but Ministers are natural leaders and planners. In some cases, their moral flexibility even makes them coolheaded enough to do more for the Movement than their more passionate allies.

Character Concepts:
Honest Liar: This Minister is struggling to win the trust of their local Anarch community because they've been caught lying about their priorities before. This time, they're genuine, but how will they convince their allies of this? Perhaps they can pin the blame for their past misdeeds on someone else.
Social Magnet: Hardly the most fearsome bloodsucker in the city, they have this energy about them that makes others want to be where they are, hear what they have to say, and discover what they know.
Community Pillar: They're at the center of a mortal congregation, cult, or cultural community, guiding the lonely and helping the poor. They use this influence to support the Anarch Movement with information and with blood.
Everyone Else
In general, everyone is invited to join the Anarch revolution. There's space for both the Unchained Malkavians, Red Nosferatu, Abstract Toreador, Ipsissimus Tremere, Emancipated Hecata, Caitiff orphans, Duskborn delinquents, and even, with a little convincing, the so-called Free Ventrue, the theoretical Enlightened Tzimisce, and childer of the Banu Haqim or the Lasombra, who are otherwise rumored to vie for Camarilla membership.
But being welcomed to join the fight isn't quite the same as being trusted. The Movement may be brutally disorganized at times, amid massive changes, and it grows too quickly to hold onto its territories without constantly expanding. This doesn't mean it's blind to danger from within. You'll have to prove yourself no matter what your background is, but especially if your bloodline stinks of Camarilla culture.
As for the rarer clans, the Ravnos, the mythical Salubri... Well, as with everything, it depends on the locals. Perhaps they'll be welcomed on equal footing like everyone else, or perhaps they'll be used as tinder in a bonfire planned for Camarilla courtiers.

105

Chapter Three: The Movement

FACES OF THE REVOLUTION

If you're interested in Anarch politics, there are a few movers and shakers who have come to personify the Anarch struggle.

Saima Aro
Like many Anarchs, Aro was once just one poor mortal among countless others, her appearance or ambition in no way indicative of a future existence as one of the undead. She came to the U.S. as an immigrant in 1868, fleeing the Great Famine in Finland, and worked as a serving girl in households in Milwaukee and Michigan. A staunch communist, she was often fired from her posts because of her unyielding agitation.

Aro originally became a vampire in a Sabbat mass Embrace in 1877, ending up in a pack of confused and ravenous undead with only a vague idea of the various Sabbat philosophies they used to avoid becoming wights. While many of her compatriots were destroyed or succumbed to the Beast, she managed to regain a semblance of her former humanity and dig herself out of a haze of blood and Hunger. She was never quite a wight, but for a time, she'd existed as a forest-dwelling terror, attacking small settlements at night with her pack for sustenance.
Aro found the Anarch Free States in her search of a community and something to believe in. Since then, she has become a grim sentinel of Anarch orthodoxy, subscribing to a belief that for a domain to be successful, it must be cleansed of Camarilla loyalists and other such scum. Never an intellectual, she takes her cues from Anarch leaders who, in her eyes, have proven their commitment to the cause. She focuses on the practical reality of finding traitors and destroying them.
Aro's reputation among Anarchs is dark, as she believes in quick field trials followed by a summary execution. Sect leaders support her, however, since she does their dirty work while allowing them to remain clean. She's also something of a hero to

106

many young Ravnos in the sect as the rare, comparatively older member of their clan who's capable of protecting the bloodline.
Her signature move is to weigh down the body of a vampire and sink it in a muddy lake to lie there in torpor for eternity.
Clan: Ravnos Generation: 11th Embrace: 1877 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 5 General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 5, Mental 6, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 10, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 10, Firearms 10, Investigation 8 Disciplines: Animalism 3 (Bond Famulus, Feral Whispers, Animal Succulence), Fortitude 2 (Resilience, Toughness), Obfuscate 4 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage, Ghost in the Machine, Vanish), Presence 2 (Daunt, Lingering Kiss), Protean 3 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons, Earth Meld) In Stories: Among the Anarchs, Aro is something akin to the Archons of the Camarilla, someone who comes to the domain on the trail of a traitor, seeking to destroy them before they can betray the sect. As an enemy, she's dangerous and relentless. If she believes the players' characters to be traitorous, she'll start picking off their contacts one by one. As an ally, Aro's arrival can feel like a stroke of luck. She destroys the enemies of the players' characters, often in grimly inventive executions. The problems start if the characters have done something that could be understood as a betrayal of the Anarch Movement.
107

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Chapter Three: The Movement
Theo Bell
Theo Bell was known as a Camarilla Archon for much of his unlife until he famously betrayed the Camarilla and destroyed the Ventrue elder Hardestadt by shooting him in the face. The rumors of this act were crucial in getting the Third Anarch Revolt off the ground.
As a mortal, Bell was the child of an enslaved family in Mississippi. He was Embraced by a Camarilla luminary, the Brujah Justicar Udolfo Cerro de Léon, who brought him to Europe and took him on a tour of various Courts. Eventually, Bell became an Archon and a loyal servant of the Camarilla, always believing he was doing the most good by enforcing the Traditions.
In the later years of his service, Bell learned to detest many of the elders and ancillae who held power in the Camarilla. He saw the consequences of their actions up close
108

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
and understood the terrible toll they took on mortal society. Still, he believed that as an institution, the Camarilla was the better alternative. He fought against the Sabbat and against Kindred who broke the Masquerade.
One of the reasons Bell's attack on Hardestadt at the Convention of Prague was so shocking was that he was known for his loyalty. The action was motivated by a deep sense of betrayal from learning that Camarilla agents had leaked information about Anarch havens to the Second Inquisition. Bell felt that by doing so, the elders he served had put everything he'd ever worked for at risk.
Afterwards, Bell joined the Anarchs, where he immediately became a controversial figure. Some saw him as a cop, barely better than a Camarilla Sheriff despite his one revolutionary act, while others welcomed him because his defection was a sign of Camarilla weakness.
Bell himself has been uncomfortable in the Anarchs but has tried to make the best of it because he has no other options. He continues to do what he did as an Archon, protecting the Masquerade and fighting the Sabbat, even if his new allies sometimes worry him.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 9th Embrace: 1857 Blood Potency: 4 Humanity: 6 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 9, Social 7, Mental 8, Disciplines 8 Secondary Attributes: Health 10, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 10, Firearms 10, Investigation 9 Disciplines: Auspex 2 (Heightened Senses, Premonition), Celerity 4 (Rapid Reflexes, Fleetness, Blink, Unerring Aim), Dominate 3 (Cloud Memory, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 2 (Resilience, Toughness), Obfuscate 2 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage), Potence 4 (Lethal Body, Prowess, Brutal Feed, Draught of Might), Presence 4 (Daunt, Lingering Kiss, Dread Gaze, Summon) In Stories: When Theo Bell is there, something bad is about to happen. The Sabbat is about to attack, or there's war with the Camarilla. The worst, however, is internal dissension in the Anarchs. Bell's reflexes are still those of an Archon, and when he sees a Masquerade breach, he'll execute the offender, something that causes controversy among the hierarchy-averse Anarchs. As an enemy, Bell is relentless. In physical combat, he's fearless, but he also has a faction of Anarchs behind him, and his opinion can sour them on the players' characters quickly. The Masquerade has its proponents even among the unbound.
109

Chapter Three: The Movement
Salvador Garcia
A famous intellectual of the modern Anarch Movement, Salvador Garcia made his reputation in the Second Anarch Revolt. He was born in Spain to a family of farmers and participated in revolutionary politics while he was alive. His Embrace made it possible for him to fight for freedom without the limitations of mortality.
This is what he has done for a century and more. After initial losses for the Anarchs in Spain, he escaped to the U.S., plotting the Second Anarch Revolt in Los Angeles with other luminaries of the Movement, such as Jeremy MacNeil. It started in 1944 with the destruction of the Prince of Los Angeles and spread from there as other domains found inspiration in the act.
The revolution resulted in the creation of the Anarch Free States in Los Angeles, still one of the most successful Anarch domains anywhere in the world. For many years, Garcia was occupied with trying to put his ideals into practice in the Free
110

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
States, creating the ideal society he had envisioned and getting lost in the minutiae of local politics.
With the Third Anarch Revolt, Garcia was revitalized, deciding once more to concern himself with the Movement as a whole. This may have come as a relief because it freed him from having to deal with the nightly reality of his political dream, something he's never been good at.
Garcia is a handsome and charismatic Spaniard, Embraced in his 20s when he'd already experienced his share of action. He's energized by speaking to crowds and likes trying to make allies out of his enemies. After all, freedom ultimately benefits everyone. An ancilla, he has lost touch with mortal dreams and aspirations, and these nights, his idealism tends to ignore the rights and needs of the living. Whether this is a blind spot or not depends on who you ask.
Many younger Anarchs see Garcia as old-fashioned, even ridiculous, with all his highminded talk of libertas, but he has plenty of followers because when the Third Anarch Revolt started, he joined the fight once again, unlike some of his contemporaries. Taking part in overthrowing Camarilla regimes lent him crucial credibility. In the eyes of many, he's still got it.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 9th Embrace: 1892 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 5 General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 8, Mental 7, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 8, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Leadership 9, Streetwise 9, Politics 9 Disciplines: Celerity 1 (Cat's Grace), Dominate 1 (Cloud Memory), Fortitude 1 (Resilience), Potence 2 (Lethal Body, Prowess), Presence 3 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Entrancement), Protean 2 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons) In Stories: Salvador Garcia shows up when something big is about to happen among the Anarchs. He's a harbinger of revolution--a Camarilla domain overthrowing the Prince or an attack being prepared to topple the neighboring Prince. He's not particularly interested in the small and the local, preferring to think in terms of the entire Movement. For players' characters, Garcia's presence may validate their ambitions or act as an unwelcome foil to them. He's an idealist and tends to favor plans to build utopian domains where all Kindred can be free. If the players' characters seek to emerge as the rulers of a new domain, this might not be what they want.
111

Chapter Three: The Movement
Rudi
A grassroots-level Anarch leader in Copenhagen, Rudi has a knack for collecting strays, helping them get on their feet. In the process, he teaches them his version of the Anarch creed. His unofficial gathering of Anarchs is called Rudi's Army and wields surprising power despite its seemingly disparate nature.
Rudi still holds on to the remains of his mortal existence, and he's young enough that he can meet old friends without arousing suspicion. The milieu around him hasn't changed, and he tells himself that he's still the same person he always was, just trying to help the people around him. He knows that vampires poison the communities they're in but believes that he'll somehow make the exception.
A gay man in life, Rudi still tries to maintain contact with the community that meant so much to him, even as the associated emotions and desires start to get confused with the eternal Hunger of the
112

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
dead. He tries to be a good person even though he knows that his Hunger, and that of his Army, drains the life from the mortals around him.
A popular leader, Rudi is seen as someone who walks the walk, spending his time on the streets and in the havens of his followers. Although he often has the role of a teacher, he tries to avoid being cast into a leadership role because, in his mind, Anarchs must be equal. Still, his natural talent makes him someone others follow despite his ideals.
Copenhagen is nominally a Camarilla domain, but the Prince is weak enough that, effectively, the Anarchs have taken over. Paradoxically, being theoretically a Camarilla member has given Rudi more freedom to build his Army than he otherwise might have because he doesn't have to fight the Ivory Tower. The problem is that when something bad happens and his vampiric nature causes him to hurt someone, there's nobody to blame except himself.
Clan: Gangrel Generation: 10th Embrace: 2009 Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 8 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 8, Mental 6, Disciplines 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 7, Leadership 9, Streetwise 9 Disciplines: Animalism 3 (Sense the Beast, Feral Whispers, Animal Succulence), Fortitude 2 (Unswayable Mind, Toughness), Protean 2 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons) In Stories: Rudi is what passes for a well-meaning, good person among the Kindred, but he's a vampire, which means that eventually, his Beast is going to sabotage his aspirations. He works best in an introspective, street-level story where the focus is on how the base Hunger for blood perverts and twists even those who try to do good. As an enemy, Rudi's strength is in his ability to fade into the woodwork and emerge again with new allies. He seems to know everybody, and he's admired by a lot of people, mortal and undead alike. Because of this, he's hard to take out. The repercussions would be severe, and possibly from unforeseen quarters.
113

Chapter Three: The Movement
Hesha Ruhadze
An elder occultist and Church of Set true believer, Hesha Ruhadze likes to present a benevolent façade for himself as well as the clan and faith he represents. Despite his sincere faith, he has at times found himself at odds with Church of Set orthodoxy because in the modern nights, the hierarchy is dominated by Kindred much younger than he is. While Ruhadze insists on genuine revelation, many in the Church prioritize their own political needs and adjust their faith accordingly.
Ruhadze affects a sophisticated manner, coming across as highly educated and even fashionable in his own way. All of this is a well-created persona, built over his actual roots as a mortal living village life in the 17th-century Funj Sultanate. His first experiences of a local version of the Church of Set were as a mortal, when many of the people he knew came to worship the Kindred leaders of the
114

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
blood cult. Ruhadze was Embraced by these members of the Ministry because of his intelligence and talent.
As Ruhadze learned more about the tenets of the Church of Set, he internalized a simple idea: occult and religious truth exists in the world, can be discovered, and has been known to ancient Kindred in ages past. By seeking to confer with the ancient blood gods and discover writings from those with direct knowledge of the Antediluvians and other entities of vampire myth, it would be possible to discover true wisdom.
While Ruhadze has kept his haven in many different domains across the centuries, his basic quest has remained the same. Now that the Gehenna War rages across the world, he feels that there are unique opportunities to make discoveries that have long eluded him. Ruhadze has always assumed that the true picture formed out of various pieces of occult wisdom is going to support his faith. If this doesn't happen, his intellectual integrity may lead him down strange paths indeed.
Ruhadze is not an ideological Anarch. He finds it easier to conduct his business in domains affiliated with the Movement, but if that changes, he'll switch to another sect without giving the matter much thought.
Clan: The Ministry Generation: 8th Embrace: 1687 Blood Potency: 4 Humanity: 4 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 8, Mental 9, Disciplines 8 Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Subterfuge 9, Academics 10 (Ancient Religion, Philology), Occult 10 Disciplines: Animalism 2 (Sense the Beast, Feral Whispers), Auspex 1 (Sense the Unseen), Fortitude 1 (Unswayable Mind), Obfuscate 3 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage, Mask of a Thousand Faces), Potence 1 (Soaring Leap), Presence 3 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Entrancement), Protean 5 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons, Shapechange, Metamorphosis, The Unfettered Heart), Blood Sorcery 5 (A Taste for Blood, Extinguish Vitae, Scorpion's Touch, Theft of Vitae, Baal's Caress; Rituals: Blood Walk, Clinging of the Insect, Eyes of Babel, Ward Against Spirits, Essence of Air, Incorporeal Passage, Heart of Stone) In Stories: Initially, Ruhadze comes across as a proponent of religious freedom and open inquiry, curious and generous to those who share his esoteric interests. He participates in the Anarch political process to the degree that he can protect his clan and church. It's only with time that the players' characters may figure out that he'll sacrifice anything and anyone for the wisdom he seeks, and that the apocalyptic ascension prophesied by the Church of Set is not going to be a paradise on Earth for most people.
115

Chapter Three: The Movement
Agata Starek
A successful Anarch revolution requires two parts: first, you demolish or subvert the old order, and then you build a new one to replace it. An Anarch legend, the Bahari-inspired Brujah Agata Starek represents the nihilism of focusing only on tearing down what has been built.
Starek believes in fighting for the underdog, but her way of doing it is mercurial and destructive. Often, when the underdog finally wins, Starek turns against them.
Born in Kraków, Poland, Starek has traveled widely, often showing up at various crisis points and violent confrontations. She has a habit of inventing stories about her past, making it difficult to establish concrete details. The goal of these stories has often been to ridicule the idea that there's an explanation in her personal history for why she's like this, as she maintains that she merely embodies the revolutionary virtues of the Anarchs and the Bahari approach to personal growth.
Many Bahari have disowned her, claiming that she doesn't understand or follow their principles.
Starek is often seen as a terrifying, even demonic figure among Camarilla cities and more established Anarchs because of her penchant for gleeful diablerie. She seems to take particular joy from drinking her enemies dry, particularly the
116

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
Ventrue, and has a unique facility for escaping consequences that have doomed many less accomplished in the art.
Despite her destructive nature, Starek has many admirers among the Anarchs. To those who have suffered bitter humiliation under Camarilla rule, Starek is like an avenging angel, coming down to burn their tormentors to ashes. Who cares what happens after that?
Clan: Brujah Generation: 9th Embrace: 1955 Blood Potency: 6 Humanity: 4 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 9, Social 7, Mental 7, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 11, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Athletics 10, Brawl 10, Intimidation 9 Disciplines: Auspex 2 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition), Celerity 5 (Rapid Reflexes, Fleetness, Blink, Unerring Aim, Split Second), Fortitude 3 (Resilience, Unswayable Mind, Toughness), Potence 3 (Lethal Body, Prowess, Brutal Feed), Presence 1 (Daunt), Protean 2 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons) In Stories: In a political story set among the Anarchs, Agata Starek represents the nihilistic impulse for destruction. Whether you decide to use her as a Storyteller character or create a faction that embodies her values, she'll be the opposite of every Anarch who wants to build something. She can also be useful after the Movement or the players' characters have suffered an overwhelming loss. Perhaps a Camarilla Prince has demolished their domain, forcing them into exile from their own havens. At such moments, the nihilistic promise Starek represents may feel like a last chance for justice.
ANARCH DOS AND DON'TS Different Anarch domains have their own cultures, but some things are widespread. DO  Contribute to whatever needs to be done. Do the outskirts of this city need to be patrolled? Offer to do it.
Make yourself useful.  Establish mortal contacts. Every Anarch needs to be in the loop when it comes to the mortal world, and a
good Mask helps with hunters too.  Keep your end of the bargain. You promise something, you deliver it.  Bring the fun. Nobody likes a perpetual killjoy (known to some as a Ventrue). DON'T  Be a poser. Bragging about things you can't back up is a bad idea.  Bring the heat. Just because we don't live under the Camarilla's boot anymore doesn't mean you can go
around with your fangs out.  Relax. Keep on your toes. Things shift fast inside Anarch domains. · Think you can make friends with your fists. Charm and charisma will take you further than unnecessary
violence.
117

Chapter Three: The Movement

ANARCH ORGANIZATION

The philosophy of anarchism is antiinstitutional and anti-hierarchical. It tolerates no Princes and does not dole out titles as rewards. That doesn't mean that the Movement is without leaders. Rather, authority is earned through action rather than generation or Blood.
Gang Rule
Most Anarch cities are shared between gangs of all sizes. The politics of keeping these gangs from open warfare can be intricate, and some cities exist in a perpetual state of tension.
In a city divided between different gangs, the local laws can shift from neighborhood to neighborhood. It's a dangerous environment, but it's full of opportunities for the daring.
A gang can range from a tight-knit coterie of four to five members to large groups of 20­30 members, protecting a bigger domain together. Often, gangs have both Kindred and mortal members--with most of the mortals knowing very little about what is really going on at night. Ghouls are the exception, since they often play an important role as messengers and henchmen between night and day rule, sometimes alongside Duskborn.
Gang Rule Concepts
War Council: The Anarchs here are only focused on one thing: where to strike against the Camarilla next. Their hold on the city isn't permanent; it's

merely a spot to gather intel and strength. Cities visited by War Councils are usually packed with excited rallies and a lot of bloodthirsty Anarchs, but later they become almost empty when the fight has moved on.
Capulets Versus Montagues: The city is dominated by two gangs of equal size and strength that do not get along, whether that shows in small tensions or open animosity. To try to settle in a town like this, one has to choose a side quickly and then probably stick to it.
Power Vacuum: The dominant gang has been dethroned, and nobody knows who is in charge. A complex vortex of both dangers and opportunities, different gang-like factions control different parts of the city every night. Anyone can move in and try to claim domain; holding it will be a different matter.
Anarch Commune: Several gangs hold the city together, making decisions through (mostly) peaceful negotiations. It isn't always easy, but it's what they fought to get, so they endure the endless debates. This is more likely to work where a majority vote has been agreed upon rather than where everybody has to agree unanimously every time.
Brujah City: Gangs of Brujah rule this city together, tolerating the presence of other clans but not allowing them much say in any big decisions. If you're not Brujah, you'd better enter with someone from the clan who can vouch for you (see Brujah United, p. 103).

118

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Barony
In an Anarch city, a Baron is a single ruler with authority over the whole area. The most common way for a Baron to be established is for one of several Anarch gang bosses to rise to preeminence.
Typically, Barons are much more present among their subjects than a Camarilla Prince and must constantly negotiate with various other stakeholders to maintain their position. A few are authoritarian, claiming that the success of the revolution gives them a mandate to rule.
Character Concepts
Respected Ruler: This Baron managing to be perceived as both fair and efficient. They've been in power for a good while or have even been asked to take the job. It's probably best to be on friendly terms with them.
Appointed by the Majority: Sometimes, nobody wants to fucking lead, and you gotta just pick a person. Often, the unlucky leader is given a time limit to prove their worth, and if they suck, they'll be kicked out of the city, maybe even hunted down (to motivate them to do a good job). This could be a great way to gain influence for the brave vampire, who volunteers to help, or a certain path to getting in big trouble.
Agitator: This Baron took the title to use it to go to war against other cities. They might resign when the fight is over or claim the city they've conquered. Sometimes these individuals are great leaders in battle but less so out of it.
Failing Autocrat: This Baron who bit off more power than they can chew; it's only a question of time before they'll be deposed. Their city is a pressure cooker of violence that's just about to erupt.
The Benevolent Mobster: This Baron rules over their domain with the aid of many loyal supporters. They're friendly, even welcoming, but have a strict set of rules for existing on their turf. They could offer you protection, but it comes at a price. Protection from what? Probably their own supporters.

Common Roles
In many Anarch domains, the ones who step up to do the work that needs doing get to decide how it's done. If the community likes how they do it, they'll let them keep at it. If they don't, another soon appears to take over.
While the below are by no means official titles, they appear often enough in common parlance that most established Anarchs know what they refer to. There can be several of each in an Anarch city, as every bigger gang usually has its own.
Gang Boss: Some Anarch gangs are completely egalitarian, and every decision is made by voting. But many have a de facto leader the others look to for guidance when there's trouble brewing.
Lieutenant: A lieutenant in a leader's second-incommand who backs them up in conflicts and takes over for them if they're indisposed. Often, they are someone who has known the leader for a long time and has the gall to talk straight to them, even when no one else dares to. They are the Mercutio to undead Romeo.
Secretary: Somebody has to keep track of what's agreed upon at Anarch meetings. It can be hard enough to settle debates in the first place. Nobody needs to doubt what was said after the fact.
Treasurer: Revolution is expensive, and as money flows through warm hands into cold and back, this vampire makes sure to count it, clean it, and pass it on to where it's most needed for the cause.
Tail Gunner: Among bikers, the tail gunner rides in the back, making sure nobody gets left behind. Among Anarchs, they keep an eye on the youngest or weakest in their gang both on missions and when there's a fight.
Mortals and Ghouls
Despite all the talk of independence and freedom, Anarchs tend to enslave a significant number of ghouls, now dependent on their vitae. It's hard to avoid, since they tend to be more integrated in mortal society, and ghouls become an important

119

Chapter Three: The Movement

shield against exposure. Some treat their ghouls almost as equals, justifying the way they use them by their closeness and shared goals.
Interacting with Mortal Politics
While your typical Camarilla manipulator probably reserves most of their occasional engagement with mortal politics to lobbying and blackmail, Anarchs are often more involved. Few can keep up a mortal mask long enough to be an ongoing central force in government, but when it comes to the birth of grassroots movements, NGOs, and student rebellions, Anarchs are pleased to take part, painting banners, offering legal advice, or playfully suggesting making Molotov cocktails.
Anarch-flavored examples of interfering with mortal politics:  Joining a movement or political party to take part
in decision making  Attempting to instigate civil disobedience and
protests  Using unions and nonpartisan organizations
to delay decisions that would benefit Camarilla interests  Volunteering to collect signatures for a potential new party, which is also a wonderful excuse to knock on doors
Common Groupings
in the Movement
These are some common types you'll find in an Anarch city domain. Use them for inspiration to create stories for your players or to think about where your own Anarch character belongs.
Idealists
The ones fighting at the front lines, driven by idealistic fervor or pure hatred towards the Camarilla.

This group is mostly composed of young, angry Kindred who are eager to test themselves in the fight against the establishment. To be one of them is to be in a state of the rush of roused Blood and bold words and to bask in the glory of finally learning something useful and having real purpose. The hidden hierarchies here are about who has proven themselves in battle. This is a fast-paced culture of dares, where violence and battle scars are celebrated. Cynics might claim that getting people to feel more important than they are is a great way to get them to sacrifice themselves for the cause they were rallied to believe in. Many Anarchs consider time spent with this group a rite of passage to join the Movement.
Character Concepts
The Eager Acolyte: Inexperienced but willing to learn, they want nothing more than to follow a passionate Anarch general into battle--or anywhere else, for that matter.
The Seasoned Fighter: They could still be a fervent believer in the cause, or they understand that no other group will let them indulge their lust for chaos and mayhem.
The Former Camarilla Goon: This vampire is out for payback against the ones who made them a slave in the Camarilla grinder. Revenge is what motivates them, and it can't come soon enough.
The True Believer: The Anarch Revolt must be spread like wildfire everywhere a Kindred population exists. No one is free from Camarilla oppression until all are!
Protectionists
Having tasked themselves with protecting Anarch territory, they defend when others attack.
This group is dedicated to guarding what has already been taken. Its members engage in city politics oriented toward the future, focusing on infrastructure, peace between opposing city factions, and maintaining a functional relationship with the

120

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

mortal population. Just as ideologically invested as the Idealists, these Anarchs are usually ready to take up arms when their territory is attacked, but in the meantime, they care about building something worth fighting for.
Protectionist Anarchs can be peaceful punks or swift and harsh in their response to anyone who acts against the interests of their domain. For these Kindred, anyone acting against the Movement from within is just as big a threat to it as outside enemies.
Character Concepts
Sweeper: This type of Anarch keeps an eye out for unwelcome elements inside a city domain. When newcomers step out of line, they deal with it. It is sometimes a thankless job, as some consider sweepers little better than snitches.
The Fanatic: Following a calling to protect the Movement as a whole, the fanatic travels from domain to domain to remind power-hungry Barons what they should be fighting for and help newly established Anarch domains to get settled.
The Lurker: Some revolutionaries are better suited to the shadows, collecting knowledge, spreading misinformation or rumors when needed, lobbying for lasting change, or infiltrating Camarilla holdings.
The Good Soldier: Loyal to their post, this Anarch soldier protects a city by patrolling its borders, defending its inhabitants, and doing whatever else smarter, more established Anarchs tell them to.
Go-Betweens
Their closeness to the mortal world is how they serve the Movement.
Some Anarch factions welcome the Duskborn as equals; others are just as antagonistic as any heavily traditionalist Tower city. It's the nature of the Movement that attitudes toward anything other than the shared goals differ vastly. These mostly Duskborn Kindred, along with some fresh-faced, but true vampire fledglings, make themselves indispensable

by acting as the Movement's agents on the inside of mortal society. To Anarchs who value mortals highly, which many do, and cannot easily pass for mortals themselves, Go-Betweens are essential.
Character Concepts
Tech Boi: A Go-Between with some crucial modern tech skills that older Anarchs lack. They trade their knowledge for the privilege of getting hunting access on another's domain or in the fight against the technology-averse Tower.
Procurer: Someone who procures things, services, or vessels from the mortal world that are otherwise difficult or annoying to access. They may be daywalkers, accessing offers that are closed at night.
Indentured: A Go-Between who has earned the protection of a more prominent Anarch, bordering on being a servant. They might even be Blood Bound. Their treatment might be against Anarch philosophy, but it's OK since this individual isn't really considered a member of the Movement.
Opportunists
They might believe in the cause, but they sure do get a lot out of serving it.
Of course, they're in the Movement too, the power-hungry climbers who take any opportunity to move up in the world. More driven by their own ambitions than whatever goals are presently at the forefront of their Anarch allies' minds, they're here primarily because it serves them. It's easier to fly under the radar in most Anarch domains while doing something shady, at least if you say the right things and make the right facial expressions.
Character Concepts
Weasel: An instigator of trouble, sower of dissent, always acting to their own benefit. Embedded in a gang, Baron's inner circle, or another prominent Anarch faction, they reach for power from the sidelines.

121

Chapter Three: The Movement

Mobster: They prove themselves in a crisis by providing aid no one else can give, but afterwards they make sure to take full advantage of whatever power it earned them, with no intention of ever letting go.
Indispensable: Having made themselves crucial to their faction or city domain, they reap more benefits from their work than is just or well deserved. They might control a blood bank, an NGO, or the inside man in the local SI agency.
Fixer: This is the "I know a guy" guy. They're ready to do the dirty work of procuring whatever is needed and will do what it takes, especially bending Anarch ideology for the sake of a practical purpose. They won't tell you what it took, though, so you can stay confident that you're doing the right thing.
Semi-Camarilla
Not full Anarchs, these Kindred represent a Camarilla presence within the Anarch city.
Some Kindred leave the Tower willingly; others are simply forced out and have nowhere to go. Sometimes, for whatever reason, Kindred who remain tied to the Camarilla end up with the Anarchs. They are watched closely and probably have limited freedom of movement in the city,

but they still serve a purpose of some sort. The Anarch Movement isn't only a battle, it's also diplomatic relations and carefully planned political moves--things that require a little moral flexibility and for the social scene to sometimes get messy.
Character Concepts
Informant: This defector or double agent holds a lot of information about a particular Camarilla city or faction, which they readily turn over to the Movement on a regular basis. No one likes or trusts them.
Diplomat: A Camarilla representative in an Anarch city, this Kindred exists among Anarchs on a nightly basis and has sympathy for certain aspects of their philosophy. Probably a decent orator, they have contacts in both sects.
Brainwashed: This former Anarch-turnedCamarilla is back with the Anarchs without wanting to be. The Tower convinced them of their purpose and gave them a sense of order and belonging. Unwilling to give up on them, the Anarchs are attempting to deprogram them from what they broadly can agree is cult-think.
Guest Hostage: A beloved childe of a Camarilla Primogen or some other hotshot being kept as leverage in an Anarch domain--with or without Stockholm syndrome.

122

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

TWO WAYS TO LIBERATE A CITY DOMAIN

There are many ways to take over a city, but only a few that work if you want to have a chance to hold it. The Masquerade may be "a Camarilla thing," but that doesn't mean that secrecy isn't of the utmost importance for Anarchs, too. They'll therefore try to make battles coincide with mortal civil unrest or other big events that'll have police and media distracted. Sometimes such events need a helping hand to happen.
Here you find two examples of the process of taking a Camarilla city:
The Swift Takeover
Preparation: A mere Anarch foot soldier may not notice that anything is going on, but months of preparation can come before an attack that lasts minutes. Fighters need to be recruited, intel collected, deals struck, and plans and backup plans laid out.
Attack: When the attack comes, it's organized chaos. Fighters strike in key positions, and if the attackers have the intel, the most dangerous players are taken out first.
Aftermath: Assuming the attack was successful, the domains are divided between the liberators.

They might get to pick based on who was most impressive in the battle, if they didn't already divide the spoils in advance. Tensions may arise if some feel cheated.
The Back-Door Play
Preparation: It begins with a small gang of disguised Anarchs gaining access to a Camarilla city. Once in, they begin gathering intel on the domain's weaknesses and sow dissent among Kindred with lower standing in the Court.
Attack: If the infiltrators have been successful, the city begins to turn on itself. While the Court is busy, they let their Anarch allies into the domain through some back-door passage. The Court won't even notice it's under attack until Elysium is already burning.
Aftermath: The attackers take out key Camarilla figures but may give other Kindred the choice to join the Movement or get out of town. In the months that follow, some unrest is to be expected between the Anarch settlers and the ex-Camarilla Kindred--those who switched sides under duress or found the revolution wasn't what they'd hoped may question their choice for some time to come. 

123

Chapter Four:
OUTSIDE
THE GATES
That is not for you, Beckett. I know you have an obsession with the secrets of our ancients, but some truths are better left buried. How could any Kindred of the modern nights trust and venerate their elders if they knew the reality of what had gone before?
 The Banu Haqim Fatima al-Faqadi chides the Gangrel archaeologist Beckett
Autarkis. Independent clans. Nomads. Cults. The unbound. These outsiders are traditionally dismissed as bit players in the cold-blooded power games of the dead. But if a Primogen Councilor or Anarch firebrand would bother to ask the independent coterie of Caitiff feeding off single mothers in a low-wage district of their city, or the cultists who never leave their 60% Farsi-speaking suburb, and actually listen, they would learn more about real politics than any Machiavellian courtier could teach them. Welcome to the deadly liberty beyond the gate, to the often-bizarre politics of the forgotten and the hidden, the inbred family predators, the necromancers, the cannibal yoga teachers, the weird and lonely tenants, the night truckers, the true and the voluntary ignoramuses, and, last but not least, the postterminally unhinged.
This chapter describes several lesser-known types of independent dead and the unique political challenges they face. By choice or not, they've set themselves apart. Their independence from the sects doesn't mean they're free from cutthroat politics; it just means the players are different, the stage smaller, and the outcomes often even deadlier.
125

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

OUTSIDER CLANS AND GROUPS

Among the Kindred, a few groups are particularly known for their independence from sectarian conflicts among the Camarilla, Anarchs, the Ashirra, and the remnants of the Sabbat. Primary among these are the Caitiff and Duskborn, who tend to exist on the margins of Kindred society and often find themselves going it alone or in small groups.
Caitiff and Duskborn
Rejects and outcasts, their othered nature makes them mistrusted by other Kindred because they break the known conventions of the Blood. Numerous in recent decades, Caitiff and Duskborn are likely to band together and forge their own domains, though many such domains are brought into the Anarch Movement sooner or later.
Political Assets
Blending In: Vampires of weaker Blood more easily blend in among mortals, making them harder for true vampires to keep track of and giving them the advantage of working with mortals who might actually be loyal to them for reasons other than dependency.

Flexibility: When you've never gotten to be comfortable for long, you get good at adjusting to new circumstances and can get something useful out of any situation.
Hecata
Famously divided before their family reunion, individual Hecata have found homes within Camarilla and Anarch cities, but some leverage their old money and maintain domains independent of the sects--Venice famously belongs to the Hecata and probably will as long as it remains above water. Hecata characters of various bloodlines make interesting options for neutral coteries selling services to other factions.
Political Assets
Connections: When family is everything to your bloodline, you never have to handle any problem on your own unless you want to. Even those who cut off their relatives can come back, although it may cost them. History: Many Hecata bloodlines have existed in the same domains for many decades. They have roots in the business and culture of their cities and can leverage that knowledge against outsiders as long as they're on their home turf.

126

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Salubri and Ravnos
Though each clan survived apocalyptic conditions for their bloodlines, neither is particularly numerous tonight. Individuals may achieve some measure of acceptance in the sects, but many simply eke out their nightly existence without much companionship. Occasionally, small broods of Salubri or Ravnos travel together, join smaller factions, or serve as couriers between domains.
Political Assets
More Fiction than Fact: What other Kindred aren't aware of or don't even believe in, they won't have prepared for. That Fascination Factor: Their otherness marks them as targets but also as something utterly fascinating to the average Kindred, who will hear out what they have to say to get a little closer to them.

Tzimisce
Many Tzimisce find their covetous natures abhor the power structures and community of adherence to sect rules. While some of them thrive within the Sabbat, others prefer the ultimate freedom of being their own lords, ruling over small, independent domains or carefully guarded sections of sectarian domains.
Political Assets
Ruthless Strategists: A Tzimisce will stop at nothing to defend their territory. Sheer will can take you far, even against an overwhelming greater force. Boundless Servitude: The mortal servants of certain Tzimisce are much more loyal than is normal, even for a ghoul. Where the Dragons have nurtured Revenant families over generations, they can often count on competent mortal allies to take care of much of their business, almost as equals.

Gangrel
Stereotypically, the Gangrel are seen as independent minded and inclined towards a solitary or nomadic existence. Great swaths of their childer were convinced to join first the Camarilla and now the Anarchs, but many prefer the existence of the autonomous traveler.
Political Assets
Respect: Many Kindred treat the independent Gangrel with more respect than other outsiders, considering their wandering ways as their true nature. They can often act as mediators between different sects and the autarkis. Pack Mentality: Independent Gangrel recognize and help each other. Even the ones not currently on the road usually have a past as nomads or vagabonds or know somebody who does.

127

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

TYPES OF OUTSIDERS

Kindred on the margins tend to fit in one or more of four categories:
 Hermits : Some Kindred go it alone. Whether they're isolated by choice or by circumstance, this individual or small coterie is without a larger group of outcasts to back them up. Most commonly, Hermits have their haven far from any city domain.
 Nomads: This type moves around between domains, requiring either staunch independence or craven pandering to every local authority. Nomadic Kindred often trade in favors or information as they travel, making them useful enough that even if the locals aren't always pleased to see them, they won't go out of their way to hurt them either.
 Settlers in Sectarian Domains: Within a Camarilla or Anarch domain, Hierophants

of Kindred churches and dons of a Hecata crime family may play by the rules to a degree but operate as neighboring factions. Kindred businesses, such as the Circulatory System, the Hunt Club, and smaller, local establishments, such as the Succubus Clubs or their imitators, often maintain an air of neutrality in mixed domains or provide their services to Kindred of any affiliation for the right price.  Citizens of an Autarky: Sometimes enough independents band together to claim a city domain of their own, and any sect-aligned Kindred settling there has to answer to them. Some sectarian cities are sect in name only, autarkies in reality. Ancient Constantinople is often held up as an ideal autarky, once ruled by a trinity of elders with their own laws and traditions.

THE INCONNU The Inconnu is a rumored sect of non-interventionist elders, who are occasionally considered autarkis. Most ancillae and younger Kindred these nights only know of instances of Inconnu that were revealed to be frauds. Older tales, from before the Beckoning, suggest they once had considerable power and used it to manipulate the great sects in secret.

128

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

WHO IS THE AUTARK?

Autark comes from the Greek autarkeia, meaning "sufficiency in oneself" and autarkhein, "absolute ruler." These are two very different meanings, and the reality of the autark's existence can be just as varied. While one makes do with scraps to exist unnoticed and unbothered in the crevices of Kindred society, another has become the absolute ruler of their city domain, powerful enough that challenging them would be more trouble than it's worth for their sectarian neighbors. Some autarkis prefer to go it alone; others are part of bigger groups, and their independence is a relative thing--while they don't answer to any sectarian ruler, they're still tied through Blood, faith, or other bonds to the Kindred they share the autarky with.
What Makes Autark Play Interesting?
Even in the Court of Prince Villon, being introduced as "the Autark of Heidelberg University" casts a reverential silence over Elysium. There's potential in playing within one of the sects, as an outsider who can engage with its intrigues but isn't necessarily a central part of them. But an autark can also easily avoid the political bullshit, making this kind of play perfect for groups that want to focus their game on the classic core experience of being a vampire--hunting, being hunted, and maintaining or denying their own humanity.

Or, if you'd like, an autark coterie can ignore most of the Kindred world while engaging solely in mortal politics, perhaps championing a cause from their breathing days. Whether the Kindred come knocking with their own schemes at some point depends on the Storyteller.
Autark Archetypes
Every autark is different, but these are some common types:
Exile
Not all autarkis have chosen to be cut off from the sects. Instead, they are exiles or refugees who wear their stigma like a crown. They were banished by their peers or soft sires, who could not bear the thought of destroying their progeny, or they had to flee in the last moments before dawn, hiding under coats and carpets.
Exiled autarkis in enormous city domains like Rio often gather around a shared set of Convictions and Touchstones related to the reason they stay apart and hidden. For active exiles, politics becomes centered around sheltering from the sects, hot mortal politics, running interference, distractions, and maintaining clandestine connections with sect agents.

129

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

HOSPITALITY FOR NOMADS Nomadic independents are often met with several restrictions they must abide by, with many of these selectively enforced and levied on a coterie-by-coterie basis. Others make similar rules for themselves to avoid problems before they arise. Possible restrictions include: · Feed only in certain areas or from specific populations. · Avoid feeding entirely within claimed domains. · Slumber only in designated areas. · Stay no longer than three nights. · Communicate only with designated liaisons. · Attendance at Elysium or similar events is mandatory. · Visits must be chaperoned by an established coterie or Shadow. · Visitors must recite and swear to uphold the Traditions, Prince's Courtesies, rules of Elysium, or other local
rules. · Visitors must submit Blood to a local sorcerer to ensure their clan, sire, and crimes of diablerie are known. · Visitors must report news from recent travels to the local authority or the entire domain.

Fake Exile
Fake Exiles want to return to their parent sect, preferably with a large enough bang to win them praxis or a seat in a body like the Primogen Council. They are despised by the true outsiders and hunted by their enemies. Their unlives are defined by constant political games of counterespionage, distraction, and paranoid fantasies of regaining lost status. They play the long odds in a high-stakes game that will, later if not sooner, present them with the choice of betraying the outsider world they have been kicked out into. Sheltering in a rival sect may be an option, but this is often a fool's game.
For an ex-Camarilla autark to be let into the Anarchs, they must gain trust, represented by a major political boon or by offering something of significant value to the Anarchs. Playing a Fake Exile

desperate to regain a lost position is excellent for a short and deadly political chronicle with a set end goal: to return from exile or face final death trying. Longer, more complex arcs of this type could see the outside world of autarky exert a stronger, more attractive pull, perhaps ultimately proving to be the lesser of evils.
Collaborator
Collaborators attempt to turn their independence into a marketable asset. Ferrying goods or vessels between domains, carrying messages, acting as spies and neutral parties in negotiations, they do not necessarily want to join any of the larger factions but might enjoy the position they've created for themselves. They are often hated by other autarkis, who see them as two-faced cowards.

130

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

NOMENCLATURE Kindred language, much like mortal language, is wonderfully imprecise. Ambiguities lead to humor and poetry as well as conflict.
Autarkis: Self-ruled. One outside Kindred society. Among the ancillae and elders, the term is a singular and plural noun as well as an adjective. Younger Kindred often use autark as a singular. Some use this term the same way as they use independent; others claim that it refers only to those who are truly autonomous, excluding any independents who remain loyal to a cult or bloodline.
Autarky: A domain independent of the great sects. Plural: autarkies. Unbound: Kindred who reject the Camarilla. Sometimes includes Anarchs as well as autarkis. Independent: Kindred who are not part of one of the great sects: the Anarchs, Camarilla, the Ashirra, or Sabbat. See Autarkis. Independent Clans: Formerly used to describe the Hecata (under the name Giovanni), Ravnos, Setites (both the Ministry and the Church of Set), and the Banu Haqim, whose elders wouldn't or couldn't join one of the greater sects. Tonight, few elders can command an entire clan, and the term is falling out of use.

Vagabond
Vagabonds move from domain to domain, refusing to settle down or commit to one sect. As outsiders, they're treated differently from couriers or messengers who primarily deal with a single sect or faction, but that difference is sometimes a matter of degree.
Autark vagabonds often avoid other Kindred when possible, slinking through domains without formal introductions or so much as a hello. Getting caught by the authorities is a dangerous proposition, and they fear other Kindred as much as lupines or Second Inquisition agents on the road, assuming they know about these in the first place. Local authorities may tolerate their presence but restrict feeding and shelter to certain disfavored districts.

Subservient Autark
This is an autark who is loyal to one or more sectaligned Kindred but has not yet been invited to join. Or perhaps they maintain some semblance of independence by holding off on the decision. They might be someone the dominant faction has need of, such as an individual with an important mortal Mask that makes collaboration with mortal decisionmakers easier. They could be someone the local ruler considers of absolutely no importance--like a thin-blood failed Nosferatu whose haven is on territory that nobody else even wants to go near. Or they could be someone who has been "temporarily" ousted from Kindred society proper and is serving their sentence as a non-citizen with all the obligations and none of the rights of real Kindred.

131

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates
PROBLEMS FOR OUTSIDERS Political autark problems include things most sectarian vampires never have to worry about, like: · The Second Masquerade: Non-sectarian characters may need to maintain a "second Masquerade" to
remain unnoticed by a city's major Kindred players or authorities. · Close Mortal Connections: Blending into tightly knit mortal religious organizations, social scenes, and
fringe subcultures helps autarkis form the base for their power structure. This may even mean obeying a mortal who doesn't know you are a vampire. · Unusual Beliefs: Proselytizing and celebrating an alternative ideological creed have often been frowned upon by the sects, who prefer to be the sole authority of their members. · The Nightly Struggle: Using politics can be necessary to sustain oneself night to night. To some independents, mortal pawns are all they have to scrape together resources and find a decent place to rest during the day. · Isolation and Alienation: It's challenging to be the only coterie of Kindred in a domain dominated by another supernatural society, be it packs of werewolves, legions of restless dead, or even stranger, unknown forces beyond the coterie's comprehension. · Neutrality: Some lesser factions are seen as a playground or staging ground for other Kindred. Managing a neutral domain, where the Camarilla Prince and Anarch Barons meet to parley under your watchful eyes, can take a skilled hand. · Serving a Need: Independents thrive on their own necessity. They must often provide a service to other Kindred, like collecting blood, erasing records, or guiding fellow Kindred through a treacherous part of a domain. These services may be needed by all factions on the front lines of the Gehenna War, or simply be dangerous tasks that local authorities prefer to stay clear of.
132

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

THE GEOGRAPHY OF AUTARKIES

Stories that take place in autarkies play out very differently depending on the type of territory and the proximity of other domains. Independent domains are often located either very far from sectarian Kindred or in harsh territory, such as undesirable slums or hard-to-manage gated communities. For where a Camarilla Prince or Anarch Baron sees something of value, they will eventually maneuver to have their vassals claim it. An independent domain near others must either keep its mortal territory undesirable enough to ward off such advances, or be protected by literal or metaphorical high walls to keep the neighbors out.
Some of the most common autarkies are bloodline cities and religious communities.
Isolated Cities
Isolated cities tend to be just far enough away from other major Kindred domains that travel is difficult and can take several evenings and much planning. Communication with other domains may have been commonplace before the rise of the Second Inquisition, but now it is rarely and carefully done.
Examples of Isolated City Politics  Domain and haven allocation and negotiation to
ensure that local Kindred can easily move around the city, but strangers struggle to find their way in  Preparing to handle problems that would normally be sect business

 Handling the fallout when someone has broken the seal of silence and alerted nearby Camarilla domains to the city's existence
Little Princes
Throughout Kindred history, these ultra-isolated domains have appeared from time to time. It's hard to say how many of them exist, because they are inhabited by one or a small handful of vampires who believe themselves to be totally alone in the night or have made the choice to pretend they are. Like the "Little Prince" of the famous French children's story, the autarkis here exists in splendid solitude. They may never have encountered others like themselves, or didn't have the knowledge to recognize them when they did. Some bloodlines have carried through millennia with only ever one member at a time, each vampire destroying themselves in the Embrace of a childe, believing their curse commands it.
Examples of Little Prince Politics  Engaging with mortal politics to justify or ease
your undead existence  Taking actions to keep the city domain hidden
from the greater sects  Religious or fanatical schemes that serve the
isolated vampire's fantasy of their own extreme importance as the only one in existence

133

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

Satellite Cities
Satellite cities are a common type of autarky. Smaller cities that house few Kindred tend to be about a four-hour commute from other, larger domains. Distance is a friend to autarkies, with stronger authorities forced to tolerate minor domains that are less accessible. When the local Church of the Angel is a four-hour drive down roads patrolled by lupines or agents of the Second Inquisition, it tends to be left alone. Depending on the latitude and time of year, such domains can be isolated for months at a time.
These cities tend to have few Kindred and may have a long-established local authority. A Tzimisce autark might proclaim herself Prince but follow old traditions passed down from her elders or new laws of her own design. A former Anarch enclave set itself up in an isolated city in the 1950s, inspired by Salvador Garcia, but over time, it has become the satellite city of a new Anarch domain.
Mortals and their cities tend to sprawl. What was distant in the age of steam is now much closer thanks to modern technology. A new overnight commuter line can wreak unexpected havoc among the Kindred. Once isolated cities become satellite cities or subdomains with time.
Examples of Satellite City Politics  Keeping things friendly with the nearby sect
domain yet uninviting enough that those Kindred don't get the idea to come bother you  Making sure the city autarkis are on board with the action plan for handling sect contact and that none of them are starting to agree a little too much with the sect  Handling Anarch visitors trying to convince you to join their cause and lose your unlives on the front lines of their battles for imaginary freedom
Mortal Hubs
Occasionally, major territories are claimed by independent groups that are hubs of mortal activity: airports, major hotels or resort districts, cruise ships,

sports arenas or concert venues, specific hospitals or networks of hospitals, and so forth. Where blood flows, there will be Kindred. Autarkies in charge of prime real estate must negotiate with their neighbors, often maintaining neutrality until a tyrant grows the backbone to take them on.
Examples of Mortal Hub Politics  Negotiating deals with neighboring city domains  Keeping up the charade of being an active,
mortal community member who is just very invested in the running of the hub  Dealing with common squabbles between community members who work closely together and see each other too much
Subdomains
Minor autarkies often exist near, or even within, larger domains.
