A Hierophant of the Church of Set or a thin-blood support network may thrive in an affluent suburb or smaller city an hour's drive from a larger domain claimed by a Camarilla Prince. The greater city tolerates the autarky either because the leadership doesn't see it as a threat or because it has some use for it. A subdomain may be the buffer zone between a Camarilla city and lupine territory, or the autarkis are specialists who can provide something the greater city needs, for instance.
Domineering Anarch Barons or Camarilla Princes rarely relish rivals dwelling close. Not only does their mere existence inspire the young and impulsive to challenge a tyrant, but such domains can prove a safe harbor for bad seeds. By hook or by crook, such rulers will usually entangle small domains, eventually forcing them to bow and accept membership or service within the larger domain.
For younger Kindred, a neighboring autarky can be a source of learning, entertainment, supplies, or terror. Strange Kindred with odd beliefs and Disciplines can lure the curious to their side. Kindred churches tend to welcome neonates asking about history, myth, and salvation, when neither Prince nor Baron has answers.

134

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Examples of Subdomain Politics  Pretending to lick the greater city leader's boots
while secretly working to outmaneuver them in deals where you prefer a different outcome

 Ensuring that autark Kindred are not caught breaking the rules of the greater domain on their territory
 Competing for rank and status within the subdomain

HOW AUTARKIES SUBMIT Autarkies with powerful neighbors often give up some measure of freedom for their own survival, as Anarchs and Camarilla alike claim their philosophy is universal and applies to all Kindred.
Subdomain autarkies may have their own ambassador or Primogen representing their interests, so long as they agree to most or all of the rules of the great sect. A Church of Set domain might agree to follow all the Traditions but interpret their own Hierophant as the eldest for the Tradition of Destruction. A hacktivist collective may be tolerated operating out of a huge casino, hotel, and entertainment complex so long as they feed exclusively within their territory, send one emissary to every Elysium, and pay a tax of a major boon each month. The Circulatory System is tolerated at highway rest stops, train stations, and bus depots in exchange for providing a powerful coterie of Ventrue with the mortal vessels matching a rotating menu of rare vintages.

INSIDE AN AUTARKY

These are some of the themes you can explore once the players' characters hold their own autarky or have their haven in one: Small Domain Politics: Fighting for power without the looming expectations of a greater sect can mean that there are no rules and no expectations, and that final death is absolutely on the table. Political skirmishes for city rule can become deadly battles for survival.
External Pressure: The nearest sect might not currently be in control, but they absolutely want to be.
Resource Control: Even without a Prince, someone still needs to manage feeding rights and territory disputes--or the panic among the kine when feeding gets out of control.

Opposing Governance Opinions: The vampires settled in the domain all have opinions on how it should be ruled. They may agree that they don't want to be sectarian, but their ideas of what they do want are vastly different.
Trade and Diplomacy: Independence doesn't mean not engaging with your neighbors. The ones you ignore are likely to come for you when you least expect them.
The Creeping Dread: Sometimes small domains become infected by strange ideas without their Kindred noticing before it is too late. Paranoia, cultish thinking, and conspiracy theories spread like lichen over a forgotten corpse.

135

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

FACES OF INDEPENDENCE

More than a few Kindred have refused the enticements of the great sects over the centuries, choosing to walk their own path through the night. Some of them are among the best-known Kindred in history, their refusal to bow to Camarilla Princes or the Anarch Barons just the beginning of their iconoclastic approach to unlife.

Malik Diallo
A New York club promoter and nightlife entrepreneur in life, Malik Diallo has become one of the few leaders among Clan Salubri. He saw a Tremere Archon destroy his sire on the night of his Embrace but managed to escape, finding refuge outside the Camarilla sphere of influence. As he has come to understand the history of his own clan, he has been radicalized, realizing that his bloodline has suffered through the centuries perhaps more than any other.

Diallo has come to represent a common idea among the few remaining Salubri of the modern nights: all Tremere must be destroyed. They almost succeeded in wiping out the Salubri, and vengeance and justice demand that they pay the price. According to Diallo, the Tremere never stopped persecuting his clan, something he learned through his own bitter experience. They may seem close to invincible, but he believes that fate is going to grant him the opportunity to see them swept away.
Because of his agitation and rising fame, Diallo has become a lightning rod for traditionalist Tremere who believe it is their duty to finish the job started by their clan founders so long ago. They try to destroy Diallo on a regular basis, proving him right.
Because of Tremere persecution, Diallo has had to spend his unlife using a mortal Mask to tour with his club in different cities. Unfortunately, every time he manages to establish a new mortal identity, the Tremere figure it out, and he has to start all over again.
He would have been destroyed already, but he has found unusual allies and protectors in autarkies where the locals don't much care for the Tremere. They support Diallo just to spite the Tremere, not for any great affection for the Salubri cause.
Along the way, Diallo has become something of a self-taught occult history wunderkind because to

136

COURTS OF THE DAMNED
him, understanding the past is crucial to his own survival. He must know why the Salubri are so hated and feared to be able to successfully lead a counterattack. He has a habit of siring new childer in domains he passes through, and he wants to be able to explain to them what the clan is all about.
Clan: Salubri Generation: 12th Embrace: 2019 Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 7 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 4, Social 7, Mental 6, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Streetwise 8, Persuasion 9, Occult 8 Disciplines: Auspex 2 (Heightened Senses, Premonition), Dominate 3 (Compel, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Fortitude 1 (Unswayable Mind) In Stories: Diallo brings in the heat. When he comes to a domain, Tremere pursuers are usually not far behind. Furthermore, he doesn't care about rules for who can sire childer and when, believing that they were invented to keep his clan small and weak. Even if he leaves, the problems in his wake often persist to bedevil the locals. For Salubri players' characters, Diallo can be an invaluable ally and source of information and identity. He can explain the clan's struggle and help players' characters do something about it. His focus is political, but he's very happy to meet Salubri of a more philosophical and visionary bent.
137

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates
Jaliah Imani
A stray vampire may only have a tenuous connection to their bloodline. That is the case with Jaliah Imani, Embraced in a fit of emotion in the bathroom of a Washington D.C. high school. Imani had just started as a teacher, and her unknown sire stole the future she'd finally secured for herself in an act of possessive frenzy it took her years to understand.
In her early years in Camarilla domains, Imani tried to understand both herself and the vampire society around her. It was a struggle to find enough information about her sire to realize that she was a Tzimisce and even more of a struggle to learn what that meant. Meanwhile, she was in constant conflict with the Princes and Sheriffs of every domain she tried to make a haven in because their rules didn't make sense to her.
138

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

It all boiled down to this: in life, she'd had the blessings of family and community, but as a Black woman in the U.S., she'd also had to eat a lot of shit. As an undead monster, she didn't feel like doing it anymore. She'd tried to be a good and decent person, but since life had been robbed from her, it was time for her to make her own decisions.
Unfortunately, the Princes of the Camarilla didn't agree with these ideas, forcing Imani to escape from more than one domain.
Everything changed in 1998 in New York when Imani met an old Romanian Tzimisce on his way through the domain. To her, the old man was like a prophet, a revelation of what the heritage of her Blood really meant. He told her about the European domains of old, before the Camarilla, when a vampire lord was the sole sovereign of their domain with no need to bow to anyone. He painted a picture of mighty and terrifying Tzimisce ancestors, their choice to dispense either cruelty or mercy entirely in their own hands.
From this encounter, Imani formed a philosophy of what it should mean to be a Tzimisce in North America in the 21st century. She became a teacher once again, finding other lost members of her clan and teaching them about their noble, terrifying legacy. By now, some of what she talks about is her own invention, mixed with stories from that one chance encounter in New York.
A Tzimisce needs her own domain. That's the case with Imani as well, and she attempts to fulfill the vision of her bloodline by building her own vampiric paradise in small-town America.

Clan: Tzimisce Generation: 10th Embrace: 1964 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 6 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 7, Mental 6, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Persuasion 8, Academics 8, Occult 8 Disciplines: Animalism 2 (Bond Famulus, Feral Whispers), Auspex 1 (Heightened Senses), Dominate 5 (Compel, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind, Rationalize, Terminal Decree), Protean 3 (Eyes of the Beast, Vicissitude,* Earth Meld), Blood Sorcery 1 (Koldunic Sorcery--Earth**; Ritual: Clinging of the Insect) *Players Guide, p. 81 **Blood Sigils, p. 61 Clan Bane: Her charge is the leather-bound book of her teachings. In Stories: The Masquerade means that access to the history of the Kindred is not at all a given if you're a young vampire. How do you know the traditions of your clan? It's easier in Camarilla domains (especially if you're a Ventrue or a Tremere), but for a stray Tzimisce, to say nothing of the Ravnos or the Salubri, you have to rely on hearsay and rumor. The understanding of one's bloodline often informs motivations and future goals. That's the function of Imani in a chronicle: her teaching energizes young Tzimisce, motivating them to create breakaway autarkies of their own in a bid to claim their vampiric birthright.

139

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates
Gretchen "The Pale Scorpion" Vogt
A nurse in life, Gretchen Vogt was a serial killer who specialized in murdering elderly people in her care without getting caught. After a few years in her native Hamburg, she moved to the U.S. to avoid notice. She spent a year or two in each hospital before switching to keep under the radar.
Vogt's sire had no idea what he was dealing with when he Embraced her. He thought he was getting an intelligent, attractive medical professional who could help him with the many ghouls he was running experiments on. Instead, he was soon a decaying corpse, his research operation taken over by his murderous childe.
140

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

As a vampire, Vogt is quite open about her predilections. She says that even as a mortal, she felt a certain calling to be there at the end of life. The moment of death was so satisfying, fascinating, that she simply couldn't wait for it to occur naturally. Fortunately, the ancient art of the poisoner was alive and well, with all the updates provided by modern medicine.
As one of the Kindred, Vogt has discovered that there's a wider spiritual and intellectual dimension to the world. She finds the idea of ghosts annoying and wonders if they might also somehow be destroyed so that when she kills someone, they'll be well and truly extinguished. Her nickname comes from her appearance and her habit of turning on her allies when there's an opportunity to add their remains to her collection.
Declared Anathema among both Camarilla and Anarchs, Vogt rules a domain of her own, built around a decrepit, underfunded hospital. In its basement, she has the privacy she needs to continue her sire's intellectual work. Her shamelessness has made her something of a Hecata celebrity, a dark role model for Kindred who are troubled by their humanity and embrace their violent impulses without going over to the mysticism of the Sabbat.
This has created a political project of sorts around Vogt, a domain where nihilism rules and vampires believe their intellect will save them from the way of the wight. It's unlikely that this is sustainable at all, and many Kindred seduced into her orbit have already been lost to the Hunger. Vogt worries somewhat that what happened to them will also happen to her.

Clan: Hecata Generation: 11th Embrace: 1978 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 1 General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 5, Mental 8, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Subterfuge 7, Occult 10, Medicine 9 Disciplines: Auspex 4 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition, Scry the Soul, Spirit's Touch), Celerity 1 (Rapid Reflexes), Fortitude 3 (Unswayable Mind, Toughness, Fortify the Inner Façade), Oblivion 5 (Binding Fetter, Where the Veil Thins, Aura of Decay, Necrotic Plague, Skuld Fulfilled; Ceremonies: Summon Spirit, Compel Spirit, Host Spirit, Bind the Spirit, Lazarene Blessing)* *Players Guide, pp. 84­98 In Stories: Sometimes there's a reason why an autark is not welcome among the sects. Vogt is an example of this, the focus of an unstable, catastrophic political project built around delusional nihilism. Her followers believe that they can give in to their worst impulses without losing their minds, and if they start to doubt, it's usually way too late. Vogt can also make for an unpleasant ally for players' characters who have ventured far from the familiarity of Camarilla or Anarch domains. Perhaps she knows something about the dead that the characters desperately need, so they have to ignore her confessed murderous nature.

141

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates
Leandro Cárdenas
When many Lasombra joined the Camarilla in a deal brokered in Chicago, one of the rules was that if an individual Lasombra wanted to join the sect, they had to prove their allegiance by destroying another member of their clan. This led to a spate of attacks on autark Lasombra and members of the clan who already existed in Camarilla domains.
142

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

For many Lasombra, this has been a difficult time, but for Leandro Cárdenas it was an opportunity. Originally driven out of Washington, D.C. because he'd betrayed local ancillae to the Anarchs and then the Anarchs to a group of hunters, Cárdenas had been cooling his heels in Gary, Michigan, when he heard about the requirements for Lasombra to join. This sparked an idea: he could sire new Lasombra vampires and sell them to his desperate clanmates to be destroyed in a suitably dramatic fashion as the price of entry.
An experienced fixer, rat, and turncoat, Cárdenas has a wide network of contacts among the Kindred and the kine. As part of his service, he'll make up an identity for the recently Embraced Lasombra, making it seem as if they were much older than they actually were. Thus, if a Camarilla Sheriff asks around, it sounds like the prospective new Camarilla member has destroyed someone with a reputation inside the clan.
While the operation has gained Cárdenas boons and money, it has also cemented his position as an autark doomed to skulk around small border domains. The Lasombra who have used his services don't want to be reminded of his existence, and many of his clanmates consider him a vile creep for sullying the Blood in such a way. Lasombra are not known to be humanitarians, but something in the cavalier way Cárdenas has treated both his victims and his own Blood rubs many the wrong way.

Clan: Lasombra Generation: 10th Embrace: 1927 Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 4 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 7, Mental 7, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Streetwise 8, Subterfuge 8, Occult 8 Disciplines: Dominate 3 (Cloud Memory, Mesmerize, The Forgetful Mind), Obfuscate 3 (Cloak of Shadows, Unseen Passage, Mask of a Thousand Faces), Potence 3 (Lethal Body, Prowess, Brutal Feed), Oblivion 2 (Ashes to Ashes, Arms of Ahriman; Ceremony: The Gift of False Life)* *Players Guide, pp. 84­98 In Stories: Cárdenas shows up when he smells an opportunity created by a new political alignment. He's good at figuring out power shifts and what different people need. He's also unburdened by ethical boundaries when it comes to providing for his clients. For the players' characters, Cárdenas can be a last resort, who can get them something so objectionable, nobody else wants anything to do with it. Unfortunately, the price he asks isn't small, for he understands the value of the service he provides.

143

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

RUNNING AUTARK CHRONICLES

After months, years, or even decades playing in Camarilla and Anarch cities, some troupes collectively yearn for something different or something more. Autarkies present a new option. Chronicles comprising only Hecata characters, domains ruled by Kindred churches, or mercenaries of the Circulatory System and Pony Express can be a welcome divergence from typical tales of Camarilla versus Anarch versus Sabbat.
These are some of the ways you can change up the dynamic from other Vampire chronicles in autark chronicles:  Focus on clan stories that don't make sense in a
Camarilla or Anarch domain, such as Hecata, Tzimisce, Ravnos, or Salubri.  Explore Kindred faiths by focusing on a domain run by one of them, whether or not they also deal with the larger sects.

 Play unencumbered by the vast history of Vampire while focusing on the personal trials and tribulations of ignorant Caitiff, Duskborn, or others.
Handling Lore
Autark chronicles come with their own challenges. Primary among these is the amount of setting knowledge that can be required. New players may not find a chronicle friendly if they need to learn the basics of a Kindred church, rare clans, and everyone's relationship to the Anarchs, Camarilla, and Sabbat. Consider using characters that don't know much either, letting the players discover the lore through their characters.

AUTARKIS MERIT ·· Moral Dissident: Autark Only. Autarkis do not have to compromise their morals to belong to a sect or ideological faction and are often much more humane. But as the saying goes, no good deed goes unpunished. To reflect strength of will, spite, and fierce independence, choose two extra Convictions and Touchstones.
The drawback is that the outsider can only take this Merit if they pay for it by buying appropriate Flaws. These represent enemies, fabricated sinister rumors, psychological scars, or even curses levied against them, or reflect a lonely life of someone judged too dangerous to be close to.

144

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Autarkis and Convictions
Without greater laws, unbound are often driven entirely by their Convictions. Autark coteries may be united around shared moral or political values and fight anyone opposing them. Commonly, such Convictions are derived from struggles the characters burned for during their breathing days. Examples could include a coterie fighting for Irish

independence from the English or defending Romania from Russian and Turkish influence or a clique of immortals seeking vengeance for a crime long forgotten by the living world. Expect Touchstones and mortals to play a greater part in autark chronicles, especially where there is nothing resembling a political sect structure to advance within.

STORYTELLING STRANGE WAYS Interacting with independents often means entering new social scenes or subcultures, where the norms and expectations are entirely different. What gives clout in a new scene, or what fashion and behavioral codes apply, is almost impossible to predict. To emulate this, as the coterie enters a new social group, decide on a single weird factor that is simply a must to be someone in a scene. It can be a drug to be used or avoided, a turn of phrase never or always to be used, an odd hobbyist group to blame or celebrate, or a genre of literature to be celebrated or loathed. Make it weird and hard to guess. And don't give any hints. If you don't know about it, no one will tell you. You'll have to observe or find out through trial and error, get under a member's skin, perhaps start a relationship with one of the locals to learn what seems so obvious to everyone else. Before they crack the code, all Social tests in the scene take a one- or even two-dice penalty if we're talking about a truly arrogant or secretive group.
This method is a crude simplification, but it works well to make the players' characters feel like strangers in a strange land as the in-the-knows snicker and scoff at them until they are finally let in on the secret and gain a more solid sense of belonging.

145

Chapter Four: Outside the Gates

CULT POLITICS

Acommon independent faction is a cult or religious organization. In such factions, power plays look different compared to formal political structures. In cults and churches, power formally derives from what or who is being worshiped and is often transmitted through an immutable hierarchy of holiness, rather than being sold as an obvious need for structure. This makes all the difference.
For inspiration, Table 3 lists a selection of such organizations of the dead.
The System of Holiness
Anyone claiming leadership or even influence among the faithful must keep up a façade of holiness rather than merely projecting power or trustworthiness. Depending on the cult in question, charismatic leadership, prophecy, divine favor, and grand gestures are often faster routes to power than the nurturing of contacts encouraged in societies attempting to emulate Enlightenment or even Humanist ideas of governance.
If mortal politics are a place where sociopaths thrive, cults and their power games suit psychologies that contemporary psychologists would describe as narcissistic or downright psychopathic.
In the churches of Blood, political games are played for different, more permanent stakes than in other hierarchies of Blood. Once a priest has been ordained, no matter if they are later ousted, they must always be treated as holier than the normal flock. A church cannot admit being wrong, since this would throw doubt on the faith itself, so even

defeated enemies must often be kept around in some capacity, rather than be exiled or executed.
Churches may be more static than even the great glacial sects of the damned, but church politics are still a vicious game. Here is a list of power moves and strategies found in social groups governed by faith:
Revelatory Authority: The leader claims to receive psychic messages and orders directly from the cult's divinity.
The Theater of Martyrdom: Those who suffer for the faith gain legitimacy and influence.
Doctrine Manipulation: If it can be justified by the holy text, it can be done.
Sanctified Alliances: Some potential allies are better than others because the divine leader would approve.
Excommunication as the Cure: The true believer quietly encourages the faithful to shun a rival, not for what they've done, but for how they've failed in their faith.
The Proof Is in the Miracle: The leaders of the cult stage miraculous acts to prove divine favor.
Redemption as a Power Play: Publicly forgiving an enemy for their sins makes you seem magnanimous and merciful.
Shared Secret Knowledge: Those in the cult share secrets no one outside the cult is in on, binding them together. The secret can be wonderful, shameful, or both. The one who controls that knowledge controls the cult. 

146

Chapter Five:
THE STORYTELLER'S
MANIFESTO
The personal is political. There's no escaping vampire politics once you have been bitten--at least not if you take part in one of the societies of the night. Because even if you try to mind your own business, others out there will see your choices as challenges to their rule and make how you feed, whom you share a haven with, and what bloodline you belong to out to be political.
This chapter is about how to use politics in your game as the Storyteller--whether it's the main focus or something that simply happens to the characters from time to time. First, it presents advice on how to make political themes work in your game, and then it moves on to big setting events in Vampire: The Masquerade and how to present them so your players can have fun with them.
149

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

HOW MUCH POLITICS?

Politics among the undead is a core component of Vampire, but you decide how much spotlight it gets in your narrative. Below are three options to get you thinking about the role of politics in your story or chronicle.
As the Background
Politics doesn't have to be at the front of your narrative for you to use this book. A handful of pointed events in an otherwise non-political story can create the feeling that the machinations of the Kindred are constantly simmering just beneath the surface.
Examples:  Set Dressing: The coterie enters Elysium.
Powerful vampires vie for the Prince's favor, secrets are whispered into ears in hushed corners, and someone is goaded into a face-losing Frenzy. The character they came to meet has just been outmaneuvered by a rival and is now looking for someone to take it out on.  Enemy of My Enemy: The coterie gets offers to work together from two opposing heavyweights, each with different strings attached. Choosing one automatically angers the other.  It's Just Politics: The coterie suddenly faces an obstacle. Their usual allies refuse to help, claiming the coterie pissed off the wrong Kindred and that the timing couldn't be worse.

 You Didn't Hear This from Me: While talking with their contact, the coterie hears a rumor that their rivals are temporarily vulnerable. Maybe the rumor is coming from someone who has an axe to grind with their rivals? If your players engage with political themes, you
can gradually increase the intrigue by introducing more Kindred with stakes in the domain's power games.
Well-Suited For: Blood Cult, Champions, Fang Gang, Hunting Party, Nomads, Questari (Vampire, p. 197)
As the Theme
A story can treat politics thematically even if your troupe isn't interested in playing politically. Cleaning up after or getting caught in the wake of a power struggle can drive the coterie's mission, create tension with others who have a stake in it, or hint at who might come after them if they walk away.
For inspiration, check out crime dramas where political power collides with the everyday or political fiction told from the point of view of nonpoliticians.
Examples:  Political Assignment: The coterie has been
drafted by their domain ruler to kick down the doors of their political rivals or guard against an impending revolution.

150

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

 Legacy: The coterie is following in the footsteps of their sires or other predecessors and thus inherit their political rivals. Perhaps they're up against the political clique that was behind their predecessors' downfall?
 Whodunit: The coterie is investigating a Tradition violation. Unbeknownst to them, the crime was politically motivated--and the tracks lead all the way to the top. A chronicle that features politics as a theme
can evolve into a full-blown political game if the players pursue those avenues, or, just as easily, see its intrigues fade into the background when the focus shifts elsewhere.
Well-Suited For: Cerberus, Commando, Day Watch, Vehme, Watchmen (Vampire, p. 197)
As the Activity
If you want to go all in, your story is about doing politics: the characters are after power, and your game will be about the political intrigue needed to seize and keep it. You can still have a diverse coterie--after all, every political mastermind needs investigators, spies, enforcers, and confidants.
Political thrillers are a rich source of engaging political stories, whether set in government offices or among those trying to undermine them. Stories of organized crime inspire exploring loyalty, betrayal, and violence outside the justice system. Soap operas show that lies, alliances, and double-crossing can be gripping even when the stakes are purely personal.
Examples:  Infiltration: The coterie is made to join a
rival faction to gain information or influence decisions, either to accomplish a specific goal or as long-term double agents. The mirror of this story is a mole hunt, where the protagonists root out a suspected infiltrator within their own faction.

 Securing Mortal Influence: The coterie identifies a mortal institution they can bend to their will, be it a police department that can investigate a rival's underworld retainers, a zoning department with the power to demolish opposing havens, or a criminal organization with underworld resources.
 Deposing a Leader: The protagonists try to replace the leader of a faction--possibly their own. Such stories consist of subverting or eliminating the leader's keys to power (p. 158). Deposing a leader can be entirely bloodless, but keeping a deposed leader around is usually a bad way to start your reign.
 Claiming a Vacancy: A domain, title, or similar stake has become vacant, and the protagonists must triumph in the rush to fill the power vacuum. Perhaps there's a ruler who needs to be persuaded, or the coterie has to bribe, threaten, or convince all other claimants to take it.
 Holding Your Spoils: The protagonists have taken control of a stake (such as a domain or a nightclub) and must now fend off both the faction they took it from and everyone else who wants it. There will always be someone coming after what you have.
 Becoming What You Hated: The protagonists despise those in power and know they would do it better. But as they get closer to their goal, they have to keep choosing power over their principles, until finally they must resort to the same methods the old rulers used to maintain their power. Remember to include opportunities for the
players' characters to use non-political skills. After long scenes of intense negotiation, doing a bit of investigation to find your rival's weakness or fighting off an attack on your resources can be a great change of pace and let the players share the spotlight.
Well-Suited For: Maréchal, Plumaire, Regency, Sbirri (Vampire, p. 197)

151

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto
PLAYING WITH TITLES Based on the themes of the chronicle and the goals of the coterie, the characters of one or more players can start the game with a title or acquire one over the course of the chronicle. This is especially typical in a chronicle where the coterie is a part of an influential domain, such as a Regency coterie that rules on behalf of someone else. Different combinations of titles can create highly motivated coteries, such as the Sheriff and her enforcement team, or a young Prince and his title-holding advisors trying to hold on to their newly acquired power.
Keep in mind that titles come with authority and duties that set the holders apart from free political actors. Titles acquired during a chronicle can also put a strain on the players if title-holding characters overshadow the others. Make sure that having titles within the coterie aligns with the kind of chronicle everyone wants to play.
I love her, of course. Victoria, my Blood Bound wife. The ceremony demands it of me, and the Blood makes it true. But we are both too old to have any illusions about what this really is: politics. The Camarilla and the Ashirra are brought closer together through our union. It's a noble goal, and if I'm to suffer emotional discomfort because of it, so be it. I never expected Victoria to spend her nights with me. She's used to her freedoms and loves the changes in the mortal world more than any Kindred of her age I have ever met. She'll spend her nights engaging with the pretense of being a neonate, whether I like it or not. I only wish she'd spend more of her considerable energies fulfilling the true purpose of our union. If the Vermilion Wedding is to be the symbol we need it to be, she should represent her clan and sect proudly by my side. Now, I've often found myself in the strange position of being taken into the innermost confidences of the Camarilla, alone, without her to tell me what these old European Kindred truly wish to see.
 The Banu Haqim Tegyrius idly confessing to a terrified servant

MECHANICS IN POLITICAL STORIES

You have an idea for a political narrative but wonder how to best utilize Vampire's mechanics in telling it? This section provides guidance for Storytellers on using Chronicle Tenets, Convictions, and Touchstones in a political chronicle, and on playing the Beast in political scenes.

affirm their beliefs and suffer the consequences--or play out the tragedy of losing themselves in the pursuit of power.
Fundamentally, Convictions are loopholes that let players and their characters break Chronicle Tenets. The Storyteller should hang their stories on the Tenets and their violation.

Chronicle Tenets
Tenets set the floor for what constitutes monstrous behavior in your chronicle. Politically focused chronicles tend to have fewer opportunities and less screen time for killing, so consider Tenets that can be broken without violence (see some suggestions on p. 36).
If you feel like you're not finding enough opportunities for incurring Stains, remember that 1 Stain can--as an option--be incurred by a breach that is justified by a Conviction, while a deliberately monstrous act can incur 2 or more Stains (Vampire, p. 239). A Conviction may negate 1 Stain from a truly vile act, meaning that 1 is still incurred.
Convictions
Stories about accumulating power are often about losing sight of yourself or becoming what you hated. For the Storyteller, the players' characters' Convictions are invitations to put them in situations where their beliefs are challenged, allowing them to

Touchstones
If you want Touchstones to feature prominently in your political story, you can use them to make the stakes of your domain more dramatic to the players:  A Touchstone lives in an area that is valuable for
feeding, putting their fate at least partly in the hands of whoever is controlling it.  A Touchstone has a powerful Resonance or even a Dyscrasia, making them a sought-after prize for many vampires in a way that actively puts them in harm's way.  A Touchstone works, studies, or volunteers in a valuable but rarely visited domain, like the city's healthcare system or a prison, making the domain extra significant to at least one character and giving you a reason to set scenes there. In any case, the fact that a player's character has a deep emotional connection to a mortal makes them vulnerable. Threatening or harming a Kindred's closest mortals should mark any character out as particularly monstrous.

153

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

Messy Criticals and
Bestial Failures
Political scenes often center on talking and involve fewer tests than, for example, intense action scenes. The outcome of a dramatic negotiation scene can be determined by a single contest between the main negotiators, although you can certainly have a pivotal political scene where the players make Skill tests for sizing up the opposition, making moves, catching implications, and rallying allies.
Every time the Storyteller decides that a test is warranted, both success and failure should take the narrative forward in some way, even as the characters may lose control over the Beast.
If your troupe still finds that the Beast is diverting too much attention from the political intrigue, remember these rules:  A player can Take Half instead of rolling dice to
automatically score a number of successes equal to their pool divided in half, rounded down (Vampire, p. 123).  Instead of interrupting or derailing a scene, a messy critical could result in simple failure as the Beast clouds the character's senses (Vampire, p. 207).  Instead of acting out a Compulsion, a bestial failure can result in the character taking a level of Aggravated Health or Willpower damage or increasing their Hunger by 1 (Vampire, p. 207).  As the Storyteller, you have the option of keeping the consequences of a messy critical or a bestial failure in the fiction without imposing a mechanical penalty. This is especially powerful in political chronicles, where someone thinking less of your character is a real setback. Table 4 presents ideas for messy criticals and bestial failures in scenes where the characters are primarily talking and everyone present is either a vampire or at least a trusted retainer participating in the Masquerade.

Frenzy
Kindred etiquette needs to tolerate the Beast to some extent, much like how the violent tempers of gangsters in crime fiction are shrugged off--at least until they become a liability to the organization.
Below are alternatives for the Fury Frenzy rules presented in Vampire (pp. 219­220) when you don't want a Fury Frenzy to derail your political scene.  Incapacitated: Instead of making a test to resist
Fury Frenzy, a character can choose to leave the scene and vent their rage somewhere else. They're unable to contribute to the scene any further. At the Storyteller's discretion, the character can suffer 1 or more Superficial Willpower damage as they strain to stay in control.  Dragged Out Kicking and Screaming: If the test to resist Fury Frenzy fails, the Storyteller can decide that the frenzying vampire has enough allies to restrain and remove them from the scene, leaving their opponents basking in the schadenfreude. At the Storyteller's discretion, the restraining characters can suffer 1 or more Superficial Health damage that cannot be halved.  Vicious but Brief Scuffle: For a more brutal version, the frenzying vampire gets their claws on the offender right before both sides rush in. The frenzying vampire and the offender inflict Superficial Health damage to each other equal to the successes of a single Strength + Brawl test, taking into account relevant modifiers such as Discipline use and halving as normal. The frenzying vampire is then removed from the scene, and the restraining characters may suffer 1 or more Superficial Health damage that cannot be halved. The same options can also be used to narrate Hunger Frenzy in a political scene, while a Terror Frenzy usually resolves itself as the vampire flees. Remember that failing a test to resist any Frenzy can result in Stains for the character, and Frenzy-fueled violence can warrant additional Stains.

CREATING A POLITICAL DOMAIN

Whether you're using a published chronicle or creating your own domain, abstraction is the Storyteller's friend. This section presents tools to prepare a domain ripe for power struggles and political maneuvering while keeping things manageable at your end.
The Political Map
The Political Map of your domain is a type of Relationship Map you can use to illustrate the central political characters of your domain, their relationships, which faction each character belongs to, and the relationships between the factions (p. 31­35). Don't feel pressured to map out every character in the domain at the beginning of a

chronicle--the entirety of the Political Map should be gradually revealed as the players' characters learn more about the politics of the domain.
If you're struggling to get started, try to come up with a pivotal disagreement between two Kindred central to your domain. The sides of this clash will be the main factions you can elaborate on.
Factions
Factions are efficient tools for both creating and running the politics of a domain. Just two or three factions with competing visions for the domain will create conflict and stakes before a single Storyteller character has been created (see major and minor factions in Chapter One, p. 33). Factions also help

STORYTELLER CHARACTERS AND THE BEAST Make sure to have Storyteller characters struggle with the Beast as well! If your storytelling style involves using Take Half or static difficulty for Storyteller characters, use the players' characters' critical successes or high margins of success as opportunities to have their rivals build up toward a Frenzy or manifest a Compulsion.
You can also let the dice decide, and during a dramatic moment where a Storyteller character's Beast might come out, roll a single Hunger die. On a 1, consider it a bestial failure. On a 10, consider it a messy critical.

156

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

communicate the political landscape to the players as well as the opposing sides the domain's Kindred are pressured to take.
The number, size, and structure of factions depend on the size of the domain. In a small city capable of sustaining a dozen Kindred, coteries are the main factions. The factions of a huge metropolitan area are comprised of coalitions--or coalitions of coalitions--of aligned coteries.
While the factions of your domain might align with sects like the Camarilla and the Anarch Movement, every domain will have factions that struggle for power, even if they nominally fall under the same sect. In addition to sect-aligned factions, any domain can feature independent factions.
To make a faction feel like a political coalition, define some core properties:  Faction Identity: A tagline that quickly
establishes the theme of the faction for you and the players: "the business elite that dominate the Primogen Council" or "a ragtag group of revolutionaries."  Agenda: The wider belief or goal that unites individuals into a faction. "Authority must be wielded by those left rightfully in power by the now-gone elders," or "the Camarilla in Helsinki should be torn down and its members made to face the sunrise."  Modus Operandi: How does the faction go about achieving their vision? Do they use intimidation and deep connections in the local government, or do they recklessly cause considerable physical harm to both their opponents and their resources?  Schisms: What things are contested within the faction? Do the revolutionaries disagree on their methods or on what to do after the revolution? If you ever find that a faction has achieved its agenda, the faction's schisms will inevitably split it into new factions, trying to guide the emerging status quo toward their own visions.

 Key Members: You will need at least one Storyteller character to be the face of the faction for the players. As you create more members, use the agenda, modus operandi, and schisms to show both the unifying and divisive traits of the faction. The above properties are in no way exhaustive.
You might also consider listing the stakes the faction holds, locations where they congregate, and the faction's aesthetic symbols, colors, and coded phrases.
EXAMPLE FACTION: CRIMINAL UPSTARTS Faction Identity: An unruly band of neonates
embedded in the underworld of the city. Agenda: "This domain has too long been ruled by
those who refuse to adapt to modern nights." Modus Operandi: Using criminal organizations
to destroy or incapacitate their rivals' resources, infiltrating mortal organizations with thin-bloods.
Schisms: Should thin-bloods be considered full members of the faction or just exploited because they have no other faction that will have them? Is using Thin-Blood Alchemy or Blood Sorcery a bridge too far? Key Members:  Adam: Gangrel gang leader with a chip on his
shoulder after being passed over for Sheriff  Joy: House of Carna Tremere Court Ritualist
cozying up to the Prince  Strawberry: A thin-blood who the others look up
to for standing against Adam Stakes:  Has taken over the criminal biker gang Flamers  Controls the waterfront for feeding and criminal
enterprise Locations: Closed-down factory, docs Aesthetic: Tattoos, New Age mysticism, open carry firearms, biker jackets
