Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

Political Storyteller Characters
Preparing the needs and flaws of the characters on the Political Map helps you improvise their reactions to the coterie's schemes. To quickly create a political actor for your domain, pick an example Storyteller character from Chapter Six and jot down one of each of the following:  A want, preferably something that advances
their faction's agenda.  A personal weakness, such as something the
character will never do or would risk everything to protect.  A relational weakness, such as an old enemy or a recklessly high boon they owe.  A key to power, such as an Ally, Influence, Resource, Retainer, Status, or stake that keeps the character in power against their competitors. A character's key to power can be another Storyteller character, creating a relationship with political implications.

 A prize that the character is willing to give out as a favor or reward to fulfill their want. If a character will give out a boon, think what that boon might eventually buy. Some of the above can be public secrets about a
character to get the coterie started, but a complete and accurate understanding of a Kindred's wants, weaknesses, and resources should be a valuable and jealously guarded commodity.
EXAMPLE Adam, the Gangrel leader of the Criminal Upstarts, wants to get a title in the domain to shut up anyone who still thinks the Criminal Upstarts are not a serious political threat. Blood Sorcery creeps Adam out, and he will always find another way to do things. He is in a situationship with a Malkavian ancilla but has caught feelings in a way that makes him vulnerable. His keys to power are his control of the Flamers biker gang, his childe Jeffery, who runs arms trafficking in the city, and his willingness to let thin-bloods be part of the faction. To get what he wants, he is ready to cut someone into the gunrunning as a partner, or loan out his gang to solve a one-time problem with force.

158

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

KEEPING TRACK OF A POLITICAL DOMAIN

Creating an intricate political domain can be very rewarding, but Storytellers should be careful how they invest their time. These tools can help track events and characters in a way that allows you to adapt to changes.
Faction Countdowns
A faction countdown is an ordered list of events that will happen if no one tries to stop the faction from acting. It can consist of anything from gathering resources to acting against other factions, consolidating gains, and recovering from setbacks. Two factions can have contradicting events in their countdowns to represent conflicts that hang in the balance. You can also use countdown events to reveal something about the goals, resources, or weaknesses of the faction, such as a faction's two key members having a public falling-out.
If you keep track of dates in your chronicle, you can put faction countdown events into a calendar and resolve them as they come up. The other way is to keep the countdowns as mostly sequential lists, where you can cross them out as they happen or when characters are catching up with the latest gossip. If politics is happening in the background of your chronicle, this more abstract way of using countdowns is a great way to update the political landscape in the downtime between stories.

Plan Trackers When a player's character sets out to reach a political objective that will take some time to achieve, you can start a plan tracker for that character that shows the number of activities that need to be completed to reach the goal, represented as boxes. Five boxes are a good default, but you can vary things by using more or fewer boxes for bigger or smaller plans, marking off two boxes for critical successes, or removing a mark as a consequence for a bestial failure.
EXAMPLE: The Malkavian Anastasia wants her faction's leader to support her claim to the title of Herald. You reason that there are many things Anastasia could conceivably do to secure the leader's support, but it won't be a simple one-off thing, so you create a five-box tracker and write "The leader supports Anastasia for Herald" at the end. Every time Anastasia or one of her allies discovers a weakness they can leverage, she acquires a resource that can be used to buy off the leader, or similar, you mark off one box.
Remember that a box tracker is only a tool for Storytellers to keep track of how much effort has been put into furthering the plan. If the shared fiction compels the project to come to an abrupt success or failure, let that happen.

159

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

Tracking Storyteller Characters
Political chronicles can involve large casts of Storyteller characters who stay relevant from story to story as the coterie remains in the same domain. Remembering and switching between characters can be greatly enhanced by maintaining an index or an electronic spreadsheet with each character's name, portrait, needs, flaws, dice pools, and relevant history with the players' characters.

If you want to maximize your adaptability, you can even create spare Storyteller characters with names, personalities, portraits, and dice pools but no predetermined faction or relationships. When there's a sudden need for a political Storyteller character to enter a scene, you can slot one of your spare characters into a faction and introduce them without skipping a beat.

160

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

MORTAL POLITICS

Come now. Let us cease this pretense and talk candidly about the second game. The hot game. The game of power in the world of light and life. The mortal world.
Mortals can provide everything Kindred need. Money. Cover identities. Havens. Blood. Potential new Embraces. All are mortal resources, and to control them, Kindred must sometimes play the dangerous "hot game" of mortal politics. Almost no Kindred manipulator is ready for the cost and the consequences of dabbling in the fast-moving and unpredictable mess that is mortal politics in the 21st century.
Forget what you have been told about the Kindred controlling everything. Not even at the height of their power during the Victorian Era did Camarilla Princes have more than a handful of representatives in the House of Lords, and perhaps enough dirt on the royals to manipulate a prince consort or two. Control over the army or the navy? Not a chance. Influence over specific events and outcomes is the very best the Kindred can hope for if they give it their all. Especially in this age of Inquisition, the damned must carefully consider every attempt at manipulation a risk that may end in final death, exposure of their childer, or worse. Still, sometimes it must be done, because mortals, indeed, are the most important resource to the leeches.
Below are some of the ways Kindred use mortals for their own political goals and how you can reflect the risk in the game mechanics.

Politics by Proxy
Kindred who want to affect a mortal political decision must work through a proxy. Downing Street employees, members of Congress, or members of the Duma do not generally take meetings after dark, and deals over drinks between party officials in D.C. cocktail bars only go so far, no matter the size of the campaign donation. Making any kind of political test through a Retainer or hired front is harder than doing so in person. Such tests are at a +1 to +2 Difficulty, and a failure means the effort is wasted or that the proxy betrays the Kindred manipulator. A bestial or total failure (no successes) or just two failures in a row means investigation by the authorities, or even a visit from SO13, SAD, BOES, or a similar agency.
Defensive Moves
In the 21st century, most vampires rely on old contacts, proven allies, and associates who act without being prodded or poked at all. But sometimes, when the agency alphabet soup is boiling over in a domain, inaction is more dangerous than trying to redirect or discredit the opposition.
The rising trend towards authoritarianism and distrust in "the deep state" has made attacks against even deeply rooted intelligence organizations possible once again; however, the Kindred can only do so much. Instead of direct action, it is often through "oversight programs" and "fiscal responsibility" initiatives that the dead manage to defund, weaken, and misdirect assets like healthcare

161

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

and international intelligence corporations that threaten their existence.
In a city-sized domain, defanging the local SI by cutting their budgets, firing long-time employees, and casting doubt on the veracity of their data is not something to be settled with a test; it is the subject of a multi-session story, often going up against experts with 6/4 Difficulties, working in teams. A single failed opposed roll means armed officers at your Haven.
Undermining Official Authorities
Most of the time, trying to affect mortal politics is too dangerous. Instead, many smart bloodsuckers try to influence institutions far away from City Hall or Capitol Hill. Prime among these is the world of organized crime. This is familiar territory to the undead; most crime networks operate along eerily similar lines as Camarilla Princes and Primogen. Some even argue that the Cosa Nostra is, in whole or in part, a mortal version of the Ivory Tower, with the Traditions being almost identical to the famous mobster code that emphasizes omertà and respect for its powerful members. For one thing, it is much easier to deal with criminal networks at night, eliminating the need for a proxy. Secondly, many illegal syndicates respect a copious capacity for applied violence, something Kindred excel at. On the flip side, such violence is often met with instant and total retribution. It matters little whether your opponent knows you are a monster if their preferred method of war is firebombing every single address that they can connect to you or your hirelings.
In general, allow tests at normal Difficulty when interacting with or influencing mobsters. But should a roll be a total failure, make sure violent retribution is the immediate consequence, costing the Kindred a variable amount of dots of Advantages (often Resources, Haven, Contacts, Allies, or even a Touchstone) fitting the scope of the attempted manipulation. This is known as blowback.

Blowback
Suggested blowback costs in the number of Advantage dots:
· Attempting to direct an attack against another illegal or Kindred target.
·· Attempting to use a criminal organization to buy out or destroy a legal company or minor institution.
··· Attempting to affect local city politics or establish total control over a major local criminal gang.
···· Making a criminal organization the target of a Second Inquisition agency instead of your own network; trying to affect the national response to a health crisis, for instance, by downgrading a total curfew to a mandate to maintain social distance.
This blowback is most immediate and felt when failing a test when interacting with criminal organizations, but the same system can be used to determine the cost of a bestial or total failure when playing the "hot game" with national institutions or directly with Second Inquisition organizations.
MORTAL POLITICS IN PLAY A city's mortal political climate inevitably bleeds into Kindred reality as well. A city with deep inequality breeds unrest, corruption, and crime, making it easier for the Kindred to exist unnoticed. When the military gets more funding, Second Inquisition agencies are more likely to come after you with brand-new tech. An activist group might target a vampire-owned corporation because of what it trades in. A controversial urban redevelopment can spark feuds over domain territory. Mortal politics are not just something the Kindred use and hide from but something that sometimes targets them even without having a single clue as to their true nature.

162

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

HORROR IN POLITICS

Politically active characters need a lot of information and opportunities to affect what takes place in their city, but too much influence can give them near-supernatural political power. This section explores how to keep the horror aspects of Vampire intact in political play.
Information Asymmetry
Don't let players get a complete, reliable understanding of the city. Hinting at unseen events and occasionally blindsiding them with new twists keeps paranoia alive. Having a few central Storyteller characters act under false pretenses or betray the coterie once their goals are met reinforces that everyone is out for themselves. That said, be wary of overusing deception. If every lead turns out to be false and every deal is broken, your game will grind to a halt as players lose their sense of agency.
Powerlessness
Make the stakes of other vampires' schemes feel immediate and severe for the players' characters. Petty vendettas between courtiers escalate into brutal displays of dominance, loved ones vanish across domain lines, and feeding rights are revoked on a whim to reward a ruler's lackey.
Unlike the fear of the unknown, the horror of powerlessness can come from knowing exactly what's about to happen. Faction countdowns (p. 159) can foreshadow disasters for the players' characters while also reinforcing that their

misfortunes result from the politics of the domain, not the Storyteller's whims.
And if the players' characters do find themselves with considerable influence or manage to pit stronger foes against one another? Then they should truly feel afraid, because nothing unites rivals faster than a shared threat.
The Cost of Power
Make gaining power come at a terrible price. Offer the players' characters tempting advantages that require compromising their Convictions or the Chronicle Tenets, or let them think they got away scot-free, only to discover they made a trade they weren't prepared for.
Once they're successful, let them feel the burden of keeping what they've taken. Those at the top rule because they hold the loyalty of their keys to power--those who could topple the ruler if they chose. A shrewd ruler will replace any key whose loyalty is questioned and eliminate anyone whose loyalty is no longer needed. And the keys themselves, who sit in the ruler's inner circle? They have their own keys to power, which they must manage, and their own rivals who would gladly take their place.
Power then becomes a trap. Those with less envy what the characters have: their peers fear being left behind, and their superiors become paranoid that they'll be replaced. No matter how high they climb, they'll always feel someone reaching for their throats.

163

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

THE WORLD OF VAMPIRE

The setting of Vampire: The Masquerade has a wealth of exciting, horrific, and dramatic events that can be used as background material for a chronicle. Here, our goal is to demonstrate how to make Vampire's history playable so it's relevant for your characters.
The principal game-mechanical tool for integrating lore into character is the Loresheet.
Using Loresheets
For players, picking levels in a Loresheet signals interest in a specific event, location, character, or other aspect of the world. When starting a chronicle, it's worth considering which Loresheets (and lore topics in general) best match its theme and tone. In a chronicle developed from the bottom up by the troupe together, this is easily done in pre-game discussion, keeping everyone aligned. At the other end of the spectrum, the Storyteller might present chronicle concepts to the players, complete with a list of approved or restricted Loresheets. Some Storytellers might find it natural for characters to start without Loresheets and unlock relevant ones in play.
Playable Lore
The following examples show ways Storytellers can make lore relevant to players and their characters. It focuses on recent Kindred events. For more, In Memoriam provides details for Kindred events going further back, as well as expanded rules for Memoriam and advice for using that mechanic to flash back to past eras.

Collapse of
the Tremere Pyramid
Loresheets: Descendant of Karl Schrekt (Vampire, p. 405), Descendant of Meerlinda (p. 220), Lost Secrets of the Milwaukee Chantry (Let the Streets Run Red, p. 288), Machinations of Saulot (Book of Nod Apocrypha, p. 39), Occult Artifacts (Chicago Folios, p. 166), Praepositor (Forbidden Religions, p. 92), The Pyramid (Camarilla, p. 189), Revenant Family: Ducheski (Chicago by Night, p. 283), Vienna Zero (Blood Sigils, p. 180)
In 2008, Second Inquisition agencies assaulted the Tremere Prime Chantry in Vienna, leaving behind rubble and confused accounts of a terrorist strike (see Live from the Succubus Club, p. 130). Some suspect the Tremere were sold out by other Kindred. The strike destroyed the clan's leadership or sent them into hiding, leaving an opening for longpersecuted splinter factions.
Since their inception, the Tremere have been a highly structured and hierarchical clan. Loyalty was maintained through the enforcement of Blood Bonds to a degree unknown in other clans. The

164

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

2008 strike shook the power of the Tremere Pyramid forcefully, giving rise to competing power centers and thaumaturgical traditions such as Houses Carna and Goratrix. The loyalist followers of House Tremere found themselves just one faction among many.
As a result, there are many opportunities for ambitious Tremere, such as the players' characters.
Story Concepts
 Reclaim Lost Sorceries: As Tremere elders went into hiding, many took their most important occult books with them. Because of this, the remaining ancillae and neonates must rediscover rituals scattered in old havens and abandoned chantries.
 Unify the Clan: In the opinion of many loyalist Tremere, the hierarchy worked and should be rebuilt, with dissenters forced back into the fold. Similarly, members of other Tremere Houses,

such as House Carna, see themselves as the true inheritors of the legacy and envision a grand unified clan under their own leadership.  Undermine or Destroy Other Sorcerers: The Tremere used to be the Kindred sorcerers, despite the Banu Haqim also possessing sorcerous powers. A few bloodlines of Tzimisce and Ministry were also rumored to have sorcerous potential. After the fall of the Pyramid, all these competing sorcerers have crawled out of the woodwork, and in some domains, there are entire communities of redworkers. All these rivals must be eliminated for the Tremere to regain prominence.  Study Your Own Way: Whether by choice or necessity, many Tremere are on their own: fighting off rival sorcerers from other clans and trying to do it all. Can you make it by yourself, or is it time to Embrace or recruit other Tremere to help?

LEARNING BLOOD SORCERY AND OBLIVION Learning Blood Sorcery is more difficult than other Disciplines, but it's not impossible to do even without Tremere or Banu Haqim tutelage. Ruleswise, you merely need to drink the vitae of another Kindred who knows the Discipline. And while some Kindred may spontaneously develop other Disciplines based on their feeding habits (Predator Types), Blood Sorcery doesn't work this way. This assumption leads to the underground redworking scene described in Blood Sigils.
If you want to make learning Blood Sorcery more difficult in a chronicle, add the requirement that anyone without Blood Sorcery as a Clan Discipline must be taught Blood Sorcery by a member of the Tremere or Banu Haqim, and Oblivion cannot be gained from a Predator Type except by Hecata and Lasombra. If it helps make a more interesting chronicle, learning Blood Sorcery or Oblivion could also be possible through certain occult books in addition to explicit instruction. Limiting the learning of these Disciplines restricts the occult scene.
To find an intermediate solution, some additional clans that historically possessed Blood Sorcery may still be able to teach it. In such a world, the Tzimisce, Ministry, and certain bloodlines of the Gangrel may possess the Protean Sorcerer Merit, and some members of the Ministry possess Oblivion's Corruption. These add rivals to the occult scene. Optional Merits
· Protean Sorcerer (Prerequisite: Gangrel, Ministry, or Tzimisce): You can learn and teach Blood Sorcery as though it were a Clan Discipline. This replaces Protean as a Clan Discipline for you.
· Oblivion's Corruption (Prerequisite: Ministry): You can learn and teach Oblivion as though it were a Clan Discipline. This replaces Protean as a Clan Discipline for you. You cannot have both Oblivion's Corruption and Protean Sorcerer.

166

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Common Attitudes: "We have other redworkers now. We don't need the Tremere anymore."
Rules and Narrative Elements: Learning Rituals and Blood Sorcery, feuding factions within Clan Tremere.
Discoveries: "The elders in Clan Tremere never told me that the Church of Set could teach sorcery..."
The Conclave of Chicago
Loresheets: Archons (Chicago Folios, p. 158), The Convention of Chicago (Chicago Folios, p. 159), Cult of Shalim (Chicago by Night, p. 272), Descendant of Marconius (p. 219), Descendant of Montano (Chicago by Night, p. 274), High Clan (Vampire, p. 400), Justicar Lucinde (Chicago Folios, p. 162), Kevin Jackson (Chicago by Night, p. 277), Talley (Chicago by Night, p. 286), Sheriff Damien (Chicago Folios, p. 168)
In the early 21st century, after the assassination of Hardestadt the Elder, the Fall of London, and the Anarch insurrection in Berlin, Camarilla luminaries declared the Conclave of Chicago. The convention hosted luminaries from around the globe. Debates ranged from the growing thin-blood population to how best to combat the Second Inquisition.
The central item on the agenda was Lasombra admittance into the Camarilla. Following Prince Kevin Jackson and Sierra's example, incoming Lasombra would pay a heavy price, such as handing over an elder clanmate, for acceptance. Individual Princes have created their own admission rules, but the Chicago Conclave established the precedent.

With the Second Inquisition and a violent Anarch Movement threatening the Ivory Tower, Lasombra inclusion couldn't have come at a better time.
The Conclave's decisions ripple throughout Camarilla cities, forcing characters to confront new political realities.
Story Concepts
 Off the Books: While many scheduled conversations and committees filled the official agenda, informal discussions took place far from prying eyes. With numerous Princes, Primogen, and Kindred of note in one city, the Chicago Conclave provided perfect cover for backroom deals. Now, some may need to deliver on agreements they never knew their sires made.
 A Lasombra Justicar: Talley and Sierra paved the way for Clan Lasombra's inclusion as Camarilla members, but that was only the start. The Amici Noctis blackmail, bribe, and influence Princes from around the globe, all in the hopes of one night claiming a formal, official Lasombra Justicar. And they are always looking for Kindred who can get a job done.
 Generational Divide: The original Lasombra converts paid a heavy price for Camarilla membership. They were often asked to destroy a single vampire of their own Blood to become members of the Camarilla. Those Embraced after the Conclave have avoided this terrible cost, with some hard-line Lasombra tormenting these youths who have had it too easy.
 Word and Will: When a Justicar or Archon empowers a Kindred to complete a task, they operate from perceived political strength. Who would deny someone working on behalf of a Justicar? Princes, however, despise being told what to do, especially within their own domain. Such an empowered Kindred may feel emboldened by their charge, but they may also find themselves with resentful rivals. Common Attitudes: "You want sway? Then you
have to be in the big rooms when the big things are

167

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

decided. Otherwise, you're just eavesdropping from behind the door."
Rules and Narrative Elements: Camarilla politics on a grand scale, using the famous event to earn reputation and climb the social ladder.
Discoveries: "Last year we were all Sabbat. Tonight, we're all Camarilla. The Justicars declared it so. But if that's the case, then why does every vampire I see still look like they want to drive a stake through my chest and toss me out for the sun?"
Fracturing of the Banu Haqim
Loresheets: Descendant of Al-Ashrad (Blood Sigils, p. 177), Descendant of Idder (Live from the Succubus Club, p. 158), Fatima al-Faqadi (Camarilla, p. 185), High Clan (Vampire, p. 400), Occult Artifacts (Chicago Folios, p. 166), Tegyrius the Vizier (Gehenna War, p. 159), Victoria Ash (Camarilla, p. 190) The culminating event that split the Banu Haqim in two was the return of the blood god, Ur-Shulgi. The ancient methuselah took command of their ancestral stronghold Alamut and demanded that all Banu Haqim abandon their mortal faiths or face destruction. With little recourse, Tegyrius the Vizier and Al-Ashrad the Amr led a faction out of Alamut and into the Camarilla's protective walls while deepening the alliance between Camarilla and Ashirra domains.
In the intervening years, the feud between schismatic and loyalist has only widened and, in most

cases, grown more violent. Even the fall of Alamut to the Sabbat has not mended the fracture. To their credit, Tegyrius and Al-Ashrad have solidified their alliances with the great sects through the Vermilion Wedding, but Ur-Shulgi looms ever closer.
Caught between loyalty and personal belief, every Banu Haqim has an impossible choice to make.
Story Concepts
 Dealing with Prejudice: For centuries, the Banu Haqim hunted Kindred. Some for sport, others on contract, but their reputation as proficient killers remains intact. For some Camarilla members with long memories, the presence of the Banu Haqim is akin to letting a fox inside the henhouse. Proving loyalty often requires increasingly dangerous missions while suspicious Kindred wait for the slightest misstep to justify their paranoia.
 On the Offensive: While the most Banu Haqim solidify their position as Camarilla members, a select few, tasked by Tegyrius, go on the offensive. Their command is to hunt down those loyal to Ur-Shulgi and destroy them with prejudice.
 The Envoy: Most encounters between schismatic and loyalist Banu Haqim end in violence. But an ambassador from Ur-Shulgi, sent into a Camarilla city, presents a unique opportunity. The envoy intends to bring Camarilla Banu Haqim back into the fold, using a strict interpretation of the Laws of Haqim as a pretense. Even if the envoy's efforts prove fruitless, if they survive the initial encounter, they may gather intelligence on their former brethren.
 Love-Struck Schismatic: The Vermilion Wedding between Tegyrius and Victoria Ash represents a formal alliance between their powerful sects. But in the subsequent years, Tegyrius' love for his Toreador wife has proven genuine, or else he has chosen to accept the reality of their mutual Blood Bond. This gives many Banu Haqim pause, as they worry the great Vizier places his personal feelings above the political needs of his clan. No

168

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

doubt this is a weakness to exploit. Common Attitudes: "Insane methuselah pops up and says, `Follow me or die.' I understand the problem. Part of me might want to go along to get along. Another part of me might want to run for my fucking unlife." Rules and Narrative Elements: The war between Banu Haqim within Camarilla domains and those loyal to Ur-Shulgi, a dueling interpretation of the Laws of Haqim. Discoveries: "This isn't some pesky political debate. We're past all that, past compromise and understanding. This is a civil war, a brother's war. And this is a fight to the death."
Reemergence of the Salubri
Loresheets: Blood Plagued (Anarch, p. 197), The Book of Nod (Chicago by Night, p. 267 or Book of
Nod Apocrypha, p. 37), Descendant of the Fallen Lord (Live from the Succubus Club, p. 161), Golconda (Vampire, p. 389), Low Clan (Vampire, p. 401), Machinations of Saulot (Book of Nod Apocrypha, p. 39), Sect War Veteran (Vampire, p. 392)
A fallen clan, the Salubri were never numerous, even in their heyday over a thousand years ago. Among them, many bloodlines maintained they were pacifists, advisors, and tenders of mortal flocks. They kept mortal herds healthy and helped Kindred ward off the Beast. Among other Kindred, they were mistrusted politicians, knights, and soul-eaters. Legends suggest that the Tremere usurped their place among the 13 great clans. Court Ritualists were more useful than shepherds.
For centuries, the Salubri were a forgotten myth, epitomized by the legend of the Seven. In Eastern

Europe, the Levant, and North Africa, seven Salubri were guaranteed protection from the Princes of the Ashirra. Throughout Western Europe, the Seven were an odd bloodline devoted to the crime of diablerie. Rumors of hidden broods occasionally emerged among the Tremere, who taught their childer to hate and hunt these spiritually debased monsters.
This changed as the Fallen Lord emerged within the Sabbat in the '80s or '90s and spread his bloodline across Europe and the Americas. In the wake of the sect wars, errant Salubri have begun to make their way into Kindred society, with others emerging from centuries of hiding to connect with their new kin.
Salubri stories often focus on forging a place for oneself, discovering the history of their bloodlines, and eking out an existence from the margins.
Story Concepts
 Sabbat Refugee: Outcasts among outcasts, a Sabbat survivor asks for haven within the domain, but no one has heard of their supposed bloodline. Camarilla and traditional domains may turn them aside or demand a heavy price for tolerance within the domain. Anarchs or less traditional domains may force the newcomer to turn on their brethren, leading a scouting party to find and eliminate any Sabbat threats.
 The Knight and the Sorcerer: A traveling pair of Kindred visit the city, seeking fragments of history from Kindred loremasters, Hierophants, and elders who can speak of an old clan known as the Salubri. As one claims Tremere heritage, the local Tremere see this Tremere-Salubri pairing as breaking an ancient taboo, but they may not know why their books and elders advised hunting the Salubri and suppressing any knowledge of them.
 Claiming Station: An outsider who has been tolerated has begun calling in boons from among the city's authorities. Many have thought this individual a Caitiff, but they're known for helping to pacify the kine of Kindred herds and

169

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

blood dolls. This outsider clearly wants to gain the authorities' ear--but to what end?  Itinerant Healer: The domain has been troubled lately, perhaps by a blood plague or neonates losing themselves to the Beast. An outcast from a neighboring domain claims they can help, but how did they learn techniques to deal with these issues? What strange faith drives them, and how will the authorities react to this new, charismatic savior in their domain? Common Attitudes: "What the fuck is a Salubri?" Rules and Narrative Elements: Presenting oneself and one's clan to the Prince or local authorities, navigating as an outsider, seeking out knowledge of one's forebears. Discoveries: "I've never heard of a Salubri, but I've heard Chicago is still lousy with elders, and one of them must know something."
Rise of the Second
Inquisition
Loresheets: Blacksite 24 (Chicago by Night, pp. 264­265), The First Inquisition (Vampire, p. 388), FIRSTLIGHT (Chicago by Night, p. 276), Operation Antigen (Fall of London, p. 237), The Society of St. Leopold (Chicago by Night, pp. 284­ 285), The Pony Express (Chicago Folios, p. 167), Road Courier (Live from the Succubus Club, p. 164)
When the NSA penetrated the Nosferatu SchreckNET in 2004, the first domino fell. The United States military, the Society of St. Leopold, global intelligence agencies, and dozens of other mortal organizations started working together to combat the vampire threat. Over the next decade, Kindred found themselves under attack and subsequently cleansed. Vienna, Montreal, Marseille, Las Vegas, London--one by one, they were toppled by the Second Inquisition.
After the initial shock and awe, Kindred adapted. Cell phones and other electronic devices vanished while analog forms of communication returned. Gatherings became smaller and less frequent. In this

conflict, younger, more technologically savvy Kindred stepped up and carved out niches for themselves in an ongoing war against an insurmountable foe.
Facing annihilation, players' characters act as spies, saboteurs, and in rare cases, informants.
Story Concepts
 Spy Versus Spy: Just as the Second Inquisition monitors vampires, intrepid neonates actively surveil the Second Inquisition. Using human assets and signals intelligence, they shadow FIRSTLIGHT hunters, break into encrypted ESOG servers, and assassinate Gladius Dei operatives. Surveillance networks and recruiting mortal informants are valuable for counterintelligence work.
 Turned Traitor: The best soldiers against the Second Inquisition are those plucked from its ranks. A disillusioned priest from the Society of St. Leopold or a seduced (and perhaps later Embraced) member of the UK's Joint Threat Response Group are turned assets that can tear the Coalition apart from the inside.
 Soon You'll Be Eating Rats: Some Kindred assume the conflict with the Second Inquisition is unwinnable, and if vampires are going to make it through the next century, they need to explore unconventional avenues. What if vampires' existence didn't require mortal bloodshed? If Blood Sorcery or Thin-Blood

170

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Alchemy could make animal vitae palatable to all Kindred, regardless of age, the Second Inquisition would have less reason to consider vampires a threat. Can the Kindred make themselves so unthreatening that their enemies leave them in peace?  Turning of the Screw: Hunter organizations occasionally turn their intended prey into assets. The hunters spare the vampire's existence for a price. The catch-and-release tactic transforms the intended pile of ash into an informant, sent back into the wild to gather intelligence. Some vampires find themselves in this position, feeding scraps to handlers to survive. And every night brings the same choice: sell out someone else to survive, or refuse and watch the hunters come for everyone they've ever cared about. Common Attitudes: "Is that a goddamn cell phone in your hand? Are you trying to get us all killed?" Rules and Narrative Elements: Adapting to a dangerous threat, navigating the digital age without being destroyed. Discoveries: "We can't sit and wait for the SI to burn down our homes. We have to infiltrate their organizations and use their own tactics against them. That's the only way we survive."
The Setite Schism
Loresheets: The Church of Set (Anarch, p. 193), Descendant of Count Jocalo (p. 218), High Clan (Vampire, p. 400), Hesha Ruhadze (Anarch, p. 192)
With dangers from both the Second Inquisition and the Sabbat, the Ministry needed allies. Clan elders first made overtures to elders of the Camarilla, but those ended in a hotel bombing in the middle of Paris. Their second attempt to find a home in the Anarch Movement proved more fruitful. Now, the majority of the Snakes operate openly within Anarch domains.
Over the centuries, many outsiders have assumed that the Ministry and the Church of Set are one and the same, but that's not exactly true. The Church is a Kindred faith akin to the Church of Caine and

the Bahari, while the Ministry is a clan, a lineage of vampires. Historically, most members of the Church of Set have been from the Ministry (with two distinct influxes of Hecata refugees), but in the modern nights, this is less true than ever before.
As young members of the Ministry are Embraced into the Anarchs, many ignore their clan's own tradition of faith. At the same time, the Church of Set finds new adherents from other clans.
This schism between faith, lineage, and politics opens doors for those willing to exploit it.
Story Concepts
 A Murder in Paris: When the Ministry's elders met with Camarilla diplomats in Paris, the gathering ended in an explosion that destroyed both delegations, effectively ending any hope of an alliance. The common belief is that the Banu Haqim are to blame, having sabotaged the endeavor to ensure their own admission. But is that the truth? And does anyone care to find out?
 The Enemy of My Enemy: The animosity between Anarch Ministry and the Church of Set runs so deep that the Church is making overtures to Camarilla Princes. Justicar Lucinde despises the Ministry, but the Orthodox Setites would rather work with the Ivory Tower than cede any more ground to their former brethren. The Anarchs in the Ministry would have an incentive

171

Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto

to infiltrate the talks, while Church loyalists could be willing to secure the alliance at any cost.  Religious Expression: To worship the Red God is to place personal freedom over allegiance to any organized political faction. This often places members of the Ministry at odds with their fellow Anarchs if they remain loyal to the teachings of the Church of Set. When an Anarch leader orders a Ministry member to submit to the collective interests of the Movement, the faithful must choose between Set's chaos and Anarch loyalty.  Make Way for the End of the World: The Red God is a force of chaos, and some Snakes, both among Ministry Anarchs and the Church, believe Set is not only awake but active. The thought is hard to deny when they point out the current state of the world with droughts, hurricanes, raging fires, economic catastrophes, political unrest, and military conflicts both large and small. Consider this: what if the Ministry's acceptance into the Movement is a ruse, paving the way for the Red God's inevitable return? Such theories do no favors for a clan whose members are already often believed to be traitors. Common Attitudes: "Ministry, Orthodox Setites, Snakes, Church of Set... I honestly can't tell the difference." Rules and Narrative Elements: The feud between the Ministry and the Church of Set, the conflict between one's political beliefs and faith. Discoveries: "Maybe instead of accepting some Hierophant's interpretation of Set, you need to dig deep and determine your own relationship with the Red God."
The Third Anarch Revolt
Loresheets: Agata Starek (Anarch, p. 191), Anarch Revolt (Anarch, p. 198), Birth of the Anarch Free States (In Memoriam, p. 149), Descendant of Tyler (Vampire, p. 397), Descendant of Xaviar (Vampire, p. 406), Hesha Ruhadze (Anarch, p. 192), Juggler (Let the Streets Run Red, p. 227), Rudi (Vampire, p. 396), Ruins of Carthage (Anarch, p. 196),

Salvador Garcia (Anarch, p. 190), Theo Bell (Vampire, p. 383)
The moment Theo Bell assassinated Hardestadt the Elder at the Conclave of Prague in 2012, he ignited the Third Anarch Revolt. The Brujah quickly abandoned the Camarilla and followed Bell into the loving arms of the Anarch Movement. During this same time, as the Gangrel left the Camarilla, many individual members of the clan also became Anarchs, as did many in the Ministry. United under a common cause, Anarchs began flipping Camarilla cities, including the age-old European stronghold of Berlin.
Now, the Third Anarch Revolt has been going on long enough for the fractures to show even as new Camarilla domains still fall. The great victory in Berlin led to long nights of terror and fear as Second Inquisition agencies moved in, attracted by the violence. Various Anarch ideologies clash, masking older conflicts over territory and resources. The wider Anarch Movement still has its leaders and luminaries, but on the local level, the sect often consists of local gangs with their small domains.
Some believe in the idealistic vision of Salvador Garcia; others lionize the nihilistic Agata Starek. Many who defected from the Camarilla, particularly Brujah, look to Theo Bell for leadership.
The Anarch Movement is on the cusp of something new, providing a wonderful opportunity for anyone willing to seize it.

172

Story Concepts
 Power of Personality: A fledgling Anarch cannot stand alone. Their best means of survival involves gravitating toward one of the many personalities that influence the Movement, such as Rudi, Garcia, Starek, Ruhadze, Juggler, and Bell. But whom does one follow? Each demands different prices for protection and offers different visions of the revolution's future.
 Chronicles of the Movement: A Kindred historian has studied the Brujah's history in Carthage, the First and Second Anarch Revolts, and the birth of the Anarch Free States. They travel from city to city, offering their knowledge and expertise to those interested. Education is, after all, the first step toward enlightenment.
 Breaking the Tower: With Agata Starek and her ilk as prime example, the Anarch revolutionaries wage overt war against the Camarilla. In their eyes, the Inner Circle and their Justicar dogs are tyrants, and compromise is a dirty word. If the only good Prince is a destroyed Prince, who's the first to go?
 Not Every Brujah Wants to Break Stuff: A rare few Brujah still hold membership within Camarilla cities and find themselves in a curious position. Their Princes suspect them of treachery while their clanmates brand them as traitors. These Camarilla Brujah work especially hard to prove their worth, often acting as Sheriffs for a city or as undercover spies within the Movement. Common Attitudes: "No one took the
Movement seriously until Bell killed Hardestadt. Now they've got the Brujah and the Gangrel and the Ministry, and they're taking cities left and right. That's a problem we need to deal with."
Rules and Narrative Elements: Political among Anarch factions, freedom for the individual versus freedom for the organization as a whole.
Discoveries: "The truth is that the Anarch Movement is just a bunch of gangs. Right now, they're duking it out, but eventually, one of them will get the upper hand. When that happens, God help you if you're wearing the wrong color."

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Chapter Six:
CHARACTERS AND STORIES
The guillotine... A weapon from a more civilized age. I think I saw that in a film once. Well, old friend, you thought that the age of the guillotine was long past. No more rebellions, no more agitators clamoring for the blood of the privileged. You'd survived the French, American, and Russian revolutions and could exist in peace, sucking the blood of the living and every vampire younger than you. Well, guess what? I had this guillotine specially made just for you. Regarding the Camarilla, there is one thing I agree with you about: sometimes the old ways are the best.
 The Anarch ideologue Salvador Garcia addresses a skull
Meant primarily for the Storyteller running a political chronicle, this chapter presents characters and stories you can use as they are or modify to fit your needs. Politics is about people and their conflicting desires, so you need to populate your setting with characters in adversarial roles. Once you know who wants what, it's easier to come up with stories of your own.
The stories in this chapter also serve another purpose: to demonstrate what political stories can look like. You can use them as inspiration for how to add political themes to an existing chronicle in addition to running them as they are.
175

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

CHARACTERS

Use the following characters as a quick means to come up with Traits for Kindred political figures, rivals, leaders, and adversaries. Storytellers may later augment these or craft complete character sheets for major characters the players' coterie encounters, but these can be used to easily add some additional variety to those found on pp. 374­75 in Vampire: The Masquerade.
A NOTE ON STATUS Status doesn't have to be uniform across titles. A Prince may have five dots of Status if their hold on their domain is secure and relatively unquestioned, or only four if they're threatened, ineffectual, or reclusive. Remember, though, that Status matters. Especially in Camarilla domains, dealing directly with anyone whose Status is significantly lower than your own is often frowned upon. Even the nominally more egalitarian Anarchs take Status seriously, sometimes as a result of seniority and trust, sometimes because they've simply constructed their own hierarchies of power.  Prince: 4­5 dots  Herald: 4 dots  Primogen: 4­5 dots  Keeper of Elysium: 3­4 dots  Sheriff: 3­4 dots  Coterie Leader: 0­1 dot  Primary Anarch Leaders: 3­5 dots  Secondary Anarch Leaders: 2­4 dots  Kindred Cult Priests: 2­4 dots  Kindred Cult Defenders: 0­1 dot  Circulatory System Higher-Up: 2­3 dots  Road Courier: 0­1 dot  Club Operator: 1­3 dots · Court Ritualist: 1­2 dots

Camarilla
Hands-On Prince
The average lick on the street is unlikely to have much cause to interface directly with the Prince of their domain. A Prince's influence will likely be felt chiefly through their agents and intermediaries. Still, sometimes a Prince needs to get their hands dirty. Some are more willing to do this than others.
Some Princes are known for their active approach to the management of their domain. They may be obsessive megalomaniacs, bent on observing and controlling every aspect of the territory over which they preside. Or they may be pragmatists, believing that the survival of the Kindred in these modern nights demands an involved and punitive leadership. A rare few may even be idealists, seeking to wield their power to ends that few can guess at. Regardless, this kind of Prince is not one you want to get in the way of.
The challenge facing the Prince who continually intervenes in the workings of their domain is to be feared but not hated. It's in their interest to be seen as harsh but ultimately fair.
Name: Viggo, Simon, Malin, Inga, Talha, Suhaib, Zulfa, Ghaaliya, Fedner, Lucien, Chantale, Yolande, Gabilus, Dérice, Somphone, Kimo, Nalani, Geethan, Asith, Jayani, Nadeesha, Henryk, Witold, Monika, Wislawa

Equipment: Disguised weapon (sword cane, razor pocket watch, or similar), access to the best vices in the city, multiple Havens ranging from decadent and obvious to secretive and well-guarded
Prince with a Light Touch
This type of Prince is less of an overt presence weighing on the local Kindred and more of a name on the wind. They may act to influence, inspire, or curtail, but typically in indirect ways, and only when absolutely necessary. The reasons for their apparent inactivity vary. One may have been deliberately selected in a bit of political maneuvering by the Primogen Council because of their relative inexperience or unambitious nature. Another is an enthusiastic student of the teachings of a thinker such as Laozi, believing that letting go of control is the path to maintaining a harmonious system of rule. Or perhaps they are too absorbed in devising some gloriously elaborate, decades-spanning work of art to keep a close eye on their domain.
Regardless, while subtlety has its advantages, the Prince who prefers inaction is in danger of being perceived as weak or ineffective. Their withdrawn nature may even allow fresh and novel horrors to blossom in their domain, as Kindred squabbles over feeding grounds cause collateral damage to the kine population, which in turn attracts the interest of hunters.
Name: Jude, Emerson, Georgina, Imogen, Rémy, Ahmadou, Leke, Ndonyi, César, Arturo, Frida, Dulce, Rafael, Evandro, Mirela, Ivone, Panos, Yianni, Vassia, Katerina, Ryuichi, Oda, Itsuko, Mari

Overly Involved Herald
By necessity, whatever goes on at Elysium, whatever is happening with the city's powerful Kindred, you can count on this Herald to stick their nose in it. This can make them rather unpopular amongst Kindred who wish to keep their secrets to themselves, which is nearly all of them. On the other hand, this proclivity can be an advantage in dealing with them--a juicy tidbit can be irresistible bait for this Herald, who may, in turn, be willing to do a minor favor for their source.

Aloof Herald
In theory, a Herald's task is to communicate the wishes of the Prince to the Kindred of their domain. In practice, not all Heralds enact their work as dutifully as one might expect. Heralds whose attention is drawn away from Elysium may neglect to relay a particular policy or proclamation in a timely fashion. Sometimes, this may be a deliberate tactic to further their goals. Other times, it's simple incompetence.
Name: Holden, Patience, Abdul, Zahra, Leo, Madeleine
Clan: Tremere Generation: 12th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 5 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 6, Mental 7, Disciplines 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 4, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 8, Etiquette 7, Occult 8 Disciplines: Auspex 2 (Sense the Unseen, Premonition), Dominate 1 (Cloud Memory), Blood Sorcery 2 (Corrosive Vitae, Extinguish Vitae; Rituals: Blood Walk, Eyes of Babel) Equipment: Ritual blade, many-pocketed trench coat, small occult library they'd rather be locked in than at Elysium

Defiant Primogen
The relationship between a Prince and the Primogen Council of a city can be, to put it mildly, contentious. Primogen may harangue, argue with, undermine, or even plan direct sabotage against their Prince. Alternatively, a Primogen unhappy with their political situation may decide to go on the attack against other members of the council. These dynamics can result in unusual alliances as council members maneuver to secure a larger share of the city's resources for themselves and their factions.
A Primogen can represent a single clan or other group, such as a consortium of extremely rich Kindred, an ideological movement with influence in the city, or an astroturf group created by ancillae as part of a decades-spanning scheme.

Enterprising Keeper
of Elysium
A Keeper is a consummate host, an impeccable curator, and a ruthless peacekeeper all in one. One may jealously rule over Elysium as a domain in miniature, taking glee in punishing any social infractions, while another may see their role as parental in nature.
Keeping the backbiting, scheming Kindred at Elysium happy, or at least civil, means keeping them fed. Enterprising Keepers may coerce, seduce, kidnap, or purchase blood dolls as entertainment. The lucky ones may be stripped of their memories and then returned to where they were found. The unlucky might be injected with a fatal dose of some research chemical to provide a moderately interesting experience for some jaded Kindred.

Tired Sheriff
Everyone's heard about Sheriffs who are bloodthirsty maniacs. But some are just tired. Someone has to clean up when a neonate goes on a rampage in a club. Someone has to deal with a Cleaver's family members when they just won't stay away. This Sheriff doesn't take any real pleasure in killing mortals, enforcing the rules, or hunting down those who break them. All they really want is peace and quiet--shame they'll never get it.
Name: Masud, Saida, Grayson, Niamh, Silas, Harmony, Mike

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Rival Coteries
Sooner or later, you're going to run into another crew of Kindred out there. Most obviously, coteries can come into conflict over territory, but there are plenty of reasons why two groups of Kindred might find themselves at odds. An upstart pack of Anarchs sets out to make a name for themselves by humiliating a Camarilla circle. Two groups of Kindred vie for the favor of a Primogen who has something they both need and only one can claim. Or maybe they've just been set against one another for the amusement of jaded ancillae.
Coterie Leader
For reasons of seniority, personal charisma, connections, or simple, brutal efficiency, this vampire is in charge of their coterie. Having risen to the leadership, the head of a coterie is unlikely to rest there. They're going to be working the angles, trying to get in better with the local Anarchs or score invites to Camarilla events, building up their name recognition in the city. Some are going to be tight enough with their coterie that they'll take them with them to the top, wherever that is. Others might ditch their old crew as soon as a better opportunity comes up, then rationalize it as cutting out a toxic friend group or wrenching free of the dead weight that was holding them back.

Coterie Face
A particularly cunning, outgoing, or seductive Kindred may become the unofficial face of their coterie, helping to smooth over difficult negotiations, bait kine and rival vampires into traps, and otherwise assist the group in scenarios where the use of brute force is less effective than subterfuge and greasing palms. Having someone who knows the streets and the locals can be a real boon for an up-and-coming coterie.
On the other hand, if you're the one who knows all the right people, you might start to think you don't need your buddies to get on in the world. You might try to cut deals or worm your way into connections you don't tell your gang about.

Coterie Bruiser
The bruiser specializes in getting rid of problems that are best solved through the injudicious application of force. They may be a mad dog, barely kept on a leash by the coterie's leader, or a cold and calculating killer. Typically, they don't have much of a taste for politics. That said, they're likely to be keenly sensitive to the possibility that they're being disrespected by their crew's leadership.

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Anarchs
Brutal Anarch Leader
Anarchs run the gamut from idealistic young guns to vicious bullies whose main problem with Camarilla philosophy is that they aren't the ones controlling the feeding grounds and rubbing other vampires' faces in the dirt. This Anarch leader is the latter--someone who rules through fear and brutality. Their domain may be run in an orderly, if draconian, fashion, or they might be an all-out despot, whose rule is balanced on a knife's edge.
If they're brutal to Kindred, they're downright cruel to kine. They'll feed, kill, and torture without compunction, ruining lives for things like weapons, influence, and simple pleasure. And if they even care about rationalizing their actions anymore, they can justify their behavior as necessary for the ongoing struggle against the Ivory Tower.
Name: Mikael, Camila, Serge, Clotilde, Solomon, Prudence
Clan: Brujah or Tzimisce Generation: 11th Blood Potency: 3 Humanity: 3 General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 3, Mental 5, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 9, Intimidation 10, Leadership 8, Streetwise 8 Disciplines (Brujah): Celerity 3 (Rapid Reflexes, Fleetness, Blink), Potence 2 (Soaring Leap, Prowess), Presence 1 (Daunt) Disciplines (Tzimisce): Dominate 3 (Compel, Mesmerize, Forgetful Mind), Protean 3 (Eyes of the Beast, Feral Weapons, Earthmeld) Clan Bane (Tzimisce Only): Trinkets and offerings given by their "loyal" subjects. Equipment: Sawed-off shotgun, human shield blood dolls, flashbang

Diplomatic Anarch Leader
It's rare for Duskborn to rise up to positions of authority, but it does happen. Intelligent thinbloods can use their connections to the human world to their advantage, working lines that would be tricky for true Kindred. They may be tied to a gang, an activist organization, a hacker collective, or some other element of kine society. Regardless, they've made a name for themselves through their ability to broker deals and get people what they want.
This sort of Anarch leader may be more connected to the world of the living, but as they achieve more status amongst Kindred, they may find themselves more willing to treat their former colleagues as tools rather than people. Unfortunately, the successful small-time Duskborn leader is likely to realize pretty quickly that no matter their connections, they're seen as a joke or a threat by many true vampires.
Name: Marcus, Poppy, Juan, Aisha, Branson, Shiloh
Clan: Thin-Blood Generation: 14th Blood Potency: 0 Humanity: 8 General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 4, Social 5, Mental 4, Thin-Blood Alchemy 4 Secondary Attributes: Health 4, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Insight 9, Leadership 8, Politics 8, Streetwise 8 Disciplines: Thin-Blood Alchemy 2 (Haze, Counterfeit Discipline) Equipment: Garage lab, smartphone, 3D-printed ghost handgun
Occultist Anarch Leader
There's a lot of fucked-up shit happening on the streets these nights. Alchemists are messing with forces they barely understand, wraiths are floating around screwing with kine, and lupines are pushing into the cities to hit back against civilization. It all adds up to an unpredictable and dangerous

182

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

atmosphere, turning hunting grounds into madhouses. Under these conditions, someone who can keep an eye on and maybe even control some of this mystical BS can quickly find themselves in a leadership position.
This kind of Anarch leader has two reasons for cracking down on sorcery: one, it has the potential to draw interest from the Ivory Tower, the Second Inquisition, and Caine knows who else, and two, it's in their interest to keep it all to themselves.
Name: Amir, Roya, Sung-Min, So-Hee, D'Angelo, Jacinta

Religious Adversaries
Religious elements have always wielded political influence in human societies, from the Catholic Church to Daoist advisors. Things are no different among the Kindred, with powerful cults cutting across the Camarilla-Anarch divide and lesser ones struggling to gain a foothold through any means necessary.

Kindred Cult Priest
Some priests concern themselves solely with the mysteries and internal politicking of their cult. Others make a deliberate effort to gain adherents in the halls of power. Sometimes, this involves outright evangelizing. More often, it's about laying the bait and waiting for curious, bored, or desperate Kindred to take it.

Kindred Cult Defender

Cleopatran Cultist
Followers of the Cleopatran cult revere Yima, an ancient childe of the clan's progenitor. They adorn themselves in elaborate clothing--some historical, some more avant-garde and of the moment--and style themselves as sophisticates. While they remain monstrous to kine and are seen as hopeless and vapid by many others of their clan, their sheer charisma makes them popular with other vampires at Elysium. Some among them even claim to receive daysleep visions from Yima herself--dreams that speak of a future in which they will rule all Kindred and that fuel the political ambitions of a clan that has for centuries been banished to the shadows.

Name: Aurelia, Caesulena, Septimia Clan: Nosferatu Generation: 11th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 6 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 8, Mental 8, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 4, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Craft 9, Etiquette 9, Persuasion 10, Politics 10 Disciplines: Animalism 1 (Sense the Beast), Obfuscate 1 (Silence of Death), Presence 1 (Awe) Equipment: Lavish and possibly ancient jewelry, professional makeup kit, couture or vintage clothing
Kindred Services
The Camarilla, Anarchs, and Kindred cults aren't the only political factions in modern nights. All manner of bit players, money men, entrepreneurs, gangsters, dealers, outfits, and splinter groups get involved in political struggles. Just as interest groups, lobbyists, and funders pressure and cajole human politicians, so too do myriad vampires attempt to influence the leadership of their domains.
Circulatory System Higher-Up
Some Kindred rise up through the ranks of the Circulatory System, from humble beginnings as baggers and taggers to a world of personal assistants, obliging drivers, and dimly lit, immaculately appointed offices. They got there by being good at their job, to the point that even many other Kindred find them to be off-putting and grotesque.
Essentially executives of mass kidnapping and human trafficking, these Kindred represent a different kind of horror than a Prince or a Frenzyprone vampire ruled by the Beast. Their domain is not limited to a neighborhood or even a city. They have vision. They see blood moving across the country, around the world. Human beings are

184

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

not prey to them but a commodity, which, like all commodities, must be allowed to freely circulate.

Road Courier
The road courier may not be a major political player, but as one of the few Kindred who regularly travel between cities, they tend to pick up quite a few connections. Of course, if they end up knowing too much, they may find that they're simply another disposable piece in a game being played well above their pay grade.

Club Operator
The Kindred club operator likes stability. With rents in most major cities being what they are, margins are tight enough without hunters poking around, vampires frenzying and flipping their shit, or turf wars going down between rival Anarch leaders outside. In general, whatever local authority can enforce the law and keep things running smoothly is likely to win support from the owner of a Kindred club. Still, there are always small business owners who have greater ambitions.

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Court Ritualist
A smart leader retains the services of those who can do things that they can't. For many Kindred, that's Blood Sorcery. While the practice of that particular Discipline is still suspect among many, few can deny that it's useful to have one of its practitioners on hand. Often more interested in their esoteric studies than in earthly ambitions, redworkers can be powerful allies to the vampire who can keep them happy and well compensated. Of course, there are those exceptions who see no conflict between the pursuit of mystical knowledge and the acquisition of political might.
Name: Simi, Eunike, Ianga, Ngaio, Vincent, Aspen
Clan: Tremere or Banu Haqim Generation: 10th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 4 General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 8, Mental 9, Disciplines 8 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 9, Medicine 10, Occult 10 Disciplines (Tremere): Auspex 1 (Sense the Unseen), Blood Sorcery 4 (A Taste for Blood, Extinguish Vitae, Scorpion's Touch, Theft of Vitae; Rituals: Clinging of the Insect, Eyes of Babel, Firewalker, Defense of the Sacred Haven) Disciplines (Banu Haqim): Blood Sorcery 4 (Corrosive Vitae, Scour Secrets, Blood of Potency, Blood Aegis;* Rituals: Blood Walk, Illuminate Trail of Prey, Dagon's Call, Eyes of the Nighthawk), Obfuscate 1 (Silence of Death) Equipment: Arcane tomes, well-protected and outfitted ritual space *Players Guide, p. 98

Incompetent Court Ritualist
Good help is hard to find. For those who aren't able to get premium Blood Sorcery, there's always the knockoff, the imitator, the store brand. Sure, your full-on Tremere or Banu Haqim Court Ritualist is the real deal, capable of conjuring up all manner of chaos. But the less competent sorcerer and ThinBlood Alchemists have a few tricks up their sleeves, too. And hey, if they can't do the kinds of scary shit that their more accomplished cousins can, then at least you don't have to worry as much about what kinds of terrifying things they might turn on you if they decide they're getting a raw deal.
This caliber of Court Ritualist may be working for trade--exotic materials, blood, hunting rights--or in the hopes of gaining access to greater mystical knowledge. At this level, they may also simply be trying to prove their utility to a Sheriff who's just looking for a reason to stake them, or they could be given the authority to practice their arts in an Anarch territory on the condition that they also accept being Blood Bonded to a particular Baron.
Name: Skipper, Onyx, Anton, Olivia, Clem, Reed Clan: Thin-Blood or Nosferatu Generation: 14th if Duskborn; 12th if Nosferatu Blood Potency: 0 if Duskborn; 1 if Nosferatu Humanity: 6 General Difficulty: 4/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 3, Social 3, Mental 5, Disciplines 3 Secondary Attributes: Health 4, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Medicine 7, Occult 8, Technology 7 Disciplines (Thin-Blood): Thin-Blood Alchemy 3 (Far Reach, Envelop, Defractionate) Disciplines (Nosferatu): Animalism 1 (Bond Famulus), Blood Sorcery 1 (A Taste for Blood; Rituals: Blood Walk, Craft Bloodstone, Ward Against Ghouls), Obfuscate 1 (Cloak of Shadows) Equipment: Impromptu and/or vulnerable ritual space, laptop, burner phone
