COURTS OF THE DAMNED

STORIES

The Ferryman
Caught in the Middle of an Active Investigation Physical (optional), Social, Mental
This story takes place in Camarilla territory. Run it as a one-shot, or replace the Kindred Storyteller characters with members of the Court in an ongoing chronicle.
A dead body is discovered in the players' characters' domain, bringing an old unsolved serial killer case back into the police searchlight. Some evidence points to one of the players' characters as a possible suspect, and there's a risk they'll take the fall if they can't clear their name. However, the real killer is a high-ranking Camarilla official. Can the players' coterie turn the lead investigator off the case before he solves it, pin the blame on someone else, or get the Camarilla authorities' blessing to turn in the murderer?
Players' coteries of three or fewer can be in the same scenes. Bigger coteries are best split up and can play through different scenes as they work on a solution.
Why the Players' Coterie?
The players' characters get drawn into a problem they didn't create but which is nonetheless theirs to solve. This story showcases how membership in the Camarilla is a commitment that comes with strings attached; it also lets the coterie attempt to leverage intel and favors to get ahead in a game that seems rigged against them.

Goals
The coterie will have to work together to clear their member's name in the ongoing investigation--in a way that doesn't get them in deep trouble with the local Camarilla Court. Whether they do this by working through members of the court to be able to turn in the real murderer, by influencing or threatening the mortal cops to drop the case, or by manipulating the facts of the case, leading to a wrongful conviction of a mortal, is up to them and their personal Convictions.
Supporting Characters
This story can involve several Camarilla officials, but principally uses the following characters:
Beau Willson, Investigative Lead
Beau has arrived in town to lead the investigation. At first impression, he doesn't appear difficult to manipulate. Short and friendly, with a significant widow's peak, the middle-aged investigator often seems lost in his own thoughts, as if he doesn't care that much about the case. But he takes note of every interaction and only seems approachable because it tends to make people relax and show their hand. An honest cop in a corrupt system, Beau will do whatever it takes to solve this old case that has been a thorn in his side for the majority of his career.
General Difficulties: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 6, Mental 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Firearms 7, Awareness 8, Investigation 8

187

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Gill Miller, Camarilla Sheriff
Gill is the true Ferryman. He's had his haven in this city since the '70s and has been an active serial killer for just as long, with an intensity that goes beyond that of "normal" vampire activity. Gill doesn't just kill to feed or out of a lack of self-control, but because he enjoys it, needs it, never seizes thinking of it. Initially, he favored picking up people on the pier and taking them out to sea in his boat. This is how he got the moniker--abducting them out onto those black waters never to be seen again. There were witnesses, occasionally, but no one who could ever provide the police with a description.
It isn't generally known to the Camarilla authorities how many killings Gill has committed. The Court is aware that he used to struggle to control his Beast, but it is considered a thing of

the past. As the Sheriff, and masked as a security contractor for a major local corporation, he does essential work to protect the city's Kindred.
Clan: Ventrue Generation: 10th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 5 General Difficulties: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 8, Social 9, Mental 8, Disciplines 8 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Stealth 9, Intimidation 10, Subterfuge 10 Disciplines: Dominate 3 (Cloud Memory, Mesmerize, Submerged Directive), Fortitude 2 (Resilience, Toughness), Presence 1 (Daunt) Clan Bane: Must feed from those who are afraid.

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

May White, Seneschal
May joined the Court as its Seneschal only a decade ago and is still putting in the work to prove that she was the right choice. She has no interest in disturbing the existing power balance between the city's established Kindred if she can avoid it. Though she doesn't exactly trust the Sheriff, she also won't challenge him unless she has a good reason to do so and is confident that she'll have the rest of the Court behind her.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 12th Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 6 General Difficulties: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 8, Social 7, Mental 8, Disciplines 9 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Leadership 9, Finance 10, Politics 10 Disciplines: Auspex 1 (Heightened Senses), Celerity 2 (Rapid Reflexes, Rush Job), Potence 1 (Soaring Leap)
Harris Bronson, Cop
Harris became a cop because he wanted to be a hero, but he has since discovered that heroes aren't real and that cops get shit pay, endure constant disrespect, and have to work holidays. He's made minor trouble go away for the Kindred before, motivated by some light financial compensation, and he'll be happy to do so again. Major trouble, though, will require more than pocket change.
General Difficulties: 4/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 4, Social 3, Mental 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 8, Subterfuge 9, Streetwise 7

Locations
This story is written to take place in a small to mid-sized coastal city in the U.S., but it can easily be adapted for a different part of the world. It can use the following locations:
24/7 Diner: The diner serves hearty homestyle cooking and burned coffee to its guests at all hours of the night. The waitresses are bone-tired and their smiles forced, but this doesn't seem to bother Detective Willson, who still engages them in conversation and tips excellently even when they respond in one-word sentences and take away his food before he's finished with it. Officer Bronson comes here most nights, mostly to harass the waitresses.
The Red Sparrow: In this city, the Court can be reached through a specialty wine store with weekly tastings and ample space to host private events. In practice, many Court gatherings are held at Gill Miller's company offices, but The Red Sparrow is still where the Kindred go to request an audience with the Prince or Primogen, and May White comes here nightly to work from the shop office. A portly, elderly gentleman manages the shop and any business with the kine, taking messages to Miss White from Kindred visitors.
Gill Miller's House: The city Sheriff makes his haven in a villa in one of the fancier neighborhoods. He takes care of his house, and his garden is the pride of the local homeowner association. Gill doesn't usually have Camarilla visitors, preferring to handle Court business at Court. But it isn't exactly a hard-and-fast rule, and younger Kindred might be excused for not knowing any better--especially if they explain the issue is time sensitive.
The Ferryman has a collection of trophies from his victims in the storm shelter behind his house. His collection of torn fingernails proves beyond a doubt that he's been killing consistently for over 60 years and never stopped. Or, to the cops, they would prove that he has followed in the footsteps of the original killer, the grim heir to a horrid business.
At the Storyteller's discretion, further evidence

189

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

can be located around Gill's house. Storm Drain Outlet: Just outside the city proper,
there is a canal where, 30 years ago, storm drain pipes let excess water from rainstorms and flooding flow towards the sea. Wastewater from a recent construction project in the city center has meant that the storm drain is seeing use again. Some 20 meters up through the outlet, a heavy metal grating prevents any larger items from entering or exiting. However, the bodies have had time to disintegrate, and now bits of cloth, hair clots, and bones are pulling loose and drifting out through the canal.
The Police Station: At night, the only people in the station are the watch commander, the 911 operator, the elderly lady in records, the jailor, and, for at least half the night, Beau Willson, who is working on putting together the puzzle. There are also, of course, the meth heads in the holding cell. Four officers are out on patrol, and more can be called in if anything noteworthy happens.
The evidence from the Ferryman case sits in a box in the evidence locker when it isn't on Willson's desk.
Scenes
The Invitation: It's not an arrest--it's just an invitation for a polite conversation when the players' characters find out one of them is a person of interest in a recently reopened murder case. Detective Willson, Officer Bronson in tow, runs into them when they are out on a routine hunt (preferably just before they pounce on a victim, making them aware how close they got to being noticed) and invites them to join him for a coffee at the diner.
Willson doesn't mind sharing details about the case: a body was recently discovered after having disappeared in a way that's eerily similar to how the Ferryman's victims used to disappear in the '70s, '80s, and '90s. The cops think they might be dealing with a copycat, which is why Willson's been called in. The funny thing is, the player's character was seen on the scene the night the victim disappeared.
It's a test of Intelligence + Insight at Difficulty 3 to discover that Willson isn't revealing everything

and thinks he has more on the player's character. Realizing the Stakes: Following the interview
with the detective, one or several players' characters are picked up by May White, who impresses upon them that the Court has reason to believe the police investigation may lead to Gill Miller as the next suspect, asking them to play along for now and, if necessary, find a way to botch the investigation.
A test of Intelligence + Insight at Difficulty 4 can reveal that May is uneasy about the situation.
Manipulating the Investigation (optional): The players' characters can attempt to misdirect the investigation by manipulating Detective Willson or Officer Bronson. Make a test at Difficulty 5 to influence Willson; a failure results in him becoming convinced the characters are hiding something and committed to discovering what it is, even if it turns out not to involve the ongoing investigation. Tests to manipulate Officer Bronson are Difficulty 4, and he demands a substantial bribe, no matter the result, either to help or to shut up about the attempt.
If Bronson helps with the investigation, Willson becomes suspicious, and the investigation continues as planned. If Willson helps, it will bother him subconsciously, and although the evidence against the player's character goes away, he doesn't drop the case. Regardless, the Storm Drain Outlet crime scene will be the next thing that is discovered.
Destroying the Evidence (optional): If one or several players' characters manage to sneak into the station and locate the evidence, they find gruesome pictures and newspaper clippings revealing how long the Ferryman has been active and that most of his supposed victims were between the ages of 15 and 25. This discovery also suggests that Miller was actively trying to pin his recent kill on a player's character.
Negotiating with the Camarilla Court (optional): If the players' characters choose to attempt to negotiate with the Court through May White, they can argue that the Camarilla Court

190

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

shouldn't let Gill Miller continue his activities. White is under the impression that one, maybe he never committed the murders and two, if he did, he stopped a long time ago. If the players' characters convince her that he is still killing and is an active threat to the city, she will want to put a stop to his activities. Convincing her that this will be possible with the coterie's support is a test of Charisma or Manipulation + Leadership, Persuasion, or Subterfuge at Difficulty 5; if the characters have hard evidence against Miller, the Difficulty is 3.
Catching a Murderer (optional): The players' characters can find evidence against Miller at his house, which they can gain access to by asking to discuss the case with him, bringing him some Sheriff-relevant business, or sneaking in while he is out. They may also locate evidence at the Storm Drain Outlet.
Successful use of Dominate or Presence on Miller himself or a test of Charm + Persuasion or Subterfuge at Difficulty 5 can lure a confession out of the ancilla. Thinking that the characters are in awe of him, or at least amused by what he thinks is their inability to do anything to stop him, he reveals to them how he used to kill out on the water and take the bodies to an old sewer in the harbor, where they'd be swallowed up and carried down into the pipes below the city. He'll demand that they clean up the mess and promise a reward when they have done so.
Finding a Fall Guy (optional): The players' characters can pin the crime on any mortal over the age of 70 who still appears to be relatively healthy by moving evidence from the Storm Drain Outlet or Miller's house to the home of their intended suspect. In an ongoing chronicle, this costs everyone involved a level of Humanity.
Aftermath: If the characters convince May White to take Miller down, the Court takes care of it. The cops find the evidence at his house, along with his staked body that has been arranged to appear as though he killed himself. The sect then either stashes him somewhere safe to consider his crimes in torpid dreams or destroys him permanently.

If the players' characters fail to interrupt or misdirect the investigation, one of them gets arrested and taken into custody. The Camarilla Court will have them out of jail again before the morning, to avoid a Masquerade breach, but they will have to leave the city as a murderer on the run.
If the players' characters cover for Miller and also avoid getting arrested, everything continues as it always has. Only now, every disappearance down at the harbor is on their conscience, and Miller seems to think he owns them.
Variations
Guilty as Charged: A member of the coterie did actually commit the murder they are suspected of, and Gill Miller is merely taking advantage of an opportune situation. To do it like this, initiate this story when a player's character (accidentally) kills a mortal in an ongoing chronicle, or agree in advance with one of the players that their character will have done it.
Not Just Any Body: The latest body isn't just a random Jane Doe but was someone who meant something to a player's character. This will motivate the coterie to make sure they find a solution that includes getting revenge on Gill.
To do this in a one-shot story, have each player describe a mortal Touchstone and write their name on a note. Start the session by drawing a name at random. To do it in an ongoing chronicle, consider introducing a mortal character specifically for this purpose a few sessions before initiating this story--killing an established mortal relation without any prior warning may feel unnecessarily punishing to players, especially if it was someone they expected to have ongoing interactions with.
Anarch Deceiver: Adapt this story to take place in an Anarch domain by making Gill Miller a wellrespected member of the Movement and one of the original Kindred who took the city. Mary White and the rest of the Court are the younger but passionate Anarch community who look up to him.

191

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Nowhere to Go but Up
A Chance to Reach for Power Social, Mental
This story is written for an established Camarilla coterie and takes place in Camarilla territory. To use it in a one-shot, consider awarding each player's character additional Backgrounds and Advantages representing the work they have already done to rise within the sect. To use it in an ongoing chronicle, replace any relevant Storyteller characters with existing ones.
The city's Herald disappeared several weeks ago without leaving a word of their whereabouts. The general assumption is that they have been hurt and fallen into torpor, were destroyed, or have defected to the Anarchs. Whatever the cause, their job has been left open. The Prince is growing impatient for it to be filled, and the task of picking a new Herald has fallen to the Primogen Council. The players' coterie has a real chance to take the position for one of them, if they can convince at least two out of the three Primogen that this would be a good idea.
Why the Players' Coterie?
This story assumes that at least one of the players' characters is interested in taking over this prestigious position within the Camarilla Court and has been with the sect long enough to have demonstrated that they are worth investing in. The position of Herald will come with a significant status boost along with the possibility to directly influence city decisions by gaining the ear of the Prince and Primogen Council. It also comes with certain responsibilities, such as taking messages to other Kindred cities.

Goals
The goals of the players' characters need not be aligned. They may all agree on one of them as a candidate for the task, but several of them can also be competing for it--be aware that this can significantly add to the length of the session. To get the job, a character will have to gain the support of at least two out of three Primogen. The problem is that each Primogen is suspicious of the other two, so being seen to be too cozy with any one of them will alienate the others. A player's character will therefore have to be careful how they communicate with the Councilors.
If the Council cannot agree on choosing one of the player's characters, they will give up and pick the Prince's upstart childe, the harebrained Pietro di Sole, who has been charming his way up the Camarilla career ladder despite being charming to absolutely no one but the Prince. Making sure Pietro does not become the new Herald can make a worthy goal on its own, even if the members of the players' coterie aren't that interested in the job itself.
Supporting Characters
This story uses the following characters:
Alfio Francesco
Alfio is not a new addition to the local Camarilla. Rather, his decades of commitment to the sect, in combination with the city's small but significant Lasombra community, are what have earned him his seat on the Primogen Council. A handsome, if somewhat stern-looking man, he appears to be in his early 40s and always dresses well. Alfio has spent his unlife building a significant fortune and a position in local business that earns him respect. He hates disrespect and punishes it harshly.
He distrusts Manfredi Gallo's sense of judgment because he finds him weak-willed and neurotic.
He understands Roberta's competence but enjoys thwarting her because he dislikes her dominant temperament. He thinks it is good for her not to

always get her way. He chooses Pietro if he must because he thinks it
will get him off his back at the casino. Clan: Lasombra Generation: 11th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 5 General Difficulties: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 9, Social 8,

(Shadow Cloak, Arms of Ahriman),* Potence 2 (Lethal Body, Prowess)

Manfredi Gallo
Manfredi Gallo can be particularly sharp and intelligent when he has a problem to solve. However, he can be just as unfocused and agitated when he grows restless, especially if he hasn't fed. He has the appearance of a man in his early 50s, with a great head of gray locks and a style that favors business casual. The Court tolerates his eccentricity for the brilliance that occurs when it is most needed, and besides, he has a way of finding out when people are plotting against him, and it never ends well for them. Manfredi doesn't currently consider replacing the Herald to be a particularly interesting mental challenge, but perhaps that can change.
He recognizes Alfio Francesco's strength and importance to the Court but thinks it is important to keep his ambition in check to avoid him getting any ideas about reallocating city funds to the Lasombra or such things.
He would quite like Roberta Macchiedo if she would be polite enough to like him back. Something to do with an old dalliance. Since she doesn't, he has convinced himself he detests her and prefers to avoid her, if he can.
He will pick Pietro di Sole if he doesn't find a better option, because he has a feeling about him

and thinks he can become a decent Herald given a few years of practice.
Clan: Malkavian Generation: 12th Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 6 General Difficulties: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 6, Mental 6, Disciplines 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Stealth 9, Subterfuge 10, Awareness 10 Disciplines: Auspex 2 (Heightened Senses, Premonition), Obfuscate 2 (Silence of Death, Unseen Passage) Clan Bane: Anxiety with occasional crippling panic attacks (Social)
Roberta Macchiedo
Roberta has a great sense of ownership over the city's fate and well-being, having been a part of the Court in one way or another for close to 80 years. Obsessive about her Primogen responsibilities as well as her self-assigned tasks as a co-owner of Vivid Strategies, it is rare that she is out of her office. Her sharp black bob and prim dress suits don't quite make up for her youthful appearance, which is a continuous source of frustration for the oldest of the Primogen.
Roberta dislikes Alfio Francesco on a personal level because, through all the decades she has known him, he has always failed to pay her the respect she deserves.
She does not want Manfredi Gallo to get his way because she thinks he is working to further his own clan's interests at the cost of hers.
She will pick Pietro di Sole if no better option presents itself because she assumes she will be able to manage him indirectly--through a woman who fits his type if necessary.
Clan: Ventrue Generation: 11th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 6 General Difficulties: 5/4

Standard Dice Pools: Physical 8, Social 8, Mental 9, Disciplines 8
Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Etiquette 10, Insight 10, Leadership 10 Disciplines: Dominate 1 (Cloud Memory), Fortitude 2 (Unswayable Mind, Toughness), Presence 1 (Awe) Clan Bane: Must feed from women who are attracted to her.
Pietro di Sole
The newest addition to the Court, Pietro, was Embraced by the Prince following a lengthy charm offensive, which had the Prince thinking he'd found a true friend to share eternity with. Pietro still amuses the Prince, who has, however, realized that his childe is not as ready for responsibilities as one could hope. Therefore the Prince won't push the matter of Heraldry if the Primogen can agree on a better option. Meanwhile, Pietro enjoys everything unlife has to offer, from his fast car to the semicelebrity status his connection to the owner of Vivid Strategies offers him. The company offices bore him to death, however, so he tends to be anywhere but there.
Clan: Toreador Generation: 13th Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 7 General Difficulties: 4/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 4, Mental 3, Disciplines 4 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Drive 8, Persuasion 8, Subterfuge 7 Disciplines: Celerity 2 (Rapid Reflexes, Fleetness), Presence 2 (Awe, Lingering Kiss)
Locations
This story is written with a mid-sized Italian domain in mind, without taking place in any specific city,

194

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

but it can easily be adapted for a different part of the world. It can use the following locations:
Central Court, Vivid Strategies Headquarters: The city Court is based out of the polished downtown offices of Vivid Strategies, a marketing company specializing in corporate branding. The top floor of the offices is management only, so Kindred-exclusive. This is where the Primogen Council has its meetings, and the workaholic Roberta Macchiedo seems to have located her haven on this floor as well, the better to keep an eye on everything.
Soho House: Before the members-only club opened a location in the city, Manfredi Gallo felt restless and without anywhere decent to call his home away from home. Now, he spends the majority of his free time in the Soho House gym, lounging on the rooftop, attending networking events as a representative for the Court branding company, or lying in his private suite with a cooling face mask over his tired eyes. He considers the mortal staff there his truest friends and will get very angry if he notices anyone disrespecting them. He rarely lashes out, so the rage can seem sudden and unmotivated when it manifests.
Blue Diamond Casino and Theater: The casino and entertainment palace is one of Alfio Francesco's favored haunts. As an investor in the business, he can usually be found in one of the exclusive VIP sections. The rich clientele and many beautiful women have also attracted Pietro di Sole's attention, and he has taken to arriving here most nights, making a pit stop to rouse his appetites at the blackjack table or in one of the cocktail bars.
Scenes
Sundown Briefing: The players' characters find out that there's a possibility for one of them to become the new Herald at the weekly sundown briefing (which happens about an hour after darkness has fallen) at Vivid Strategies. The Primogen Council notes that they are among the candidates who have shown promise and that if there is interest, further interviews may settle the deal.

On their way out of the briefing, one or several of the players' characters run into Pietro, who antagonizes them, bragging that he is going to get the job and they might as well give up because he will soon be giving them orders. He appears not to entirely understand what being the Herald entails and doesn't care if corrected, claiming that he'll decide that for himself as the voice of the Prince, whose voice is law.
Convincing the Councilors: The players' characters can seek out each of the three Primogen at their respective locations. It doesn't matter if they go together or separately unless they are competing against each other. Convincing two of the Primogen to cast their vote for a player's character is Difficulty 6 in any Mental or Social test. This is lowered to 4 if they get the impression that one or both of the other Primogen would not like it, and it is lowered to 3 if they think the player's character would do a good job. Reward player creativity and attention to detail, and lower the difficulty further if the following things happen:  Alfio Francesco: The player's character criticizes
Manfredi Gallo for his neuroticism or Roberta Macchiedo for her bossy manners. The character wins big in the casino--use a d10 to simulate gambling: rolling a 9 or 10 is a big win, rolling a 1 or 2 is a big loss. Let them roll up to 10 times and suffer the financial consequences. Alfio will not vote for anyone foolish enough to lose a serious sum at the casino.  Manfredi Gallo: The player's character criticizes Alfio's ambition or mentions that they think Roberta deep down respects Manfredi's ability to stand up to her. The character convinces Manfredi that the task of choosing the right Herald is very important to the Court's future in the city. Manfredi will not vote for anyone rude to the staff at Soho House.  Roberta Macchiedo: The player's character criticizes Alfio Francesco's misogyny or questions Manfredi Gallo's relationship to his clan. The character asks interestedly about her long history with the Camarilla.

195

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

The players will be able to find out how to win over each of the Primogen using tests of Intelligence + Insight and by talking to them. They all know what the others dislike about them and will divulge it if they think the characters are likely to agree with them that it's ridiculous.
While at the Blue Diamond Casino, the players' characters are likely to run into Pietro again, who is being rather indiscreet about his powers, just not enough for anyone to do anything about.
Aftermath: If one of the players' characters manages to maneuver at least two of the Primogen to vote for them, they will become the new Herald, a decision that will be announced at an Elysium party. The party will be held at the Blue Diamond if Alfio Francesco voted for the character; otherwise, it will be gathering at the Soho House rooftop bar. If Pietro gained any of the votes, the loss disappoints him grievously, earning the player's character an enemy at Court. If he didn't, he notes that "the voice of the Prince" is probably just as important to impress as the Prince and starts flirting outrageously with them, ignoring the rest of the players' coterie.
Pietro will absolutely accept the role of Herald if offered, considering that it means more money and influence. He will like it, too, getting to hear his own voice as the Prince's representative at every single gathering of the Camarilla Court. Once Pietro gets a real taste for power, it may prove difficult to get rid of him again.
Variations
Minor Miscommunication: Despite everyone's expectations, the lost courtier returns just as the coterie member has secured the position for themselves, potentially along with extra benefits, such as a new haven, a vehicle, or a mortal job. The returned Kindred fully expects to pick up exactly where they left off, and they won't take it well if or when they find out the player's character has taken over.
Hot Potato: Instead of fighting for a position, everyone involved is trying their hardest not to get

the job. Perhaps it is a position as Seneschal to a demanding and spoiled Prince, perhaps it has no official Camarilla title but requires taking over a mortal position of company accountant and doing unholy amounts of corporate tax equations every single week. Members of the Primogen Council all want one of the players' characters to do it. However, they can get out of it if they convince Pietro that he wants to do it or they convince each of the Primogen that picking him would be good for them and frustrating for the others.
The Trial
The True Horror is the Justice System Mental, Social
The members of the players' coterie are summoned to a trial that they cannot win. It takes place soon or never in a courtroom that they cannot enter on their own. They will never know what they did. They will not know that this is a hopeless situation, as the system dangles the hope of resolution just out of their reach.
This story is meant to rekindle a sense of dread about the political systems of the World of Darkness. It can be used in any chronicle after the inevitable breach of the Traditions or in a Little Princes chronicle (p. 133) where the players' coterie is ignorant that they live inside a domain ruled by other hidden entities. The enemy entity, The Court, mimics a Camarilla Court but can appear in other domains as well.
Why the Players' Coterie?
Why does anyone become victims of cold unthinking power? Perhaps the players' coterie is well aware of everything they've done that could make them guilty. Perhaps they're haunted by their crimes, as they understand them. It will not be explained to them. They are simply chosen; the system doesn't require them to know why.

196

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Goals
The coterie's goal is to be acquitted of the crime they are accused of. They will no doubt seek out allies that may be connected to The Court and appear before them to plead their case. Every time they do so, they will be told that their testimony has been heard and that their case is still a very serious matter. In reality, the only way to escape justice is to ignore the impending trial or (see "Variant") prove that the whole trial is an illusion.
Supporting Characters
The Bailiffs
The bailiffs are the representatives and guards of The Court. They may be Kindred or something else entirely. For maximum effect, have them operate slightly differently than you'd expect vampires to. They may, for instance, only be active at dawn and dusk while the coterie is drowsy, and they may respond to staking by calmly pulling the stake out of their wound, writing a complaint to The Court with its pointed tip, and handing the characters a copy. They may be destroyed, but another pair soon appears.
General Difficulty: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 8, Mental 7 Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 8 Exceptional Dice Pools: Intimidation 8 (Legal Threats), Melee 7 (Billy Club) Equipment: Outdated police uniforms, billy clubs (Aggravated Damage +1), legal summons to trial that are impossible to understand (no matter what legal system's rules are applied to it)
The Lawyer
The characters are appointed a lawyer. He appears to be a ghoul not in the employment of The Court, and he might be the only hope the coterie has to

clear themselves of the unknown charges leveled against them. He is a sleazy, thin-haired man in upper middle age, dressed in a colorful polyester suit. He is confident, fast-talking, and a master of deception, loopholes, and subterfuge.
His advice may restore a level of Willpower every time he offers his services, even if the legalese he spouts is utter nonsense to the coterie. He charges an exorbitant amount of some absurd currency to engage his favors. For instance, he could require a complete collection of film-star cards from a certain era, a ton of fine erasers of gum arabic sourced in the Dominican Republic, or a meeting with an aging movie star that he loves. For every piece of advice he gives, he also, as if it were the most natural thing in the world, requires the coterie to perform some humiliating action, like recording themselves crawling on all fours and barking like dogs, reviewing all the menu items in a diner (forcing the coterie to throw up again and again), or attending the advice session naked. He takes no pleasure in their debasement--it's "simply how things are done."
General Difficulty: 4/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 3, Social 7, Mental 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 8 (Law), Insight 7
The Children
The children are a group of ever-present teenage kids that appear at regular intervals in the story. They seem to know everything about the plight of the characters and comment on their behavior in critical terms. The goal is to make the coterie feel they are being constantly watched and judged for their behavior, which will affect their upcoming trial. The children are mortal and do not know anything about the Kindred. They think the players' characters are ordinary mortals as well, perhaps neighbors, teachers, or friends of their parents. If pressured on how and why they know about the characters and

197

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

the crime they've committed, the children come up with perfectly rational explanations that seem to be true but fall apart on closer examination. For example: "Everyone watches your Twitch stream, it's really popular," or "You are a contestant on that new reality show." But no such stream or show exists. They are ordinary kids, possessed by spirits or mindcontrolled by The Court through Dominate. Killing them incurs a heavy loss of Humanity.
The children attack the players' coterie in social combat every time they appear. This should whittle down the Willpower of the group to dangerously low levels very fast.
General Difficulty: 2/1 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 3, Social 4, Mental 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 4, Willpower 3

Exceptional Dice Pools: Subterfuge 4 (Unnerving Explanations), Intimidation 6 (Pointing out Flaws)
The Court
The Court is as weird and unknowable as the bailiffs that serve it. In a standard chronicle, it is comprised of the Primogen Council and appears to be completely beyond the power level of the characters. In an ideal version of this story, this is the first time the characters encounter the system that governs their unlives, and they have no concept of who or what The Court is or why it exists. The Court is very serious when they hear the complaints of the coterie or perform one of the smaller trials that are part of progressing the characters' case.
But something is wrong. The Court attacks the

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

coterie in three rounds of social combat during every complaint they carry before them, the same as for every partial trial they summon the coterie to.
General Difficulty: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 8, Mental 9 Secondary Attributes: Health 8, Willpower 10 Exceptional Dice Pools: Persuasion 10 (Justice), Intimidation 8, Academics 8 (Law), Insight 9 (Guilt) Equipment: If the coterie manages to have a look at the books of law they quote or evidence they claim to be relevant to the case but never read out loud, it consists of cheap romance novels and pages from pornographic magazines from the 1980s bound in impressive-looking leather.
Locations
This story takes place in any city or domain where the characters operate. For a merciful version, it operates in a city where the coterie is sent to perform a mission, and the madness ends when they return home. But the story has more impact if this is just the way things are in the night world the coterie inhabits.
The Court: The Court, as a location, is identical to Elysium in an ongoing chronicle, and it should be the same place the coterie has been introduced to the Prince, met powerful allies, and so on. By default, The Court convenes in an impossibly expansive cellar underneath a local castle or mortal court building. Metal spiral staircases lead down and down, past rooms filled with the records of a million previous court cases like theirs, none of them finally settled. If the coterie comes to the courtroom and wants to be admitted, they find the door is guarded by bailiffs. They will be told there are several more doors before they can get in and that each subsequent door is guarded by a pair of bailiffs. If they sit down and wait, they will literally have to wait forever, and no one else ever comes. It is as if this door was created especially for the coterie's case.

Scenes
Summoned: The bailiffs appear as the coterie wakes up any evening. This scene works especially well if the previous night was one of debauchery and the coterie or some of its members did something they regret or feel embarrassed about. The bailiffs control the situation, making sure they have the characters' attention. They then state that the coterie is under suspicion for a breach of the Traditions and that their trial begins in one hour and will continue until it is concluded. Confusingly, the bailiffs inform them that this trial is a serious matter but one they shouldn't be too bothered about, since it probably won't affect their unlives in any meaningful way. With that, the bailiffs depart. If followed, they go to The Court and disappear. They can be found guarding the door to the court chambers proper.
Legal Preparations: When the coterie starts looking for more facts in their case, or talks to a Mawla or Contact about it, they're told it's perfectly normal not to be told what you are accused of or how to clear your name. Kindred justice is not mortal justice, they're told. Kindred are ageless and thus cases can run a bit on the long side. No matter whom they contact, they are given the name of a "reliable ghoul lawyer, the best in the city." All other Kindred they reach out to confirm that this is indeed how things are done in Kindred society.
The Children: The coterie gets the feeling they are being watched. Using Disciplines and Skills, they can figure out that a group of young teenagers is following them and filming them with their cell phone cameras. The children will continue following the coterie, openly or by stealth. If one group is scared away, another will appear.
The Trials: The coterie's best choice is to ignore the whole thing and go on with their unlives, but it is highly unlikely they will. Encourage paranoid fantasies about who reported them--could it be one of the other members of the coterie? A trusted ally? A Touchstone? Sooner or later, the coterie demands a hearing out of sheer panic (this can only succeed if they do it through their lawyer; otherwise, they

199

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

will wait at their door forever), or the bailiffs appear at a really inconvenient dawn or dusk and summon them to a hearing.
At the first of many partial trials, the coterie is asked how they plead, and their lawyer stares blankly at them. He states afterwards that he is unable to affect the free will of the coterie during trial. Then they are asked to tell The Court about the circumstances of their crime and if there are any exonerating circumstances--without being told what their crime is.
In the audience sits one of their Touchstones, kidnapped and drugged, making all damage from social combat Aggravated. The Touchstone responds seemingly randomly to their statements, laughing and booing erratically. The Touchstone has been restrained and dosed with something (possibly PCP or horse tranquilizer)--this explains their behavior but does not make it any less horrifying.
The children also appear in the audience, constantly recording everything on their phones. Every trial consists of three statements or monologues from the coterie and The (unimpressed or condemning) Court, represented as a social combat in three rounds. If characters win, they are told they are getting an extension of their trial, that the case is still serious and will continue. If they fail, they are told that they are considered guilty now and should not leave town, and to wait for their punishment.
Punishment: If the characters are found guilty, because they are reduced to 0 Willpower and admit their guilt (even though they have no idea what they have done wrong), are crushed in social combat by The Court, or confess their guilt voluntarily, they will be sentenced. The sentence is always final death by greeting the sun, which will be carried out at "a later unspecified date." But the next night comes, and the next, and the next. They are never called back to The Court.
In a continued chronicle, when one of the coterie members actually is about to meet final death (possibly much later), you can reintroduce the punishment scene. This can be done just after their

expected death scene, as a surreal and hellish postfinal-death encounter. The player's character finds themselves in front of The Court yet again. But this time, they are led by the bailiffs into a circular shaft hundreds of meters deep, with a strong iron grating on top. It is impossible to escape, and they are left to ponder the meaning of their unlife and the nature of The Court while the sun slowly rises. Perhaps to rise again and again in a repetitive, eternal punishment that will never let them go.
Hey, Wait a Minute... Players who enjoy a good read will no doubt recognize the setup of this story at some point and finally, perhaps in frustration, ask a Storyteller character why the hell it seems they are caught in Franz Kafka's The Trial. When they do, their Mawla, the lawyer, or a Contact tells them that Kafka is currently residing in Prague and that The Trial is not a parable on the uncertainty of human life--it's based on his observations of the Kindred system of justice.
Kindred are ageless. So are The Court, but whether they are of your kind or simply functions of the cycle of death and rebirth is unknown. So, the powers that suspect you have time, all the time in the world, to ascertain your guilt. Just accept it.
Ending the Trial: By default, this story never really ends. It continues in the background of other escapades, with the bailiffs, the lawyer, and the children appearing now and again, especially as the players' characters gather more and more serious charges on their rap sheet. This is simply what it is to be Kindred in the surreal domain they are trapped in. Perhaps it is like this everywhere. Perhaps this is what life and unlife are like for everyone, constantly on trial by their peers, their own mind, or an unknowable God.
Variation
For troupes that are not prepared to deal with the monumental angst of having The Trial hanging over

200

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

their heads for all perpetuity, consider this variant: Psychomachia: The Trial is a psychomachia,
a reality-deforming application of the Dominate, Auspex, and Presence Disciplines by a Malkavian elder or a group of Malkavian ancillae working together. Neither the bailiffs, the children, The Court, nor the lawyer are real. But the actions the players' characters perform are. The goal of this test of virtue may be one of the following:  To prepare for a real (more conventional)
trial; this is really a sophisticated way to elicit a confession.  To test if the characters will try to rebel and destroy The Court. If they do, they are sent a letter tattooed on the scalp of a monkey (reported as escaped from a zoo, if the domain is not in a city where monkeys are a common urban animal), saying "Why Do You Obey" in red letters. This is an invitation to defect to the Anarchs.  To test the coterie's mettle in general. If they survive a month under the spell of The Trial, they are offered positions as "bailiffs" for the Sheriff and sent to capture those suspected of having broken the Traditions.
Black Rooster
Wails of Past Betrayals Mental, Physical (optional), Social (optional)
When the black rooster crows, It's because the day has ended,
If the red rooster crowed, Another would crow too. They found each other in the sand, The two roosters face to face, The black rooster was big, But the red rooster was brave.
Oh! If I'm lying, Then let the sound of my song
Be erased by the wind. Black rooster, black rooster,

Black rooster I'm warning you: A red rooster doesn't give up. Only when it's already dead.
 "Black Rooster, Red Rooster," a song from the Spanish Civil War, 1936­1939
This is a story about the intersection of the politics of the dead, the living, and the undead. It's designed for a Hecata or other coterie with at least decent Oblivion or Auspex Disciplines who are willing to accept that ghosts exist. It takes place within a predominantly Anarch domain; the players' coterie can belong to any faction or be unbound.
The coterie learns of a ghost that holds a piece of valuable information about the past of an Anarch enemy or rival. While investigating how they can communicate with it, they are hunted by a group of mortals connected to their enemy and an implacable supernatural entity that claims they are committing a terrible crime.
In the end, they must choose whether to obey the rules of human morality, the Traditions, or the unknowable laws of the dead.
Why the Players' Coterie?
The antagonist of the story, George Moretti, has made an enemy of the players' coterie solely due to the fact that one or several of them are Hecata or otherwise able to commune with spirits. They quickly become embroiled in undead politics that go far beyond them. For an Anarch or very humanitarian coterie, revealing Moretti's secret, once they've had a whiff of it, can be reason enough to pursue the investigation.
Goals
The characters aim to commune with a ghost that knows a terrible secret from the past about one of their worst enemies. To do so, they must find a recording or memory of a lost revolutionary song and play it at the unmarked grave of a volunteer of the Spanish Civil War who died under shameful

201

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

circumstances. Along the way, they must avoid or successfully confront a group of mortal radicals. The coterie will be tempted to end their investigation many times during the story, and should be allowed to do so. If they do, the Storyteller should narrate the truth to the players, play the titular song in Spanish, and end with the statement "but none of this will ever be known or remembered."
Supporting Characters
George Moretti
First Comrade of the Urban Liberation Movement
Poised, tall, freckled, smiling, and clear-eyed, Moretti sports a mop of long, curly blond hair waving on top of a constantly animated and slightly too-large head. He talks too much and too intensely, coming off as a bit goofy. To many, he hardly seems to be a vampire at all. He is known to be a charismatic politician and is riding high from having closed a non-aggression pact with the local Camarilla. This will give the Movement time to consolidate over the coming decade. The price he has paid is promising to rid the domain of Hecata influence. Thus, he has made an enemy of the local Necromancers, including the players' characters.
Moretti was born in 1897 and was Embraced shortly after the Spanish Civil War in a French POW camp. He appears to be in his early 40s.
He was a communist firebrand in his youth and joined a group of volunteers traveling to Spain to fight fascism. There, he was taken captive by elite Italian troops sent by Mussolini in one of the early battles. The threat of torture was enough to make him a turncoat. In his mind, his betrayal helped prevent the unnecessary loss of lives as he disarmed and aided in the capture of a group of anarchist fighters in the buildup to a pitched battle outside Madrid. Shortly after, he deserted and crossed the Pyrenees.
After being transported to a French POW camp,

where the anti-fascist fighters were subjected to terrifying conditions, he contracted polio and was close to death. Desperate, he managed to send a message to his secret fascist masters, hoping for medical aid. Instead, he was rewarded for his loyalty with the blessing of undeath and placed as a mole inside the Brujah clan, still secretly serving his faceless master.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 9th Embrace: 1939 Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 5 (pretends to be higher) General Difficulty: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 8, Mental 6, Disciplines 6 Secondary Attributes: Health 7, Willpower 9 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 9 (Radical Politics), Melee 7 (Bayonet), Firearms 6 (Lee-Enfield Bolt-Action), Subterfuge 8 (Deflection) Disciplines: Celerity 3 (Cat's Grace, Fleetness, Blink), Potence 3 (Soaring Leap, Prowess, Spark of Rage), Presence 4 (Awe, Lingering Kiss, Entrancement, Summon) Equipment: Antique Lee-Enfield bolt-action rifle (very long range, Damage +3), bayonet (Damage +2)
Study Group 77
Mortal Radical Left Vigilantes
A motley crew of mortals (at least twice the number of characters in the players' coterie) in their 20s, sporting Army surplus or tan trench coats with various radical slogans and patches on their T-shirts. They are strong believers in social justice and tend to romanticize the socialist struggles of the early 20th century. All are students or employees at a local university and attend night lectures given by Moretti. Learning the truth about their teacher's past would break at least half of them and make a few of them side with the players' characters in a confrontation against him.
General Difficulty: 3/2 Humanity: 7

202

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 4, Mental 4
Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 4 Exceptional Dice Pools: Academics 5 (Radical Politics), Melee 5
The Haunting Shadow
The shadow is a ghost forced to serve the brutal hierarchy of the dead. It has a single mission--to haunt the coterie and make sure they understand that their meddling is breaking something it calls Stygian Law: "None dead may reveal the secrets of the dead, or fate will find them." It seems to treat the characters as if they were ghosts and thus subject to this law. This in itself should be a terrifying realization. The players' characters may misunderstand this as a reference to the Tradition of Masquerade, and for all intents and purposes, this law serves a similar function. The shadow will pack the strongest emotional punch if it is the ghost of someone the players' characters know or knew in their mortal life--a dead parent, child, lover, or dear friend.
General Difficulty: 5/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical N/A, Social 5, Mental 4 Secondary Attributes: Health 5 (the ghostly body can only be damaged by Necromancy or by hitting it with an object that held deep meaning to it in life), Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Stealth 9, Intimidation 8, Performance 6, Persuasion 6 Special: Their screams work like the Presence power Dread Gaze. When the shadow uses this power, they roll eight dice (their Intimidation pool) against the victim's Composure + Resolve, no eye contact needed.
Locations
The Bandiera Rossa Record Shop: A secondhand record store specializing in leftist folk and blues from Woody Guthrie to early Dylan. The owner

is a woman in her late 90s. She might remember meeting Spanish Civil War volunteers, and she may be used as a way to fill the players in on the history of the conflict.
She remembers George Moretti selling off all his records sometime in the 1960s. If shown a photo of Moretti, she will be absolutely shocked at how little he has changed. She will die if confronted by Study Group 77's violence or the terror of the shadow.
Buried deep in a pile of thick jazz and blues records is a rare recording of "Black Rooster, Red Rooster," a fighting song of the Red Republicans. It is marked as belonging to G.M. It has his Blood on it and has a powerful arcane connection to the fake radical.
The Stadium: An old stadium built on top of a prison that was active in the 1930s and '40s. During this interim period, returning Spanish Civil War volunteers were imprisoned here, a stark reminder that the world had not yet recognized their service to mankind by opposing fascism before the outbreak of the Second World War. If the city is in America, the prisoners were considered to be "communist sympathizers" and imprisoned on false charges. The city and the nation have done their best to forget this shameful episode, and digging it out requires an Academics (Local or Radical History) test with a Difficulty of 6 or the use of Auspex or Oblivion to catch the echoes of the past.
Lena Cortez, a volunteer of Mexican descent and the last survivor of Moretti's betrayal, haunts the place. Her bones are still hidden here, close to an old stone and ironwork tool shed that is the only remnant of the old prison. Playing the "Black Rooster, Red Rooster" record here will summon her ghost, and she will act out the trauma of being betrayed to the Italian Fascists by Moretti before the Battle of Madrid.
Scenes
The Crowing of the Rooster: The characters are under pressure from Moretti. Perhaps he sends Study Group 77 to tell them to leave town.

203

Perhaps he has the group make a mural depicting the coterie's decapitated bodies smoking in the sun outside their haven. No matter, the coterie should feel the need to do something about their enemy.
The Haunting: Feeling the players' characters' anger against the man who betrayed her, the ghost of Lena Cortez haunts the characters. This haunting should indicate she is on their side and connected to Moretti. Perhaps she points at the mural, sings the first bars of "Red Rooster," or implores them by saying his name again and again. The haunting ends with the appearance of the shadow. It screams, tearing Cortez's shade to tatters, then turns to the coterie, saying something like, "The Rooster must never crow. The silence of the grave must be observed. Leave this alone, or you will break the laws of the dead." If needed to emphasize the seriousness of the situation, the familiar shade will attack the coterie, driving them into terror with its screams. No one else sees it. This should be sufficient to set the players on the right track.
Research: By researching "The Rooster," Moretti's background, or the like, the coterie should realize that the Spanish Civil War is somehow the key to learning more about him. If nothing else, he is known to have been a volunteer in the International Brigades by other Brujah who are not his friends, but he denies it himself.
Stand Down: As the coterie begins their research, perhaps finding the truth about the old stadium, the record shop, or Moretti's past, his Study Group 77 finds them and accuses them of being fascist sympathizers. The coterie is attacked as they go on a hunt for blood, perform a research task, or spy on Moretti. The students are easily scared off by violence, but they will return in numbers, perhaps armed with a handgun or two. Play up the confusion of the misunderstanding, but don't let the coterie talk things out with the misinformed anti-fascists. Save that for the climax. If need be, have the haunting shadow scare the mortals away. Remember, they do not see it, but its scream still sends shivers down their spines.
204

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Closer and Closer: As the coterie gets closer and closer to the truth, use the shadow and the vigilantes to keep the pressure on, perhaps even letting Moretti himself confront them and reiterate his "leave town or else" ultimatum. This is where he would be backed up by the word of the Baron or the Prince, or perhaps the local Sheriff invites the coterie for drinks to calmly explain that they have outstayed their welcome and will leave or be left for the dawn the next night.
At the same time, other political forces of the Kindred community start contacting the coterie; perhaps another enemy of Moretti's offers them aid or an escape if they can reveal his secrets. "Something is wrong with that man. I have lived a life among radicals and would-be world saviors, but he is not one. Not truly," one of their contacts may state if they need to clearly understand what is at stake.
The Climax: When they find the record and the prison, they have all they need to summon the ghost of Lena Cortez and witness Moretti's betrayal. Lena is not a conscious wraith, like the shadow

that hunts the players' characters. The shadow play revealing Moretti's betrayal will repeat every time someone sings "Black Rooster, Red Rooster" near the tool shed, allowing the players' characters to show his treacherous nature to the Sheriff, Prince, or Baron at any time during the night. The testimony of the broken ghost, betrayed first by her friend, then by her country, and finally by a history that has forgotten her and the struggle she sacrificed everything for, brings any witnesses to their knees, crying tears of Bood. If Study Group 77 is there, even the mortals understand what is happening, and they turn on their master and teacher, some demanding answers, others wanting to kill him right then and there. Moretti sinks to his knees and accepts whatever fate may come, overwhelmed by the long-standing guilt and powerless in the face of his own disgusting betrayal, hidden for so long.

THE SPANISH CIVIL WAR AND THE VOLUNTEER BRIGADES In 1936, the International Brigades gathered in Catalonia to fight against Franco's fascist coup. The volunteers came from almost every country in the Western world, and within a year, more than a third of them would be dead. They faced off against the Spanish Foreign Legion--composed of volunteers from Hitler's Germany and Italian elite soldiers serving Mussolini. Underequipped, untrained, crippled by language barriers or ideological differences, and lacking armored support, their fight nonetheless secured two major victories for the antifascists, but ultimately the war ended in defeat for the Republicans in 1939.
After Franco's victory, some were incarcerated in Spanish concentration camps of the German model; some managed to escape to France and their home countries, where they were often imprisoned instead of being treated as defeated heroes. In America, the Spanish Volunteers were routinely suspected of being Communist spies and were discriminated against during the entirety of the Red Scare.
It was not until Franco's eventual defeat that they received the recognition they deserved. In 1994, the few remaining volunteers were granted Spanish citizenship and commemorated in a ceremony in Cádiz. Their descendants, provided they have helped spread the stories of their struggles and bravery, are extended the same privilege, now and forever.
This story is dedicated to their memory. For accessible information, check out George Orwell's novel Homage to Catalonia or Michael Leigh's film Land and Freedom.

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

The Parley
Learning to Negotiate Mental (optional), Physical, Social This story is written for an Anarch coterie and takes place in a city divided between Anarchs and Camarilla forces. It can be run as a one-shot or as part of an ongoing chronicle, where the players' characters are merely visitors.
The sire of a player's character contacts them with a request: An old ally of hers, Alexander Beaudry, leads an Anarch faction within the city, one that is currently teetering on the brink of conflict with a rival Anarch group. Both factions believe they can take over and oust the Camarilla Court from the city limits entirely. Unfortunately, the factions are enemies themselves. The character's sire believes it may be possible to negotiate a truce between the two groups and even encourage them to join forces against the Court--but it'll take careful handling. Since the sire herself can't attend, she's entrusting the parley to her childe and their associates.
Why the Players' Coterie?
Loyalty and obligation to a sire rope the characters into playing host to the contentious parley. When complications arise, they've got to figure out how to negotiate difficult terms and survive a Camarilla attack.
Goals
The characters must be the neutral hosts and negotiators between the rival Anarch factions to prove themselves trustworthy to the sire and further the Anarch cause. The story ends with a battle, and how well the coterie has done before influences the Difficulty of defeating the attackers.
206

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Supporting Characters
Alexander Beaudry
The leader of the Anarch group based in the city's south side. He wears old-fashioned clothing and sports a gold pocket watch. Alexander leads a small but protective Family (p. 17), with four childer of his own and six grandchilder. The group has controlled the warehouse district for the past 10 years--it's a fairly hospitable area for Kindred in terms of safety (low security, lots of hiding places), but the feeding is sparse. As a result, Alexander keeps strict track of who drinks when and punishes any greedy offspring harshly. Members of his Family going hungry drove Alexander to seek further hunting grounds in Grange Park, right on the edge of Naomi's territory.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 11th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 6 General Difficulties: 5/4 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 7, Social 7, Mental 5, Disciplines 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 6, Willpower 7 Exceptional Dice Pools: Brawl 9, Streetwise 8, Subterfuge 9 Disciplines: Potence 3 (Lethal Body, Prowess, Uncanny Grip), Presence 1 (Daunt)
Nathaniel
Alexander's eldest childe and his right hand. Nathaniel is particularly furious with Naomi, as a fledging childe of his was almost destroyed by Naomi's followers in a scrap within Grange Park a few weeks ago. He's hot-headed but respectful of his sire.
Clan: Brujah Generation: 12th Blood Potency: 2 Humanity: 7 General Difficulties: 4/3

Standard Dice Pools: Physical 6, Social 6, Mental 5, Disciplines 4
Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 5 Exceptional Dice Pools: Melee 8, Intimidation 7, Subterfuge 7 Disciplines: Celerity 1 (Rapid Reflexes), Potence 2 (Soaring Leap, Prowess) Equipment: Brass knuckles
Naomi Sato
The leader of the Anarch group on the city's southwest side. She wears a jacket covered in patches. She heads a Collegium (p. 16) of redworkers. Naomi's group controls two neighborhoods, focused around Lowry College, a community college situated in the larger of the two. The college is just a few blocks from Grange Park. Naomi's group fought off Camarilla influence in the area five years ago until they could comfortably call it theirs--so she's furious when Alexander's group starts making inroads into her carefully maintained territory. Naomi's Collegium has been losing members thanks to several Camarilla runins. She's in desperate need of security.
