Ebin Thomas
A spy for the Camarilla Court. He infiltrated Naomi's Collegium several months ago, and he's led a double unlife ever since, volunteering for missions, making himself indispensable within Naomi's group, and informing the Camarilla Prince whenever he can. He's fanned the flames of hatred between Alexander and Naomi, subtly reminding Naomi of old slights and injustices at every opportunity. He's patrolled Grange Park for many months, goading Alexander's grandchilder whenever he encounters them. He's under strict orders from the Camarilla Prince to prolong the meeting however he can, until Camarilla forces arrive.
Clan: Tremere Generation: 12th Blood Potency: 1 Humanity: 5 General Difficulties: 5/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 6, Mental 6, Disciplines 5 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 6 Exceptional Dice Pools: Subterfuge 9, Occult 9, Politics 10 Disciplines: Auspex 4 (Heightened Senses, Premonition, Scry the Soul, Spirit's Touch), Blood Sorcery 2 (A Taste for Blood, Scour Secrets;* Rituals: Blood Walk, Communicate with Kindred Sire) *Players Guide, p. 98
Lucia Herrera
Naomi's taciturn and loyal lieutenant. She is beginning to become suspicious of Ebin and looks askance at his goading Naomi. She dislikes Alexander intensely and believes he is a Camarilla wannabe.
Clan: Duskborn Generation: 15th Blood Potency: 0 Humanity: 7 General Difficulties: 4/2 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 4, Social 3, Mental 4

Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 4 Exceptional Dice Pools: Survival 6, Insight 8, Streetwise 8 Equipment: Wooden stakes
Camarilla Attackers
The Camarilla attackers are a crew of eight or so Kindred (the forces should be fairly evenly matched), most of whom are neonates and fledglings. They represent one faction of the city's Camarilla Kindred and are attacking on their own initiative. Defeating them does not solve the overall problem of the Tower's presence in the city.
In an ongoing chronicle, the leader can be someone the players' coterie recognizes; otherwise use Coterie Leader along with Coterie Bruiser and Coterie Face (pp. 80­81).
The remaining attackers have the following stats: General Difficulties: 4/3 Standard Dice Pools: Physical 5, Social 6, Mental 5, Disciplines 4 Secondary Attributes: Health 5, Willpower 5 Equipment: Sword or bowie knife
Locations
Fairweather Condominium: An innocuous condo building, facing a quiet residential street. The basement apartment is one of the sire's safe houses. The windows are covered with wooden shutters, masking steel blinds, and the master bedroom has a fancy double-thick door with a keypad lock. A laptop on the coffee table shows grainy security camera footage of the building's exterior, and a hacked speed camera on the street outside pings anyone moving at inhuman speeds (though it's sometimes fooled by speeding cars).
The condo has five main spaces: two bedrooms, the kitchen (blood in the fridge), the dining room, and the living room, which has been transformed into a conference space with several armchairs arranged around a coffee table. The decor is '60s chic with a few modern touches (mostly electronics).

208

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Scenes
Everything in Moderation: The players' characters sit in the comfortable living room of the large condo belonging to the sire. They consult a handwritten page of instructions left for them on the coffee table.
"If you can pull tonight off, not only will there be one less domain war for us to worry about within the city, but we might be able to plan an actual move against the Camarilla in the next few months. We just need Naomi and Alexander to agree to three terms: first, a truce between their people. Second, temporary borders for their domains--that's one they really won't like. Neither wants to cede ground. Finagle it however you need to. We only need them to keep it together until the Cam are driven out of town. Then we can renegotiate. Third, an exchange of some kind--a token of goodwill between them. Something they'll actually want and use.
"What you need to remember is these people hate each other, but they hate the Camarilla more. You can leverage that. The Camarilla's afraid of a united Anarch front, and they've let this little personal war between Alexander and Naomi fester as a result. I'm not sure, but Cam spies might have even had a hand in feeding it.
"If you're successful, we'll start working in earnest on a plan to move against the Cam. This is a big opportunity for us. Be diplomatic, and be careful.
"As always, destroy this message."
The Arbiters Assemble: The security cameras beep as several figures approach the door. A sign to the camera lets the players' characters know it's Naomi. She arrives with two Kindred in tow, Ebin and Lucia. They pick seats with their back to the wall, Lucia standing behind Naomi. Another beep as Alexander arrives. He's brought Nathaniel with him. The two leaders look at each other with loathing.
"I'm only here as a favor to an old friend," Alexander says, "Let's get this farce started, shall we?"
 Anonymous voice message

Negotiations begin slowly. Neither leader is willing to cede ground to the other or reveal what they really need. For now, they're coldly polite. The players' characters could use social combat to convince the leaders to listen to them, or they might try using their Skills to set the two sides at ease, facilitating conversation rather than compelling it.
If the players' characters are perceptive, they notice Ebin wandering around the apartment, looking at the doors and windows with apparent

appreciation. He asks about the condo, saying he's trying to get ideas for a safe house of his own. He seems particularly interested in knowing how the security system works.
Talking to Lucia or Naomi in private, the players can discover she is stretched very thin with a test of Wits + Insight at Difficulty 4. In recent weeks, when members of her group ventured farther afield, they were met by the Camarilla Sheriff. "It's like they know where we're going," she says, but she's warded

209

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

their territory with any number of privacy and security measures. She's worried: the Collegium's numbers weren't large to begin with, and they're dwindling fast.
Talking to Nathaniel or Alexander in private, it rapidly becomes clear that the Kindred are hungry on a nightly basis. Alexander's strict control of his Family has kept them safe for many years, but it's becoming harder to feed everyone as the warehouse district becomes more policed. Every member of the Family is one or two nights away from meeting their Beast, it seems.
Sharp Words and Sharper Fangs: When negotiations seem to be going well, Ebin begins to steer Naomi toward vindictiveness. He drops small references to various clashes and insults over the years. She picks up the bait readily. If Ebin has gathered any information that might turn her against the players' characters, he'll drop that in her ear as well.
Naomi says, "You want a big juicy steak and you don't want to pay for it? In my book, that's called theft."
Alexander spits, "Consider it recompense for when you attacked my grandchilde with no provocation!"
"No provocation?" Naomi says, "She was trespassing on our territory! You would've done the same, and don't deny it." Ebin leans down and whispers something in Naomi's ear. "And don't forget," she adds, "That we were the ones to run the Camarilla off the park in the first place. You're just vultures, landing on the prey we brought down."
The players' characters must take control of the situation. Social combat might convince Alexander and Naomi to negotiate or simply convince them that there's a larger enemy to be dealt with. Ebin tries to undermine these attempts at every turn. If the players suspect he may be more than he seems, they can attempt to force him to confess to sabotage. He seems quietly confident, even when being accused of betrayal--shaking him at all takes a Charisma or Manipulation + Persuasion

or Subterfuge test at Difficulty 6. Almost as if he's expecting backup.
And indeed, when Ebin confesses--or, when it seems like a deal might or might not be struck--unexpected guests arrive.
Enemies at the Gates: The security measures around the condo signal to the characters that intruders are on their way. It doesn't take a genius to realize it's probably Camarilla forces, here to kill two birds with one stone. They're armed with swords or bowie knives--quiet but deadly weapons. The three disparate factions within the apartment--players' characters, Alexander and Nathaniel, Naomi and Lucia--must fight together against the besieging Camarilla forces.
If the characters unmasked Ebin, they can get a small head start on calling in reinforcements or creating an escape route (Storytellers might present this as "you can do one thing before they're at the door"). With Ebin neutralized or killed, he won't join the Camarilla attackers.
If the Camarilla spy remains in play, Ebin suddenly appears behind Lucia and swiftly dispatches her. Naomi screams in rage. The Camarilla forces arrive as Lucia crumples to the floor.
The attack commences immediately. The attackers will only stay to fight for as long as it seems like they're winning. If they lose more than two or three Kindred, they move to retreat to lick their wounds and return another day.
Unlikely Allies? Assuming the characters emerge from their battle with their heads attached, they can impart a final warning to Naomi and Alexander: fight together or be destroyed. The Camarilla Court has been doing its best to drive the two groups apart, and the ruin of the parley is only proof of how strong they could be as allies. The Anarchs in the city can strike a mighty blow against the Court if they would only work together.

210

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

Variations
The parley can end in a number of outcomes. These variations are just a few ideas to inspire Storytellers. Everything depends on the characters' success during negotiations--did they make a good case for why the two factions should ally, and did they offer reasonable apologies or reparations to satisfy the two leaders?
More Time: If the players' coterie manages to interrupt the attack--for instance by taking one of the attackers hostage--they can enter social combat against the Camarilla leader to convince them to withdraw with a hostage of their own. This means figuring out whom to send with the attackers--depending on who currently has the most trust in the players' coterie, either Lucia or Nathaniel can volunteer. It also buys everyone a bit more time to figure out what to do next, but there likely isn't going to be a long-term peaceful solution. The Camarilla forces are committed to ousting the Anarchs from their city.
Brothers in Arms: If the parley was about to be successful and the spy was revealed, Naomi stares down at what's left of Ebin. She wheels around and offers a hand to Alexander. "Take Grange Park," she says, "Just let us in it on new moons."
Alexander takes the hand slowly. "A deal," he says. "My kinsmen shall not harry yours any longer. And... we shall answer your call should you need aid."
She grips his hand tighter. "Deal," she repeats. She rips a patch off her jacket and presents it to him. "Show this to any of my guys, they'll listen to you."
Alexander takes it delicately, handing her his pocket watch fob after a moment's hesitation. "Show any of my offspring this and they will do the same."
The players' characters can glad-hand the two leaders and bask in the glow of a truly successful parley. Their sire contacts them not long after--it's time to start planning a large-scale move against the Camarilla domain as a whole.

No Truce: Alexander sneers. "A Camarilla spy as a confidant?" he says to Naomi, "I knew your judgment was poor, but this takes the cake."
The parley's a disaster. Not only are the characters exhausted from a nasty battle, the negotiations went nowhere and neither Alexander nor Naomi is willing to give an inch. The battle has confirmed their worst suspicions of each other, and the two groups may now openly go to war. Any hope for parley between the two sides will have to wait for tensions to die down or new leadership to emerge. Grange Park will now become a battlefield.
Takeover: As the dust clears, the Camarilla attackers are defeated, and one of the leaders has met their end. The remaining one doesn't do much to hide their pleasure. They've got the advantage of surprise if they can get back to their group and get them moving--they can take over their rival's territory before dawn if they're willing to get messy with it. The players' characters have the choice to aid the takeover and actively keep the mess to a minimum, or they can try to argue against the idea and present something more compelling in its stead. If they don't stand with the conqueror-apparent, they may be able to earn the trust of the unlucky territory that lost its leader, although they'll have to walk a delicate line if they want to keep the surviving half of the parley from thinking of them as a new enemy.
Obvious Bias: If the players' characters clearly take the side of one group over the other before the attack, the rival leader storms out of the parley early. Whichever side remains can be counted as a strong ally to the players' characters and the sire, but the offended leader swears they'll take revenge on the characters for embarrassing them. Worse still, the sire is disappointed, especially if the players' characters don't side with Alexander (her closer friend). Grange Park remains a contested territory, and if the players' characters visit it, they may be attacked by the insulted party.

211

Chapter Six: Characters and Stories

Kidnapped: The Camarilla forces are too strong--they're clearly going to win. Naomi and Alexander make a break for it; only one of them escapes. The remaining leader is summarily executed.
The players' characters are staked, stuffed into a waiting van, and whisked away to the heart of Camarilla territory. When the stakes are removed,

they are standing before the Prince and his Sheriff, surrounded by Camarilla vampires. The players' characters must negotiate as best they can to escape final death. Above all, the Prince is interested in finding the sire who arranged the parley. He will exchange safety for information on her, especially if he can extract anything about her safe houses or her business deals.

AUTARK STORIES Political stories can play out in autarkies as well. Perhaps you can use one of the above stories more or less as they are if your autarky is loosely based on the structures of an Anarch or Camarilla city, changing out the key characters for unbound alternatives. Otherwise, here are a few ideas for autarky story concepts:  The Fragile Pact: In a domain shared between a few independent Kindred factions, peace is fragile but
stable, until a feeding gone wrong threatens to shatter the arrangement. The players' coterie is expected to choose a side in the conflict, pushing the future of their domain towards one of several ideologies.  The Mortal Broker: A mortal fixer controls too many secrets about undead dealings. The autarks of the city will have to decide whether to protect their asset or remove the liability.  The Refugees: Displaced Camarilla Kindred arrive from a fallen domain, demanding shelter. Letting them in also means letting in their politics, animosities, and old habits. How will the players' coterie protect their way of unlife from the sneaking influence of the Tower bats? · The Caputo Effect: In what appears a perfect autarky, the players' characters discover signs that something more sinister is going on. Slowly, they figure out that the city's ruler has been using the domain as a cover for Sabbat dealings all along.

Appendix:

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

SYSTEMS
In this appendix, you'll find new Discipline Powers, Loresheets, and other game mechanics designed for characters interested in vampire politics.

Appendix: Systems

Disciplines
Animalism Level 2
Tracing the Blood Though their deep connection to the Beast, the vampire understands when other vampires call upon their Blood.
Cost: One Rouse Check Dice Pool: Resolve + Animalism System: The character Rouses the Blood and tests Resolve + Animalism (Difficulty 3). Results are based on the margin of success, and all targets must be within their sensory range. Success reveals the target's general direction (forward, backward, sideways, etc.), whereas a critical win allows the user to know precisely which vampire triggered the effect.  Basic Win (Margin 0): The vampire can sense when Blood is Roused.  Margin 1: The vampire can sense whether the Rouse fueled a universal Kindred Trait such as Blood Surge and Blush of Life, or activated a Discipline (and which Discipline it was).  Margin 2+: The vampire senses exactly which Power was activated and roughly what it does. The Storyteller may call for a Wits + Occult test (Difficulty of a Discipline Power's level + 2) to recognize rare and uncommon Discipline Powers or gain a clearer understanding of the effect. Duration: One scene
Auspex
Level 3
Read the Room The vampire senses the overriding opinion of a crowd, fueling their understanding about how many would support or oppose a suggestion. Crafty Kindred Read the Room before proposing a course of action or even declaring praxis.
Cost: One Rouse Check Dice Pool: Intelligence + Auspex

System: The vampire activates the power and mentally poses a question they want answered, as though they were taking a poll, then tests Intelligence + Auspex. Difficulty starts at 5 for a crowd of 12 (mortals or Kindred) and decreases for each additional 12 individuals down to a minimum of Difficulty 2.
Storytellers may decide that certain questions are too complex to answer, and while numbers are always vague (some, many, most, etc.), they otherwise summarize the crowd's general sentiment. On a critical win, they detect how this sentiment changes from any actions taken during the scene.
Duration: Instantaneous, or one scene on a critical
Reveal the Invisible Leash With a simple touch, the vampire can sense who in a room their target considers themselves subordinate to.
Cost: One Rouse Check Dice Pool: Intelligence + Auspex vs Composure + Resolve System: The vampire must be in physical contact with their target and test Intelligence + Auspex vs their target's Composure + Resolve. On a win, the vampire senses who in the room the target considers their political superior (if any). If there are multiple such individuals, they only identify the most immediate present, such as a sire whose Blood Bound the target rather than the Prince. For every 2 points of the Margin, a secondary superior is identified, moving from the target's most immediate to the most distant. Duration: For as long as the vampire is touching their target
Blood Sorcery Level 2
Vinculum Magnum Savvy Kindred sorcerers manipulate their vitae to keep more Kindred under their control. This power has fallen out of favor with the Tremere since the destruction of their prime chantry and loss

214

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

of the ability to bind others. Still, many Princes, Hierophants, and other authorities seeking to maintain their dominance have sought it out from those redworkers who know of the power.
Cost: None System: The user concentrates their Blood's bonding properties, and can force the Blood Bond on more Kindred than normal. Add the vampire's Blood Sorcery rating to the number of Kindred they can Blood Bond. Vampires who cannot bond other vampires for any reason (such as the Tremere clan Bane or being thin-blooded) do not gain this benefit.
Dominate
Level 3
Chain of Command Prerequisite: Mesmerize Those laboring under the will of the vampire drag others along with their actions, demanding help and obedience with a will fueled by their master. By delegating their authority, the vampire's reach extends that much further.
Cost: One Rouse Check System: After delivering a Mesmerize command, the vampire can choose to invoke this power by immediately making another Rouse Check. Those affected become capable of executing slightly more complex tasks--even showing some degree of awareness--as they set about achieving their master's goal, seeking out other suitable candidates to help them. The target of this power adds the vampire's rating in Dominate to any Social tests they make when convincing others to assist them in their objective (and only those tasks), allowing them to better command underlings, delegate tasks, and spread the burden. Those so commanded aren't operating under any supernatural compulsion and are free to object--the main benefit is making people in authority better equipped to order around those beneath them. Duration: Until the command is carried out or the scene ends, whichever comes first

Presence Level 2
Predator's Lure The vampire uses their presence to lure a victim closer, whether for conversation or for bloodier purposes.
Cost: One Rouse Check Dice Pool: Charisma + Leadership vs. Composure + Resolve System: The vampire makes a Rouse Check to target one victim. Mortals are automatically affected. Against supernatural creatures, the user must win a test of Charisma + Leadership vs. the victim's Composure + Resolve. On a win, this Power compels an affected target to approach the user, whether or not the target knows or even is aware of the user. The victim won't drop everything or risk their safety to approach the user, and the power is ineffective in any kind of violent altercation. They politely finish conversations, complete their transactions, cross streets with caution, and wait at a safe distance until the Power ends or the situation becomes safe. After using this power, the victim may recall an intense curiosity or an unexplainable compulsion to investigate or be near a person or location. Duration: One scene or until they are within 10 feet (3 meters) of the user.
Blood Sorcery Rituals
Level 1
Preservation This Warding Circle variant allows a sorcerer to protect an object against the ravages of time. Entrepreneurial Kindred preserve the bric-a-brac of sentimental ancillae in exchange for boons, while those with a more criminal perspective use this rite to protect fragile valuables from damage during transport or theft. Preservation does not turn back the clock but preserves the item in its current state.
Ingredients: A quill or fountain pen, one or more Rouse Checks' worth of the caster's vitae

215

Appendix: Systems

Process: The user inscribes arcane sigils on the item to be preserved.
System: The item withstands damage from normal wear and tear and the passage of time. Mold and rot do not take, pages do not yellow with age, insects will not damage the item, nor will the sun fade it, so long as some modicum of protection is maintained. Intentional damage still affects the item as normal, but scuffs or scrapes resulting from transportation or minor accidents, such as a spill or a fall from a height of only a few feet, leave no trace.
One Rouse Check of vitae is enough to ward an item about the size of a chair or guitar case, or a handful of small items such as chess pieces. The addition of a second Rouse Check during casting doubles the size of the item that can be warded.
Rite of Introduction A Ritual that has mostly fallen out of use, the Rite of Introduction alerts the master of a chantry to the sorcerer's presence. In the age of the Second Inquisition, some cabals still find this useful for sending signals to their leader.
Ingredients: An iron pot or cauldron, a Rouse Check of the user's vitae
Process: The user brings the Blood to a boil and speaks a brief incantation while it boils.
System: The ritualist sends an impression of their face, their name, and a short, one-sentence message to any master of a Foundation of the Chantry (see below) within the same city. The caster can choose to withhold any of these elements but not alter them. If no Foundation is within range, the caster is aware that their message was not received.
Level 5
Foundation of the Chantry Once, the Tremere were the only sorcerous game in town, thanks in part

to this Ritual. Although all existing foundation stones cracked in 2008, this sought-after rite can still be used to bind a cabal of sorcerers together and communicate locally. Traditionalists lament that this Ritual has lost much of its power and once affected much larger areas; now it requires a more direct infusion of vitae than in decades past.
Ingredients: A polished sphere of amethyst, a white or albino mammal or bird indigenous to the region, and a human skull
Process: After slaying the animal and soaking the stone in its blood, the sorcerer draws a map of the area (up to about a 12-mile radius) on the skull and places the gemstone in the skull's eye socket or mouth.
System: The gem and skull are bound to a spiritually significant location (often called Veins of the Earth, Blood Sigils, p. 162) and become the Foundation of the Chantry. As long as the Foundation remains in place, the caster or their designee (who must be present to participate in the rite) can receive missives from the Rite of Introduction (see above). The caster knows whenever the Rite of Introduction is performed within the same city as the Foundation but must touch it to receive the introduction. Upon doing so, they may send

216

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

a signal back to the caster of the Rite of Introduction, consisting of an impression of their name, face, the location of the chantry, and a single-sentence message. As with the Rite of Introduction, the caster can choose to withhold, but not obscure, any of the above. The Ritual lasts until the Foundation is disturbed.
Oblivion Ceremonies
Level 3
Misfortune's Hand By harnessing the powers of entropy against a target of their choice, the caster invites ruin upon their assets: funds get tied up in a bureaucratic knot, allies stop returning calls, retainers suffer debilitating accidents, and contacts find themselves under observation, unable to harvest or deliver information as usual.
Prerequisites: Shadow Servant or Touch of Oblivion (Players Guide, p. 89)
Ingredients: Something related to the asset being affected (bank account details, hair or blood of a living target, voice recordings), lead, a heat source sufficient to melt the lead, and a glass of blood from someone considered to have bad luck
Process: The caster drips blood into the lead as it melts while chanting the names of old gods, historic failures, and wishes of doom against the target. Once the blood is gone and the metal is molten, it's poured over the remaining objects. Once cooled, it's buried--tradition dictates a burial at sea, but anywhere will do.
System: The caster prepares the ingredients, selects one Background of their chosen target, and makes the Ceremony test. On a success, the Background is temporarily reduced by one dot; however, if the margin equals or exceeds the Background's rating, it decreases by two. Storytellers may prefer to make this roll if the rating should be kept secret.
The changes to the Background are subtle at first, with a series of unfortunate events, coincidences, and setbacks temporarily limiting access to the victim's assets. The change normally takes a few nights before it's in full effect and lasts a week after that. On a

critical success, the caster can choose to have the changes take effect immediately, as well as extend the impact for a full month. Backgrounds cannot be reduced below zero, and only one casting of this Ceremony can affect a given target at any one time, regardless of the source. All ratings return to their original level once this Ceremony expires.
Loresheets
Loresheets are not just collections of cool powers--they also represent entanglements, obligations, and connections to the broader world. Chapter Five: The Storyteller's Manifesto provides some examples of tying Loresheets to specific events in Kindred history (pp. 164­173), but Storytellers and players are encouraged to make each Loresheet their own. Almost every Loresheet can be used in politically minded chronicles. Though each individual Loresheet doesn't always represent a person, consider putting any Loresheet connections on the Relationship Map to make them meaningful, recurring story elements.
CHARACTER-BASED LORESHEETS Many Loresheets reference specific Kindred, but the ties a player's character may have to that Storyteller character are left for the troupe to define. Some options include:  A Kindred idol  The character's political persuasion or approach  A cautionary tale  An influential Kindred whom the character is
constantly compared to  A close, personal relationship, whether positive,
negative, or neutral  A sire-childe or grandsire-grandchilde relationship  The character's prior, lingering presence in the city

217

Appendix: Systems

DESCENDANT OF COUNT JOCALO
(Ministry Only)

Among the Kindred of Europe, one of the most infamous members of the Ministry is Count Jocalo, an Iberian noble turned spy for the Church of Set back when the Clan and Church were largely the same entity. Jocalo traveled between Europe, Africa, and Asia, visiting Court after Court to gather pawns and artifacts for the Hierophants of Set. Legend has it that he was even exiled from London for his role in the Great Fire.
Jocalo was very much the stereotypical tempter and schemer, and most of his descendants follow suit, using drugs, sex, religious beliefs, or occasionally plain burglary to obtain anything needed. His own religious convictions were dubious at best. His extensive travels and independence from the usual court system led Jocalo to sire many offspring across the world, and Ministers claiming him as an ancestor spread further still during the Ages of Sail and Steam.

· A Name You Can't Refuse: Your reputation precedes you, providing the equivalent of one dot of Status with both the Anarchs and Camarilla--even autarks, the Hecata, and most blood cults won't refuse you entry into their domains so long as you don't overstay your welcome or cause obvious trouble. Most domains will also watch you like a hawk, with the fame of Jocalo's schemes coloring your reputation.
·· Tools of the Trade: Jocalo's Blood gives you an understanding of his methods. You gain a free specialty in your choice of three of the following Skills, or the first dot in the Skill if you don't already possess one dot: Insight, Larceny, Persuasion, Streetwise, or Subterfuge. You are, however, known for inheriting Jocalo's teachings, and local authorities or boon holders often come to you to get underhanded tasks done--or consider you a suspect.

LORE
··· A Taste So Sweet: Your Blood and Kiss are more potent when ensnaring others. You gain the Enduring Bond Merit (complete and partial Blood Bonds to you weaken every other month; see Gehenna War, p. 44), and gain a two-dice bonus to interact with any mortals during or immediately after feeding from them. Your Discipline powers relying on others drinking your Blood or receiving your Kiss (such as Lingering Kiss, Draught of Might, or using Scorpion's Touch to affect the Blood in your own body) have their durations doubled.

···· Relic Hound: Following in Jocalo's footsteps, you're a collector of lore and relics. You gain a two-dice bonus to all dice pools directly related to seeking out texts or items pertaining to Kindred history or sorcery (including Blood Sorcery, Oblivion, and Thin-Blood Alchemy), and you may turn a single 1 or 10 on a Hunger die (your choice) to a 2 for these tests.
····· Rapid Entanglement: The Blood teaches you to shed connections and forge new ones. Once per story or month of game time (whichever is shorter), you can exchange all the dots invested in an Ally, Contact, Influence, or Retainer for another, whether the same category (such as Influence with local law enforcement for Influence within the local funeral industry) or another Background (such as Influence with local law enforcement for a Retainer).

218

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

DESCENDANT OF MARCONIUS
(Lasombra Only)

Marconius was already old when he and a coterie of his fellow Lasombra experimented with fell Oblivion Ceremonies. He disappeared for a time, emerging around Strasbourg in the late Middle Ages. Impeccably polite and erudite, Marconius and his brood became powerful members of the city, vying with the Ventrue for influence in the region. During the Age of Steam, many of his brood nominally accepted membership in the Sabbat while others remained loners and autarkis. From the Second Sabbat Civil War through the Sect Wars of the 1990s, Sabbat purges of moderates and "the undevout" within their ranks, as well as the expansion of Kindred metropolitan domains to include smaller cities in their orbit, led many of Marconius' brood to leverage their ancestor's name to abandon the Black Hand quietly. Whether prescient or just pragmatic, many of Marconius' childer rejected the Sabbat before the cost to leave skyrocketed.
Marconius' brood has a reputation for politeness and suffers from common superstitions. Few cities have more than one weirdling, as they tend to eschew the company of their clanmates and shoo their childer away after a decade or two.

LORE

· Darkling Ward: You can master any ward or warding circle Blood Sorcery Ritual as though it were an Oblivion Ceremony with no power prerequisite. Your Wards deal Aggravated Willpower damage, rather than Health damage, and those affected by them lose all sense of direction and location until they wander away from the ward. The first time you learn a Warding Ceremony, you must also take a Folkloric Bane or Block Flaw.
·· Abyssal Scholar: Marconius' Blood gives you a knack for Oblivion Ceremonies, gaining an Occult specialty for Ceremonies. You can learn Ceremonies without needing the prerequisite power; however, each time you learn a Ceremony without the prerequisite, take an additional Folkloric Bane or Block Flaw. If you later master the prerequisite

Power, you may remove that Flaw. You must still possess the correct level of Oblivion to master Ceremonies.
··· Fey Beauty: You appear tall and lithe, with an odd, fey-like feature that makes you stand out in a crowd. You gain the Stunning Merit but suffer a two-dice penalty whenever attempting to disguise yourself, even supernaturally.
···· Nightingale's Song: Dominate ordinarily cannot be used to force someone to tell the truth, but Marconius' Blood helps you steal secrets. Once per session, you can use Mesmerize to demand a target reveal a secret; however, without a cleverly worded order, the secret may be unpredictable. Revealing damning information counts as harm, so getting specific secrets may still be impossible. Should your attempt to compel a secret

fail, you visibly shed one large, bloody tear for each point of the margin of failure (or seven tears if the Power otherwise fails). These tears cause a Rouse Check if shedding four or more, and risk provoking Hunger Frenzy in other Kindred or uncomfortable questions among mortals.
····· The Grandest Trick: Marconius' odd Blood embraces the light, if only for a moment. The effects of your Oblivion Powers or Ceremonies can survive three rounds of sunlight before they fade away. Whenever you are exposed to sunlight, you may choose to take Aggravated Willpower damage instead of Aggravated Health damage for up to three rounds. This is often enough for the weirdlings of Marconius to escape, though the damage to the Masquerade could be severe if they don't escape the sun in time.

219

Appendix: Systems

DESCENDANT OF MEERLINDA
(Tremere Only)

Meerlinda was one of the first Tremere and a participant in the Ritual of Usurpation that founded the clan. Whenever a schism arose within her cabal, she deftly walked the line between the accuser and the accused, preserving allies and avoiding enemies as she worked to rehabilitate the clan's image as usurpers and low-clan villains. Many say it was her intervention that allowed the Tremere a place in the Camarilla or that the Camarilla was a Tremere scheme to gain acceptance within Kindred society in the first place.
Her descendants tend to share her views, spinning neutrality to the benefit of everyone rather than scheming for their own gain. Now, in the era of a divided Clan Tremere, her descendants often bridge the gap between feuding houses and even work with solitarchs (autark chantries) or non-Tremere sorcerers.

· Strength of the Coven: Your sire taught you when to step back and let others take the reins. You can assist in any Blood Sorcery Ritual (see "Teamwork" in Vampire, p. 122) even if you don't know the Ritual. If you've ever assisted another Blood Sorcerer in a Ritual, you gain a two-dice bonus to Persuasion tests to convince them to share a Ritual with you or use one on your behalf. The other sorcerer may still request a reasonable payment or other compensation.
·· Wealth of Boons: Your Blood compels you to exchange minor favors with other Kindred, often to your benefit. Once per story, you can treat one boon owed to you as one category higher or a boon you

LORE
owe as one category lower. Trivial boons treated as lower are immediately satisfied: no recompense needed.
··· Sanguine Understanding: Meerlinda's Blood whispers of politeness and politics. You gain a specialty related to Kindred or their factions for Etiquette and Politics, or the first dot in the Skill if you don't have any. Furthermore, once per session, when dealing with Kindred, you can use your Etiquette or Politics (including specialties if relevant) instead of Insight, Persuasion, or Subterfuge in a dice pool.
···· Meerlinda's Heir: You emerge from Meerlinda's shadow as a well-known force on your own. Increase your Status by 1 (to a maximum of 5). Once per session, you can

demand to be treated by your faction as though your Status were one level higher (if adding your Status to a test, this can bring it above 5), or use your full Status even when far from home or dealing with another sect.
····· Bestial Neutrality: You excel at negotiating with your own Beast. You gain a Conviction related to diplomacy or neutrality, such as "Peace among the Kindred is worth any cost," which is not tied to a Touchstone and cannot be lost. Once per session when acting in line with this Conviction, you can change a single 1 or 10 on a single Hunger die result to a 2 (a simple failure) after seeing the results of the roll.

220

COURTS OF THE DAMNED

DESCENDANT OF MENELE
(Brujah Only)
Menele has walked the Earth since his breathing days as a philosopher-king in ancient Greece. His insatiable lust for enlightenment led him first to India, then to Carthage, a utopia purportedly built on the ideal of peaceful coexistence between Kindred and kine. A utopia reduced to ash by the treachery of Helena.
Apoplectic with rage, Menele razed Helena's beloved Pompeii to a cinder, lighting the spark of their unending rivalry. Helena and Menele fought across the ages until they beat themselves into mutual torpor near present-day Chicago. Menele was on the verge of renewing his ancient grudge with Helena when he was called east by the Beckoning, freeing his childer from his vice-like mental grip.
Many of Menele's descendants inherited his passion for philosophy, enlightenment, and greater ideals, while others became fearsome warriors selected for their ability to defend Menele from Helena's agents. Both were needed to realize Menele's weary dream of founding a new Carthage based on ancient Brujah ideals. Now, Menele's descendants are free to choose their destiny for the first time in millennia. The question is: What will they do with it?

· Symposium: Menele always liked a good argument and sired many childer after debating the mysteries of life for evenings on end. You gain the ability to reroll a single die on all of your Persuasion tests.
·· Carthago Delenda Est: Menele maintained an elaborate network of spies, informants, and retainers. His network fragmented upon his destruction, but you managed to claim a portion of it for your own. You have three dots to split between Retainers, Influence, Allies, and Resources, with the absolute certainty that Helena and her agents will attempt to take them from you.
··· Know Thyself: Menele believed the Brujah needed

LORE
to return to their roots as thinkers and positive agents of change. You are trained to resist the pull of your Brujah fury. Once per session, you can spend a Willpower level to reroll a Frenzy affected by your Brujah Clan Bane.
···· Knowledge is Power: Menele was impossibly old and held many secrets about the Kindred of Chicago and beyond. You were the steward of those secrets before the Beckoning drew him away. Once per story, you may request a single unspecified secret from the Storyteller about a clan or Kindred in Chicago or a similar domain that felt Menele's influence (Storyteller's discretion).

····· The Greater Mysteries: Menele spent years learning the meditative practices necessary to perceive the astral plane in his quest for spiritual perfection. You inherited a portion of his ability and remain fully cognizant of your surroundings during your daily slumber. Reduce the difficulty of all Humanity tests to awaken during the day by two. You also have the ability to perceive fractured glimpses of events to come that may affect your character while you slumber. Once per session, you may roll Resolve + Occult to interpret your daytime visions. Each success brings the vision into greater focus, and the Storyteller may offer you one cryptic hint or fact relevant to your character.

221

Appendix: Systems

DESCENDANT OF RASALON
(Nosferatu Only)
Rasalon believed in the unity of Clan Nosferatu; many of his descendants continue to maintain this belief: neither sectarian lines nor coteries should prevail over the unity of the Blood. A crusader who traveled in the Middle Ages, Rasalon established lines of communication between individual Nosferatu, and his name is still revered by many of the clan. As sectarian lines divided other clans, the legacy of Rasalon maintained lines of communication that benefited the Nosferatu for centuries. Many Nosferatu push for open lines of communication--even with their Sabbat brethren--out of fear of a mythical group of murderous elders, although Rasalon did his best to assuage those fears and focus more on clan unity.
Your ancestor's name has opened doors for you that may be closed to others, and you find yourself becoming a fulcrum of knowledge for the clan.

· Whisper to the Brood: You communicate with the Nosferatu in the closest neighboring city, plus one additional city for each dot of Politics you possess. You speak through animal or mortal messengers, local cyphers and metaphors, and/or a hodgepodge of various languages, rendering the messages no threat to the Masquerade.
·· Shared Knowledge: Your clanmates reveal secrets that help you excel in Kindred society. You gain the first dot of Etiquette, Politics, or Occult (choose any two) or, if you already possess the first dot, a specialty related to Kindred. Many Nosferatu seek you out for your knowledge, which occasionally costs you time. Once per story, you may ask the Storyteller to reveal any secret you may have heard that is relevant to a current plot or event, though which plot or event it relates to may not always be clear.

LORE
··· Underground Support: Your reputation precedes you, at least among the sewer rats. You gain a four-dot Mawla representing all the Nosferatu neonates and ancillae in nearby cities. Once per story, you can petition a Nosferatu for shelter for one day (if it includes your coterie) or three (for just you), and at least one clanmate will offer refuge, though there may be a cost. Your connections often drag you into minor squabbles between clanmates.
···· Clan of the Hidden: Your Blood resonates with other sewer rats. When activating Obfuscate Powers to hide or disguise yourself, you can extend that effect to include other Nosferatu (up to your Obfuscate + Blood Potency) so long as everyone maintains physical contact. Those losing physical contact become noticeable. With you, a group of Nosferatu can huddle under

the effect of Silence of Death for secret conversations, be hidden with Cloak of Shadows, or thwart cameras through Ghost in the Machine.
····· Hidden Primogen: All the Nosferatu in the region look to you. No Nosferatu other than explicit Adversaries reject reasonable boons from you. Nosferatu treat any boons involving you as one step in your favor: trivial boons you might owe are instantly expunged, a minor boon owed to you is treated as major, or a life boon you owe is treated as a major boon.
